# Progress

## Report blocked browser child-frame navigations (2026-11-10)

The managed browser now includes off-host iframe document requests in `blocked_subresources` under the `frame` resource type, while keeping blocked top-level document navigations in their separate refusal counter. A regression test verifies the child-frame/main-frame distinction. `tests/unit/test_browser.py` passes all 32 tests with an isolated pytest temp directory; Ruff and Pyright pass for the changed worker and test. This improves owner visibility into embedded content blocked by the host grant. No model inference was used.


## Verify live Edge blocks off-host page subresources (2026-11-10)

Added `scripts/browser_subresource_smoke.py` and wired it into the hosted Windows source workflow. On real Microsoft Edge, the managed worker loaded `https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTPS` with only `en.wikipedia.org` approved, retained the expected page text, and blocked five image subresource requests to unapproved hosts. The check records the report plus source hashes and requests no model inference. Local evidence: `.acceptance/browser-subresource-edge-fdf4c7ab2dec4c0b9ad1e77cfa408a05/report.json`. Deeper nested-frame/subresource-chain coverage and real Edge proxy-navigation acceptance remain open. Hosted Windows run [42812057478](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42812057478) passed this Edge check and artifact upload, plus the full source workflow (948 Python tests); the installer job was skipped.


## Upgrade MCP client to SDK v2 and add subscriptions/listen (2026-11-10)

Migrated the client and deterministic stdio/HTTP fixtures from MCP SDK 1.x to pinned `mcp==2.3.0`. The v2 client now negotiates supported protocol versions, runs modern `Client.listen` for explicitly subscribed resource URIs, and keeps the legacy `resources/subscribe` path for older negotiated versions. Listener events remain exact-URI filtered, bounded and marked untrusted; overflow or stream loss requests owner-driven resynchronization. OAuth now uses the v2 `httpx2` transport and result type. The focused MCP integration/unit slice passes (15 tests), the full deterministic Python suite passed earlier in this work (947 passed, 1 skipped), strict Pyright, Ruff checks, and repository-wide Ruff formatting passed, and the frozen Windows engine rebuild completed successfully. The first hosted Windows source run stopped at its formatter gate; three files were reformatted and the follow-up hosted run will recheck the branch. The rebuilt native desktop MCP acceptance also passed against real stdio and loopback Streamable HTTP OAuth fixtures, including resource subscription/notification, prompt/resource review and clean shutdown; evidence: `.acceptance/53f12c17-e61b-44ab-9dfb-0e141e03a413/`. No LLM inference was used. Updated the MCP architecture description and refreshed the locked dependency inventory and provisional notices for SDK v2 dependencies, including the platform-conditional `httpx2-jsfetch` license. Hosted Windows source run [42810648921](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42810648921) passed lint, format, Pyright, contract checks and the full Python suite (installer job skipped). Broader installed-release and third-party MCP compatibility, sampling, prompt use in model context, and full extension package lifecycle/parity remain open.


## Add OAuth authorization for HTTP MCP servers (2026-11-10)

Native Windows desktop acceptance now exercises HTTP MCP OAuth against a local PKCE-verifying server. The harness configures the server through the actual Tauri Integrations panel, launches the system browser to an automatic local authorization callback, verifies the protected tool discovery and Credential Manager token, disconnects/reconnects without a second authorization, clears sign-in, and confirms desktop/engine shutdown. A stale pre-OAuth native engine initially failed this path; rebuilding the bundle fixed it. The passing rebuilt native development app report is `.acceptance/d2a5b7eb-2f39-410f-8233-7c11e2923320/report.json`; its fixture trace confirms `code_ok=True` and `pkce_ok=True`. The smoke also retains stdio transport/resource/prompt/permission coverage and performs no model inference. Windows installer run [42800363137](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42800363137) passed the same acceptance against `build/native-release`, plus the remaining installer job checks. Third-party provider compatibility remains open.

## Restore MCP OAuth token expiry across restarts (2026-11-10)

The SDK reloads saved tokens without restoring their expiry timestamp, so a token saved before application shutdown could be presented as valid indefinitely. MCP OAuth storage now records an absolute expiry alongside the token response, and the pinned provider restores the remaining lifetime when it loads vault data. Expired tokens refresh before the next MCP request; legacy token-only profiles are treated as expired to force refresh or sign-in. The loopback OAuth fixture verifies authorization-code + PKCE, refresh after expiry, migration from the former token-only vault format, reconnect without another browser launch, and owner denial with no token saved. The combined MCP HTTP, stdio and API suite passes (48 tests), along with strict manager/API Pyright, Ruff/format, generated contract and desktop TypeScript checks. Hosted Windows source run [42795531243](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42795531243) passed the full suite, browser/API checks and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because packaging inputs were unchanged. No live provider, real user credential or model inference was used.
Streamable HTTP MCP connections now respond to OAuth challenges through the pinned MCP SDK's authorization-code + PKCE flow. The client binds an ephemeral loopback callback on `127.0.0.1` with a random path, opens the owner’s browser, and saves dynamic client registration metadata and access/refresh tokens in per-server Windows Credential Manager profiles. Connect allows up to five minutes for sign-in; subsequent reconnects reuse the token, and the desktop can show saved authorization and clear it. Configured static bearer credentials continue to take precedence. A real local ASGI authorization fixture verifies metadata discovery, client registration, callback/state handling, PKCE verifier exchange, vault persistence and reconnect without a second browser launch. A denied `access_denied` callback now displays a refusal message, saves no token and leaves the server disconnected; SDK-wrapped OAuth errors produce a sanitized API error. The combined MCP HTTP, stdio and API test batch passes (48 tests); Ruff, format, strict Pyright, API contract export/generated types and desktop TypeScript pass. Hosted Windows source runs [42791959669](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42791959669) and [42793462487](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42793462487) passed the full Python suite, browser/API checks and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped for this source-only change. No external OAuth provider, real user credential or model inference was used. Installed-app browser acceptance and broad provider interoperability remain open.

## Verify owner-driven MCP reconnect after live transport loss (2026-11-10)

Added a real stdio fixture that terminates its process during a tool call. The manager records the call as `unknown_outcome`, clears the dead connection and exposes an error state; the owner explicitly reconnects, the unchanged schema-matched read permission is restored, and a subsequent `add` call succeeds. The ambiguous call is not replayed. The full MCP stdio integration file passes (9 tests), with Ruff, formatting and strict manager Pyright checks passing. Hosted Windows source run [42789022373](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42789022373) passed the full source suite and UI/API checks. Automatic retries and third-party transport behavior remain unverified. No model inference was used.

## Reject MCP connections that exit during discovery (2026-11-10)

`MCPManager.connect_server` now checks that the connection-owner task is still alive after discovery and before publishing the server's tools. An immediate post-discovery exit is reported as a connection error and the manager does not register the stale connection. A deterministic integration regression reproduces the owner-task race. The MCP stdio suite passes (8), Ruff/format checks pass, and strict Pyright passes for the manager. Hosted Windows source run [42787219777](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42787219777) passed the full suite and UI/API checks; the installer job was skipped as expected for the feature push. No model inference was used.

## Correct installed backup acceptance data-root handoff (2026-11-10)

Hosted installer run [42738796927](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42738796927) passed the native restart, specialist recovery, frozen worker and private task-scope checks, then failed when the installed boot smoke could not see its seeded backup run. The fixture is stored under the isolated user's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XAA`, but `native_boot_smoke.mjs` set `XAA_DATA_DIR` to its separate evidence directory, so the engine opened a different database. The smoke now sets `XAA_DATA_DIR` to the same isolated local-data root used by fixture seeding. The local installed-package backup/restore acceptance passes against the staged installer and installed desktop; the restored run, four events, binary artifact, SQLite integrity and foreign-key checks all match. Evidence: `.acceptance/native-installed-backup-86876a9ff50e4ccf819292bc2996a959/report.json`. Hosted installer run [42740255498](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42740255498) then passed install, upgrade, boot, backup/restore, standard-user install, and uninstall; its report records `installed_backup_restore_verified: true`. No model inference was used.


## Fix native process-death installer acceptance database path (2026-11-10)

Dispatched hosted installer run [42736938234](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42736938234) exposed that `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs` seeded its crash-boundary output event into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XAA\xaa.sqlite3`, while its app process uses `XAA_DATA_DIR=<evidence-root>` and stores the database at `<evidence-root>\XAA\xaa.sqlite3`. The mismatch caused a foreign-key error because the wrong database contained no matching run. Corrected the smoke database path and reran the same Tauri desktop/engine death-and-restart scenario locally. It passes with one recovered candidate in `verifying`, the durable output marker restored, zero tool effects, unchanged workspace, and launch credentials cleaned. Evidence: `.acceptance/native-restart-37b9a4e6-899f-45eb-8867-15ab28809511/report.json`. Installer run [42740255498](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42740255498) passed this installed native restart check. The deterministic fixture made no LM Studio or external-provider inference calls.

## Verify MCP resource notifications over Streamable HTTP (2026-11-10)

Extended the real loopback Streamable HTTP MCP fixture to advertise legacy resource subscriptions, accept an owner subscription and emit update notifications during a tool request. The integration sends a burst of 130 server notifications; the client reports `resync_required`, retains only a bounded queue of at most 128 events, and labels returned notices untrusted. The test passes alongside the stdio lifecycle coverage (9 tests total); Ruff and formatting pass. Hosted Windows source run [42740232270](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42740232270) passed, including the full Python suite and browser/API checks. No model inference is used. The pinned SDK still does not expose the later `subscriptions/listen` protocol, and broad third-party compatibility remains unverified.

## Owner-controlled MCP resource update notifications (2026-11-09)

Added bounded resource subscriptions for advertised resources over the installed Python SDK's legacy MCP `resources/subscribe` protocol. The authenticated API exposes explicit subscribe/unsubscribe and an owner-triggered notification drain. Per-connection queues hold at most 128 events; overflow discards stale notices and emits `resync_required`. Only exact currently subscribed URIs are surfaced, events are labeled `untrusted_mcp_output`, and notifications never trigger resource reads or enter model context. The desktop provides explicit subscription buttons and a manual “Check for server updates” action, with escaped notice text and state cleared at disconnect. Stdio integration uses a real fixture process to subscribe, emit `notifications/resources/updated`, drain the untrusted notice and unsubscribe; API checks cover authentication and fail-closed handling. Focused MCP browser acceptance, Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, TypeScript, OpenAPI export and generated types pass. Native Tauri build and MCP acceptance pass with inference disabled; native evidence: `.acceptance/f0910f62-5f6f-41df-9da7-8f76a1a0028d/`. Hosted Windows source run [42735968523](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42735968523) passed the full source workflow, including the complete Python suite; the installer job was skipped on this feature branch. At this checkpoint, Streamable HTTP notification delivery and overflow stress acceptance remained open. The pinned SDK negotiates MCP 2025-11-25 and does not expose the later `subscriptions/listen` stream. No live model inference was used.

Updated 2026-11-10.

## Owner-only MCP resource-template discovery (2026-11-09)

Added authenticated bounded resource-template discovery and owner-triggered reads over stdio and Streamable HTTP. Full RFC 6570 expansion uses the pinned `uritemplate` package; the owner supplies every template variable explicitly as text. Catalogs are limited to eight pages, 256 entries and 256 KiB; each argument is capped at 8 KiB, aggregate arguments at 32 KiB, expanded URIs at 2048 characters, and cached expanded URIs at 256 per connection. A read accepts only a template from the latest catalog, percent-encodes values according to RFC 6570, and returns bounded content marked `untrusted_mcp_output`. The UI renders results as escaped text and keeps them out of model context. The stdio API test checks auth, variable validation, argument bounds and returned trust; loopback HTTP verifies expansion/encoding; browser acceptance covers the owner form; rebuilt native Tauri acceptance verifies the bundled engine/desktop, read label and clean shutdown. Evidence: `.acceptance/3d76fa92-db1e-4041-89e1-1ba13af641c6/report.json`. Focused backend tests pass (8), browser acceptance, TypeScript/API contract checks, Ruff, strict Pyright, formatting and frozen-engine build pass. The package inventory and provisional notices include the new dependency; source archive and exact BSD/Apache license texts are hash-recorded under `.acceptance/uritemplate-license-audit-20261109/`. Hosted Windows source run [42733046548](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42733046548) passed the full source workflow for template reads; the installer job was skipped on this feature branch. Prompt use in agent context, sampling, subscriptions/change notifications and third-party compatibility remain open. No model inference was used.

## Owner-only MCP prompt preview (2026-11-09)

Added authenticated prompt catalog and preview actions for connected MCP servers. Preview is restricted to the current advertised prompt, validates required and declared arguments, caps argument count/size, serializes text, image, audio, embedded resource and resource-link blocks within a 256 KiB response, and labels all server content untrusted. The desktop renders the preview as escaped text and does not add the prompt or returned content to any model context or run tool list. Stdio and loopback Streamable HTTP integration tests cover listing and text preview; unit tests cover all supported content blocks, bad base64 and size limits. The browser UI test passes. Native Tauri acceptance passes with the fixture, verifying owner prompt listing and preview, the untrusted marker and graceful shutdown with no model inference; evidence: `.acceptance/a60120c6-f629-451c-bae0-b62ce0767741/report.json`. Local checks pass: 11 combined MCP unit/integration tests, desktop browser acceptance, native MCP acceptance, TypeScript, OpenAPI/generated types, Ruff, strict Pyright and formatting. Hosted Windows source run [42727812139](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42727812139) passed; the installer job was skipped for this feature branch. Prompt use in agent model context, sampling, subscriptions and broad third-party compatibility remain open; this remains partial MCP support.

## Owner-triggered MCP resource inspection (2026-11-09)

Added authenticated owner actions to list and read resources from an already connected MCP server. Discovery is limited to eight pages and 256 resource entries. Reads require a URI in that connection's latest owner-requested catalog, time out, reject invalid base64 and oversized content, and cap the serialized response at 256 KiB. Results are rendered as escaped text with an explicit untrusted label and are never added to run prompts, tool catalogs or model context. The real stdio SDK fixture verifies authentication, text reading, unadvertised URI rejection and oversized-output refusal; the loopback Streamable HTTP fixture also verifies catalog/read with bearer-token delivery, and the Playwright desktop test verifies listing, owner-triggered reading and the trust label. The native Windows acceptance runs the staged Tauri desktop and engine, exercises the same fixture resource flow in the UI, verifies authenticated API results and confirms graceful engine shutdown. Evidence is `.acceptance/56332220-43cb-4c54-ad17-fbed17c9e613/report.json`; it records no model inference. Its first attempt exposed and helped fix the test-only `XAA_DATA_DIR` environment handoff, so the acceptance uses its own temporary database. Local verification passes: all 6 MCP stdio tests, both MCP HTTP tests, the MCP browser acceptance, native MCP acceptance, TypeScript typecheck, OpenAPI export/generated type checks, Ruff, strict Pyright and `git diff --check`. Hosted Windows source run [42724716201](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42724716201) passed after that fix, including the full Python suite and recovery acceptances; the installer job was skipped on this feature branch. Prompt use in agent context, sampling, subscriptions/change notifications, installed-release and broader third-party acceptance remain open; this is a partial MCP capability slice, not broad protocol parity.

## Verify installation under a disposable standard Windows account (2026-11-09)

The hosted Windows installer workflow creates a random local non-admin user and launches a separate PowerShell process with that user's loaded profile. The acceptance installs the package with no `/D`, verifies the default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Xyst AI Agent` path, manifest desktop/engine hashes and the packaged ACP CLI, then silently uninstalls and checks that binaries are removed while a disposable data marker and Tauri config marker remain. The test copies its inputs under a `RUNNER_TEMP`-contained directory, grants access only to the new account, and removes the created account, profile and fixture in guarded cleanup. Its JSON report is uploaded with the installer artifacts. Runs [42694973681](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42694973681) and [42697795339](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42697795339) exposed three harness issues: Windows' 48-character account-description limit, inherited `runneradmin` environment paths in the child process, and assignment of parent report fields that were absent from the child JSON. These were corrected in `cf84633` and `d025d01`. Hosted installer run [42701055533](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42701055533) passed the full job, including this standard-account acceptance and explicit data-removal acceptance. Its standard-user report confirms account SID/profile matching, non-admin token, default per-user install path, desktop/engine hash matches, packaged ACP CLI success, silent installer and uninstaller exit codes 0, binary removal, and preservation of local data and roaming config markers. Follow-up run [42705466957](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42705466957) passed after adding post-cleanup assertions; its report explicitly records `standard_user_profile_removed: true` and `standard_user_account_removed: true`. The report also confirms `model_inference_requested: false` and is preserved in `.acceptance/hosted-standard-user-42705466957/build/installer/standard-user-smoke.json`. This verifies the disposable non-admin install path and cleanup on the hosted runner; broader clean-machine migration, signing and license review remain open.

## Fail closed on blocked multi-hop browser redirects (2026-11-09)

The live Edge redirect smoke now exercises an approved two-hop chain and a chain whose second hop leaves the run's allowlist. The blocked chain exposed that Edge can remain on the intermediate redirect response after Playwright aborts the final top-level request; the worker previously returned that blank intermediate page as a successful read. The route guard now counts blocked main-frame navigation requests, and the worker returns an explicit `read_failed`; blocked off-host subresources and child frames remain recorded/blocked without failing the approved main page. Unit coverage passes (32 tests), Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass, and the live Microsoft Edge smoke passes all five cases. Local evidence: `.acceptance/browser-redirect-edge-2f28d7b115be40429393a8dac3aef620/report.json`. Hosted Windows run [42690959009](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42690959009) passed the Edge smoke and complete source workflow (934 Python tests passed, Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript, generated API contracts, and browser/recovery acceptances). The hosted five-case report is preserved in `.acceptance/hosted-browser-redirect-multihop-42690959009/`. No live model inference was used.

## Exercise the installer’s default per-user location (2026-11-09)

Both Windows installer acceptance scripts previously supplied NSIS `/D` paths under `RUNNER_TEMP`, so their green install tests did not exercise the declared `currentUser` default. The installer builder now refuses a non-`currentUser` release config and includes the declared scope in its manifest. The preservation/upgrade smoke installs the current package without `/D` under isolated `LOCALAPPDATA` and requires its binaries at the declared default product directory. The archived 0.1.0 predecessor predates the current `currentUser` mode, so only that fixture installer receives `/D` to seed the upgrade at the new package's expected directory. Hosted source runs [42673979632](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42673979632), [42676852380](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42676852380) and [42680074073](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42680074073) passed all checks, including the full Python suite and restart acceptances. Installer run [42675153777](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42675153777) exposed the predecessor-path issue, fixed in `b0b1ae6`. Rerun [42677976490](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42677976490) passed install, upgrade, schema-18 migration, boot, preservation and uninstall at the current package's default per-user directory. Installer run [42681289556](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42681289556) confirmed that default-path flow, while delete-data acceptance still failed before the explicit choice. Run [42684235900](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42684235900) passed the default-path upgrade/install/uninstall checks but failed the removal assertion because the harness put markers under a fake `LOCALAPPDATA` while NSIS used the runner's actual profile. Run [42686208558](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42686208558) confirmed the default-path flow again and exposed a second harness issue: it attempted to recreate the runner's existing `%LOCALAPPDATA%` before reaching the UI. The redundant directory creation was removed. Hosted run [42687894644](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42687894644) passed the complete installer job: the default-path install/upgrade preserved user data and migrated the schema-18 fixture, and the interactive uninstall acceptance recorded `explicit_delete_selected`, removed the guarded local and config data directories plus binaries, clicked the final Close button, and exited 0. Its reports are preserved in `.acceptance/hosted-currentuser-42687894644/build/installer/`. The acceptance uses no model inference. The dedicated disposable standard-account installation acceptance has now been added to the hosted workflow and awaits its first result; broader clean-machine migration and signing remain open.

## Repair the real schema-20 identity-link migration path (2026-11-09)

The schema-20 upgrade test previously removed the new identity-link tables but left the old `channel_bindings` table in its current shape, so it missed that a real schema-20 database has no `identity_link_id` column. Startup attempted to create an index on that missing column before running the additive migration and failed. Index creation now follows a transactional conditional column addition; the regression downgrades the table itself and confirms the existing binding, column, index and schema upgrade survive. The schema-18 installer fixture was also not actually schema 18: it was created by the current `RunStore` and merely labeled as old. It now removes the schema-19-through-22 structures and sets `user_version=18`, then verifies a source-engine migration to schema 22 with the run, events, artifact metadata and exact artifact bytes unchanged, SQLite integrity `ok` and no FK violations. Local acceptance: `.acceptance/schema18-upgrade-fixture-20261109/verify.json`. Hosted installer run [42668125217](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42668125217) passed install, upgrade, backup/restore and uninstall with this corrected seed helper, but its uploaded report did not expose the seed schema version. The follow-up assertion/report run [42670883620](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42670883620) timed out after 15 seconds starting the frozen engine's ACP version command, before upgrade seeding; a retry and fresh dispatch created no jobs. Hosted confirmation of the explicit schema-version assertion remains pending. This does not establish every historical profile or real-user recovery.

## Verify schedule persistence through two native desktop cold starts (2026-11-09)

Added a packaged-desktop acceptance that seeds one overdue one-time schedule with `missed_run_policy=skip`, launches the actual Tauri desktop and bundled engine, waits for the scheduler to persist one `missed` occurrence, shuts the whole desktop process down, and launches it again against the same isolated profile. The second cold start must show the same execution and occurrence IDs, exactly one schedule execution, zero agent runs, and authenticated API access. This path requests no inference and makes no Telegram request. The local native development build passed at [report.json](../.acceptance/native-schedule-restart-7193fd76-e169-417b-beaf-1c010a8a02f2/report.json); its JSON console summary also parses through the PowerShell installer harness. Ruff, Pyright, Node syntax, PowerShell parsing and `git diff --check` pass. The fixture, Playwright smoke and installer CI gate passed against the installed package in hosted Windows run [42663945918](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42663945918). The complete installer smoke report and nested schedule report are retained at [.acceptance/hosted-scheduled-cold-start-42663945918-attempt2](../.acceptance/hosted-scheduled-cold-start-42663945918-attempt2); they confirm installed boot, two process restarts, one `missed` execution, zero runs, and no inference or Telegram request. This verifies the missed-run policy and schedule persistence across installed desktop restarts, not delivery of an on-time schedule or live channel behavior.

## Recover a due schedule exactly once across fresh processes (2026-11-09)

Added `scripts/schedule_restart_diagnostic.py` to cover the scheduler dispatch crash gap at the OS-process boundary. It creates one due schedule and durable queued run while omitting the submit call, launches a fresh process with the real `RunSupervisor` and a no-inference fixture coordinator, then launches a second fresh process and proves the terminal run is not dispatched again. The report requires one schedule execution, one dispatch, a different worker PID, and `model_inference_requested: false`. Local acceptance passed at [report.json](../.acceptance/schedule-restart-34c8c0e0-a687-423a-8e4d-d81709565d28/report.json); the scheduler suite passes **12 tests**, Ruff and script Pyright pass, and `git diff --check` passes. Hosted Windows source run [42659063914](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42659063914) passed the full Python suite, source/static checks, browser acceptances, pinned Hermes inventories, specialist/Telegram recovery diagnostics, and this scheduled-run restart acceptance. The installer job was skipped because packaging did not change. This establishes source-process recovery, not a cold packaged desktop restart or real model execution.

## Reconcile scheduled Telegram outbox in a fresh process (2026-11-09)

Strengthened the deterministic Telegram crash diagnostic: after the worker is killed at the fsynced simulated-accept boundary, a separate Python process now opens the same SQLite database and performs restart reconciliation. Both scheduled-outbox and inbound-reply cases require the saved state to change from `sending` to `unknown` while the accepted-effect log remains exactly one entry. No bot token, Telegram endpoint, model inference or notification was used. Local verification passed for both diagnostics, the Telegram/scheduler suites (**34 passed**), Ruff lint/format, script Pyright (zero issues), and `git diff --check`. Reports: [scheduled outbox](../.acceptance/telegram-crash-c4582f9c-fd60-4422-91d3-d35b32c534d4/report.json) and [inbound reply](../.acceptance/telegram-crash-3ba9a994-95ca-4872-ac76-0455c2658e3a/report.json). Hosted Windows source run [42657511512](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42657511512) passed the full Python suite, source/static checks, browser acceptances, Hermes inventories, and both process-kill recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because packaging did not change. This proves fresh-process channel-service reconciliation, not a full desktop restart or live Telegram delivery.

## Add local microphone capture to voice input (2026-11-09)

The desktop can now record from the user's microphone after an explicit click, encode the captured audio locally as mono 16 kHz PCM WAV, and attach it through the existing loopback transcription path. Recording stops at 131 seconds to keep the decoded WAV within 4 MiB; stream tracks are stopped after recording, and the fake-device acceptance confirms every captured track ends; permission/conversion failures are displayed in the UI. Starting a recording clears a previously selected clip and disables run submission and media replacement through recording and conversion, preventing a stale attachment from being submitted. Microsoft Edge's fake-device browser acceptance checks this replacement/busy behavior, recording, WAV format/sample rate/size, and submission to a fixture transcription URL; the existing WAV attachment workflow remains covered. Desktop TypeScript checks pass and both voice-input browser tests pass. No live model, transcription service, Discord, or external audio service was used. Real microphone hardware and transcription accuracy remain unverified; the browser acceptance injects a denied permission response and verifies the actionable error plus a successful retry.

The native desktop smoke now exercises the same microphone flow inside a real Tauri WebView using Chromium's fake audio device. After the harmless diagnostic fixture, it records, checks the attachment, verifies all stream tracks are ended, clears the attachment, and continues the existing browser-cancellation and reviewed workspace-read workflow. Local acceptance passed with the rebuilt development binary at `.acceptance/native-browser-ui-cancel-bd3827c4-ee04-4f1c-afd8-fbdcd8a93c09/report.json`; `model_inference_requested` is false and launch credentials were cleaned. The installed-package acceptance requires this microphone result and applies fake-media flags only to the disposable CI runner. Hosted installer run [42655212402](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42655212402) passed both Windows package jobs; `.acceptance/hosted-voice-installer-42655212402/build/installer/install-smoke.json` and its nested native-browser report confirm microphone capture, ended tracks, attachment clearing, no transcription, and no inference. The same run passed install/boot/upgrade, backup/restore, uninstall and data preservation. Physical microphone hardware and ASR accuracy remain unverified.

## Reject truncated PCM WAV before transcription (2026-11-09)

The WAV validator previously trusted the RIFF frame-count header without reading the declared PCM payload, allowing a truncated file to reach the selected transcription endpoint. It now reads the bounded declared frames and verifies the sample byte count matches channels, sample width and frame count; a regression removes sample bytes while retaining the original header and requires rejection. The API/loopback acceptance also posts the truncated data URL first and verifies the ASR fixture receives no request, then completes a valid case. `tests/unit/test_local_transcription.py` plus `tests/unit/test_api_voice.py` pass (6 tests); Ruff, formatting, engine Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42645212802](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42645212802) passed the full source job, including 931 Python tests, the 976-entry catalog drift check, browser acceptances and process-recovery diagnostics. Its first attempt hit an HTTPBIN navigation timeout in the redirect smoke; retrying the failed job passed. No audio was sent to an external service and no model inference was used.

## Index every pinned Hermes plugin catalog category (2026-11-09)

The existing Hermes audit covered all 49 `platform` category entries, while its own scope notes left other catalog categories unindexed. Added a deterministic inventory of every tracked YAML manifest in the pinned catalog, recording category, pinned repo/commit/subdirectory/version metadata, manifest hashes, and declared required environment names. It indexes 976 entries across automation (56), desktop (215), general (62), memory (76), models (90), platform (49), tools (349), voice (26), web (50), and three unspecified category manifests. The command verifies the external checkout is at the exact pinned commit and clean; Windows source CI now checks inventory drift. Local verification passes the generator and `--check`, existing channel/execution drift checks, Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, and the full suite (**929 passed, 1 skipped**). An initial full-suite attempt hit Windows `PermissionError` creating the shared `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\pytest-of-hicks` base; the clean rerun used a dedicated workspace temp directory and passed. Hosted Windows run [42645212802](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42645212802) passed the full source job with 931 Python tests and all three recovery diagnostics. Its first redirect-smoke attempt timed out at the public HTTPBIN fixture; retry passed. This establishes discovery coverage across catalog categories and traceability for Phase 9 candidate work, not source/runtime parity; non-platform candidates remain unreviewed and uncatalogued capabilities remain outside the index. The updated evidence boundaries are in `docs/parity/catalog-inventory.json`, `docs/parity/channel-requirements.md`, and `docs/parity/external-plugin-source-audit.md`. No live model or external plugin was used.

## Resume exact-count tasks interrupted before model admission (2026-11-09)

When exact token reservation is mandatory, a specialist attempt with no saved token hold and no response cannot have reached model generation: the provider must durably reserve before sending. Recovery now distinguishes those preflight attempts from requests with unknown usage and permits owner-reviewed resume when the iteration, time, and token budgets still allow it. A saved hold, an answer without measured usage, or any other unknown attempt still blocks resume. The recovery regression proves mandatory exact mode requeues this safe task while ordinary mode remains blocked. `tests/unit/test_specialist_recovery.py` and `tests/unit/test_task_claims.py` pass (20 tests); Ruff, formatting, engine Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used.

## Preserve shared token admission for concurrent specialists (2026-11-09)

Exact-context mode dispatches two independent specialist assignments concurrently and stores a shared token hold before each provider request. The pre-inference gate previously treated another exact-counting attempt as unknown usage before its hold was written, causing parallel runs to block or fail. In mandatory reservation mode the gate now compares measured usage plus outstanding durable holds; reservation admission still enforces the shared ceiling transactionally. Runs without mandatory exact reservations retain fail-closed handling for unknown usage and sequential specialist dispatch. The specialist fixture now delays exact admission to exercise overlapping unmeasured attempts. The targeted orchestration/token-budget/review suites pass (62 tests); Ruff, formatting, engine Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42637968655](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42637968655) passed the full 928-test suite, source and browser checks, Hermes audits, and process-recovery acceptances; the installer job was skipped for this source-only change. No live model inference was used.

## Require final project validation after direct deletions (2026-11-09)

Final review already required fresh current-project command evidence after direct file creates/edits and accepted specialist integrations, but its detector missed completed direct `workspace.delete_file` calls. It now treats a journaled deletion confirmed with `deleted: true` as a project change. The regression test passes with the full `tests/unit/test_run_review.py` file (14 passed); Ruff, engine Pyright, and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42633385434](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42633385434) passed the complete source job, including 928 Python tests, Hermes audits, static checks, desktop acceptances and recovery diagnostics; the installer job was skipped because this feature branch did not change packaging. This keeps the Phase 4 gate accurate and does not require live model inference.

## Preserve schedule occurrences across DST gaps and clock folds (2026-11-09)

Fixed the local-time due calculation for daily schedules after downtime: when the scheduler comes back at 01:00 on spring-forward day, it records the older overdue occurrence as `missed` and leaves that day's nonexistent 02:30 occurrence pending until the clock has advanced past the gap, when it records `dst_skipped`. Fall-back ambiguity now respects the documented earlier-instant choice: a 01:30 schedule runs once at the first occurrence and is not delayed or duplicated during the repeated hour. The regression tests exercise durable schedule ticks across both transitions. `uv run pytest tests/unit/test_schedules.py -q` passes (12); Ruff and formatting pass, engine Pyright reports zero issues, and `git diff --check` passes. Hosted Windows source run [42631184054](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42631184054) passed all 927 Python tests, source/static checks, browser acceptances and recovery diagnostics for commit `d6a573a`; installer job was skipped because this source change did not affect packaging. No model inference or external service was used.

## Cancel a managed browser read and finish a workspace task through the desktop (2026-11-09)

Extended the Windows native desktop WebView acceptance with a complete deterministic workspace task. The UI configures a loopback OpenAI-compatible fixture, runs the harmless tool-use diagnostic, starts and cancels an approved delayed browser read, then starts a separate workspace run. The fake provider requests `workspace_read_file` for a random marker; the acceptance checks the saved tool result and visible candidate, accepts it through the desktop's objective/evidence review, and verifies the run reaches `completed` without changing the fixture. Local evidence: [`native-browser-ui-cancel-3c0e5e90-e6b5-4404-8716-8751cc36c0ff/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-browser-ui-cancel-3c0e5e90-e6b5-4404-8716-8751cc36c0ff/report.json). No live model inference was requested, and launch credentials were cleaned. Hosted Windows run [42628362113](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42628362113) passed the complete installer acceptance with the expanded flow against the installed binary; [`install-smoke.json`](../.acceptance/hosted-installer-42628362113/build/installer/install-smoke.json) confirms distinct cancelled and completed run IDs, saved browser cancellation, saved workspace read, explicit desktop review, clean exit and cleaned launch credentials. The previous installer-only run [42624437546](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42624437546) passed cancellation before the workspace extension was added. This verifies the desktop coordinator/review path with a deterministic provider, not real LM Studio compatibility or the Phase 6 real-model acceptance gate. Other installed desktop workflows and browser lifecycle boundaries remain open.

## Verify the current release notices in the installed Windows package (2026-11-09)

Added a manual `workflow_dispatch` path that runs only the hosted Windows installer job on a selected branch, so package acceptance can run before changes reach `main` without repeating the full source suite. Manual run [42620161353](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42620161353) built the unsigned 0.1.1 installer from commit `c4d9dbc` and passed. The installed smoke confirms all three release notice files match their manifest hashes, the 0.1.0→0.1.1 upgrade preserves seeded run/event/artifact data, installed browser and backup/restore acceptance pass, default uninstall preserves user data and removes binaries, and the explicit removal choice deletes application data and exits cleanly. This verifies packaging and install behavior, not license compatibility or the owner-selected X.A.A. application license; those release decisions remain open. No model inference was used.

It dispatches `cmake --workflow --preset NAME` through the existing restricted-command review, avoiding generated shell commands and guessed build directories. ## User operating guide (2026-11-09)

Refreshed the existing `docs/user-guide.md` to date its development-preview scope and align its cross-interface/release limits with current evidence. The repository README and 0.1.1 development-build notes link the guide. It covers Windows development-build scope, LM Studio setup, diagnostic-gated onboarding, Observe/Workspace permissions, explicit candidate and conflict review, run recovery, saved memory/procedure behavior, optional integration boundaries, portable backup/restore, and desktop shutdown settings. The guide distinguishes supported workflows from partial or unverified integrations and does not claim full Hermes parity or release readiness. The README's historical 16% completion figure was removed; one unweighted percentage is not supportable from the broad objective phases. Internal guide links and `git diff --check` were reviewed; CI/package verification is pending.

## Validation and channel scope direction (2026-11-08)

Per the owner's direction, defer additional live LLM testing until the final acceptance pass unless a specific implementation cannot be meaningfully verified without it. Continue deterministic fixtures, static checks, packaging checks and non-model integrations as needed; existing live-model reports remain historical evidence and must not be treated as a reason to keep running models now. Discord is one implementation choice for the objective's first messaging adapter, not a required platform named by the objective. Keep its current partial work accurately documented and avoid expanding Discord-specific scope unless a required acceptance gap or explicit owner priority calls for it.

## Reconcile a completed workspace deletion after restart (2026-11-09)

The coordinator already reconciled interrupted creates and edits against their exact requested bytes, and the filesystem worker already knew how to identify a deletion whose destination is absent. The restart dispatch omitted `workspace.delete_file`, so an applied deletion stayed unresolved and blocked the run. Startup reconciliation now includes deletes. Expired runs with only version-1 workspace read/list/search intents or workspace-mode direct create/edit/delete intents now re-enter the coordinator for deterministic recovery. The coordinator seals interrupted reads without inventing content, confirms only exact applied filesystem states, and leaves missing/unchanged/conflicting writes or any unsupported effect unresolved and blocked before inference. A deterministic restart regression seeds the original assistant proposal, durable delete intent and execution marker, removes the file before result persistence, expires ownership, and verifies startup recovery requeues the run, attaches the reconciled deletion result to the original call, then continues. The focused recovery set passes (24 tests); the full local Python suite passes (907 passed, 1 skipped); Ruff, engine Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. No live model inference was used. Conflicting replacement files, external effects, full reboot and power-loss recovery remain open.

## Seal interrupted executed reads without replay (2026-11-09)

Recovery previously left a version-1 workspace read unresolved when a `tool.executing` event had been saved, although reads have no side effects and no saved result exists to trust. Recovery now closes that call with an explicit `interrupted_read`/`read_only` failure and requires a fresh read if its contents are still needed. It continues leaving unsupported versions and side-effecting calls unresolved. The focused read-recovery suite passes (6 tests), including an execution-marker case that checks the workspace bytes remain unchanged. The full local suite passes (907 passed, 1 skipped); Ruff, formatting, engine Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42600832069](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42600832069) passed both source and installer jobs for the preceding checkpoint; this follow-up awaits its own hosted run. Live LM Studio testing remains deferred to final acceptance per owner direction; no model inference was used for this fix.

## Recover interrupted procedure and Git reads (2026-11-09)

Version-1 intents for local, read-only `procedure.view`, `procedure.read_resource` and `procedure.catalog` now permit startup requeue and are sealed with an explicit `interrupted_read` result if no result was persisted; the old call is never replayed, and a new read can be requested. External web/browser requests, commands, unknown tools and side-effecting procedure edits remain outside this safe-read allowlist. Git inspection was initially excluded because it creates a managed artifact; the follow-up below adds exact cleanup for that artifact. Fourteen focused recovery/restart tests passed before the Git cleanup change; no model inference was used.

## Recover interrupted Git inspection artifacts (2026-11-09)

Git status/diff calls now persist a version-2 unique managed-artifact filename in their intent before starting the subprocess. After a crash, recovery validates the UUID leaf name and exact run/call provenance, removes only that operation's incomplete or uncommitted artifact, deletes matching artifact metadata if it was registered, records `artifact.discarded` for desktop refresh, and seals the tool call as `interrupted_read`. Malformed names, symlinks, mismatched provenance and legacy version-1 intents remain unresolved/blocked; Git is not rerun automatically. Eighteen focused recovery/Git coordinator tests pass, as do Ruff, formatting, engine Pyright, TypeScript typecheck, 15 desktop client tests, and `git diff --check`. Hosted run [42604712667](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42604712667) passed both source and installer jobs for commit `e83bcc4`. No model inference was used.

## Recover interrupted user-approved MCP reads (2026-11-09)

Parent-run MCP intents now persist version 2 and the exact configured tool's saved user permission (`read` or `write`). An expired parent run may requeue only a version-2 MCP call saved as `read`, and recovery seals its missing result as `interrupted_read` without reconnecting or replaying the external request. The coordinator also passes `require_read` through invocation so a permission change between cataloging and dispatch cannot turn a read proposal into a write. Saved writes and legacy MCP intents remain blocked for explicit reconciliation. The 36-case recovery/coordinator/MCP stdio group passes; Ruff, formatting and engine Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42606527297](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42606527297) passed both source and installer jobs for commit `7a6c06e`, including the full Python suite, specialist recovery, desktop UI checks, packaged recovery, and install/upgrade/uninstall acceptance. No live model inference was used.

## Quarantine malformed interrupted read intents (2026-11-09)

Startup recovery validates that safe-read intents contain JSON objects before treating them as recoverable. It also validates the exact version-1 argument shape of authorized workspace create/edit/delete intents before requeueing them for filesystem reconciliation. Malformed JSON, non-object values, unexpected fields, and invalid hashes keep the expired run blocked instead of sending damaged records into the recovery resolver. The unresolved-call listing tolerates damaged argument JSON and reports an empty object with `arguments_invalid=true`, allowing owner inspection without trusting or executing the corrupted payload. Four regressions corrupt a read, create, edit, and delete intent and verify blocked state plus inspectable unresolved status. The earlier read-only change passed hosted Windows run [42607948399](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42607948399); the expanded workspace-write validation passes 27 focused API/recovery/coordinator tests, Ruff, formatting, full engine/scripts Pyright, and `git diff --check`. Hosted Windows run [42609760104](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42609760104) passed both source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite and installed-package acceptance. No model inference was used.

## Align frozen recovery acceptance with automatic restart reconciliation (2026-11-09)

Hosted run [42598554973](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42598554973) passed the Windows source job (full tests and static/client checks) but its installer acceptance stopped at the first owner-reviewed workspace-write scenario. The diagnostic expected an expired run with a supported filesystem intent to stay blocked, while the new startup path correctly requeued it for coordinator reconciliation. Updated the acceptance to execute coordinator recovery through the staged frozen worker before simulating the owner decision. The owner scenarios now preserve meaningful ambiguous states: a partial create is removed after inspection, an edit is interrupted before its execution event and keeps the saved original hash, and a deletion is interrupted after journaling but before unlink so the original file remains. Local frozen-worker reports pass in `.acceptance/owner-review-current-create-20261109/`, `.acceptance/owner-review-current-edit-20261109/`, and `.acceptance/owner-review-current-delete-20261109/`; an applied deletion auto-reconciliation report passes in `.acceptance/applied-recovery-delete-20261109/`. The revised workflow has been pushed for hosted revalidation. Full local Python suite passes (907 passed, 1 skipped); no model inference was used. Whole-machine reboot/power-loss and external-effect recovery remain open.
## Restore unfinished work when the desktop reconnects (2026-11-08)

The desktop now asks the authenticated API for the most recently updated nonterminal run when it connects and opens its saved activity, so a blocked run's owner-recovery controls appear after a desktop restart without searching history. A partial SQLite index serves this lookup directly rather than scanning pages of completed history. Opening a run only reloads saved plan and events; it never resumes the coordinator. A deterministic browser acceptance inspects and resolves an interrupted edit through the recovery panel, and asserts no resume request; storage/API tests cover choosing an unfinished run over a newer completed run, index use and authentication. Local checks: `tests/browser/startup-recovery.spec.ts`, `tests/unit/test_run_history.py`, TypeScript typecheck and production desktop build pass. Hosted Windows run [42553111903](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42553111903) passed both jobs, including the startup recovery UI step, full Python suite (907 passed), and installer acceptance. No model inference was used.

The installed desktop acceptance seeds a blocked edit into the packaged engine database before launch, then verifies a cold desktop start restores the saved run in the actual Tauri WebView. Owner-reviewed recovery leaves the run blocked, preserves the workspace hash, closes the intent without replay, and makes neither resume nor model inference calls. The cold-start variant passed with both the development package (`.acceptance/54728050-c466-4a1c-8f9f-6014c188909f/`) and the exact hosted installer artifact combined with its backup fixture (`.acceptance/native-installed-repro-42555801850/`). Hosted run [42555801850](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42555801850) passed the source job but its installer smoke did not find the restored run. Failure diagnostics captured the issue; the rerun [42557258475](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42557258475) passed both Windows jobs. Its uploaded installer report confirms cold-start recovery, owner review, zero unresolved intents, an unchanged workspace hash, no resume, and no model inference.

Expanded the packaged cold-start recovery fixture to cover all three direct workspace write operations together. The real installed desktop displays interrupted create/edit/delete intents; acceptance removes the partial create after inspection, confirms the edit and deletion only with their saved original hashes restored, and verifies all three original calls close as `not_applied_after_review`. The run stays blocked, the partial create remains absent, both restored files retain their original SHA-256, and no resume or inference is requested. Local installed-package evidence is in `.acceptance/native-installed-multirecovery2-20261108/acceptance/report.json`. Hosted installer run [42593816424](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42593816424) passes the expanded acceptance; its uploaded report confirms all three call IDs resolved, zero unresolved intents, no resume, and no model inference.

Hosted run [42558548184](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42558548184) passed the source job but its installer failed before recovery: the clean machine's frozen engine started after the desktop's 10-second connection retry had expired, leaving the UI on manual-connect. The smoke's direct API probe confirmed the engine later became ready. Extended desktop startup retries to 60 seconds, matching the packaged acceptance deadline, and added a browser regression that injects 41 transient connection failures before the API becomes ready. Both startup-recovery browser tests pass locally, and hosted run [42593816424](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42593816424) passes both Windows jobs with installer cold-start recovery.

## Completion accounting

The rough percentage estimates in older entries below were copied forward without recalculation and are historical. Current broad-spec completion is estimated at **about 56%** as of 2026-11-10: phases 0–10 are equally weighted, completed gates receive full credit, selected/partial evidence receives proportionate credit, and incomplete parity receives low credit. This is a planning estimate, not a claim that the release criteria are 56% verified. Phase 10 now has hosted Windows evidence for install, upgrade, recovery, backup/restore, standard-user install and uninstall in [run 42740255498](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42740255498); signing, clean end-user-machine coverage, third-party notice/legal review and the wider Phase 9 parity gate remain open. Further live LLM acceptance remains deferred until the end as requested.
## Objective phase-gate snapshot (2026-11-08)

| Phase | Current gate assessment | Main remaining evidence or work |
|---|---|---|
| 0 — Scope/contracts | Partial | The pinned Hermes matrix, architecture, contracts and schema exist. Static channel/backend inventory drift checks now pass in CI against the exact Hermes pin ([run 42475010536](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42475010536)); all 49 external platform-category candidates now have pinned source evidence, including the three formerly metadata-only exclusions. Broader capability/catalog discovery and exhaustive source-level parity remain open. |
| 1 — Local inference | Verified on selected local models | Native LM Studio discovery and harmless tool round trips pass; broad model compatibility and hardware/resource benchmarking remain open. |
| 2 — Durable execution | Partial | Source, frozen-engine and Tauri restart/reconciliation paths cover selected crash boundaries. Owner-reviewed recovery can now resume a mandatory exact-count specialist interrupted before its durable token hold, subject to remaining budgets; requests with holds or unknown responses stay blocked. Uncertain workspace creates, edits and reviewed deletions can now be resolved by an owner after removing a partial create or restoring the saved pre-write hash; the filesystem worker rechecks that state and records a failed result without replay. Hosted run [42547365385](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42547365385) verifies owner recovery for a create, [42548304333](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42548304333) verifies an edit restored to its saved pre-write hash, and [42550134883](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42550134883) verifies a reviewed deletion restored before its result was persisted. Ordinary Telegram, scheduled Telegram and Email ambiguous replies now have account-scoped, owner-reviewed reconciliation with explicit audited retry; MCP ambiguous writes block before another model turn, and delegated specialist MCP reads now persist schema-bound intents and recover without replay. An opt-in Windows sign-in startup path launches the desktop hidden to the tray; native acceptance verifies the real `--background` argument keeps the window hidden while the bundled engine starts (`.acceptance/78fb2ea9-b41f-4853-ad47-36b9824e4ffc/`). Hosted run [42542169124](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42542169124) verifies the startup entry survives upgrade and is removed on uninstall. Hosted run [42553111903](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42553111903) verifies the desktop restores the most recently updated unfinished run, exposes the owner recovery panel without resuming it, passes the 907-test source suite, and passes installer acceptance. The local installed-UI cold-start acceptance `.acceptance/54728050-c466-4a1c-8f9f-6014c188909f/` and hosted installer acceptance in run [42557258475](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42557258475) seed the blocked record before desktop launch, restore it from SQLite, complete owner review without replay or resume, and verify no inference. The exact installer artifact with a combined backup profile also passed locally (`.acceptance/native-installed-repro-42555801850/`). The expanded create/edit/delete cold-start acceptance passes locally in `.acceptance/native-installed-multirecovery2-20261108/acceptance/report.json` and in hosted installer run [42593816424](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42593816424); the uploaded report confirms all three calls resolved, zero unresolved intents, no resume and no inference. Hosted run [42558548184](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42558548184) exposed a clean-package startup race: the frozen engine could become ready after the desktop's 10-second API retry window, leaving the app in manual-connect mode. Desktop startup now retries for 60 seconds, and the browser regression forces 41 transient failures before success; hosted run [42593816424](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42593816424) passes source and installer jobs with the fix. Whole-machine reboot/power-loss recovery, broader external-effect reconciliation and full recovery remain open. Expired runs containing only known version-1 workspace reads or direct file writes now auto-requeue for fresh-process reconciliation. Exact applied create/edit/delete results are persisted and safely replayed from the journal; unresolved or unsupported effects remain blocked. This automatic startup path is covered locally. Hosted run [42598554973](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42598554973) passed source checks but exposed a stale installer acceptance assumption; the acceptance now follows automatic requeue and frozen coordinator recovery, with hosted revalidation pending. Hosted run [42547365385](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42547365385) now verifies the frozen worker can close an interrupted create after the owner removes the inspected partial file; hosted run [42548304333](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42548304333) also verifies an interrupted edit after restoring its saved pre-write hash. |
