## Owner-reviewed workspace write recovery (2026-11-08)

A blocked run now exposes unresolved direct create/edit intents as bounded path/hash metadata. If an interrupted create left a partial file, the owner must remove it; for an edit, the owner must restore the previous file hash. The authenticated API sends that decision to a filesystem worker, which rechecks the exact pre-write state and records the original tool call as `failed` with `not_applied_after_review`. It never retries or overwrites the uncertain action; the owner explicitly resumes afterward, and any retry must be a new model proposal. The full targeted API/write-recovery/create suite passes (61 tests), the Playwright owner-confirmation flow passes, TypeScript passes, Ruff passes, and targeted Pyright reports zero errors. Full provider/model inference was not used. A fresh-process crash acceptance now kills the run owner immediately after exclusive file creation and before the execution event, verifies the empty destination remains blocked, removes that partial file after owner inspection, then resolves the intent through the rebuilt frozen engine worker (SHA-256 `9bf261432bf323f3ef2cff8c91186fd2c9161e5c9e5c980a10c40fe2957395c4`). The unresolved journal cleared, the original failure result remained available, the destination stayed absent, and the run stayed blocked pending explicit resume. Evidence: `.acceptance/929ca6cf-0a06-4fa4-8282-60a151cc892f/owner-reconciliation.json`. Hosted run [42546315792](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42546315792) passed both Windows jobs, including the full source suite and installer acceptance, but did not yet include a dedicated frozen owner-recovery scenario. The Phase 2 gate remains partial: automatic applied-effect reconciliation, explicit owner recovery, and selected process-death boundaries still do not establish reboot recovery or all external-effect semantics.

## Hosted frozen-worker acceptance for owner-reviewed workspace recovery (2026-11-08)

Extended the Windows installer workflow with a deterministic process-death acceptance for owner-reviewed workspace create recovery. It kills the run-owning process after exclusive file creation and before the execution event, confirms fresh-process startup recovery blocks the run, removes the inspected empty partial as the simulated owner decision, and resolves the original call using the packaged frozen filesystem worker. The report must show no unresolved call, an available original `not_applied_after_review` result, an absent destination, no automatic requeue, and a still-blocked run. Local acceptance passed using the frozen development engine (`.acceptance/a8daabfb-431e-4458-ae53-e72203d21978/report.json`; engine SHA-256 `9bf261432bf323f3ef2cff8c91186fd2c9161e5c9e5c980a10c40fe2957395c4`). Ruff, Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted Windows run [42547365385](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42547365385) passed both jobs, including the new frozen owner-recovery gate. Its uploaded `workspace-write-owner-recovery-42547365385` artifact reports `passed: true`, a blocked run, zero unresolved calls, an absent destination, and `not_applied_after_review`; the frozen engine SHA-256 is `a22f1404f00dafe980a01507575e095e86e3c04f32f02fc973db4a9d31fa3646`. The create path uses no model inference. A second frozen-worker case now kills an edit after fsync, restores the exact saved pre-write hash, and verifies owner-reviewed no-effect reconciliation; local report `.acceptance/6f7ec255-b5c6-4fe3-9364-2ae259f18f2e/report.json` confirms the run stays blocked, the destination matches the original hash, and the original failure result is available. Hosted Windows run [42548304333](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42548304333) passed both jobs, including the frozen owner-reviewed create and edit recovery checks. The create artifact reports `destination_absent`; the edit artifact reports `original_hash_restored`; both report no unresolved intent, the original failure result available, and the run still blocked. The packaged engine SHA-256 is `da169820e6028537a000bb8e73671fb913d702a91dc5ead0be5852d82e52fc94`. No model inference was used.

## Owner-reviewed recovery for interrupted workspace deletion (2026-11-08)

The blocked-run recovery API and desktop panel now include interrupted `workspace.delete_file` calls. The owner must restore the file and its exact saved SHA-256; the filesystem worker rechecks workspace containment, regular-file status, size and hash before recording the original call as `not_applied_after_review`. Deletion calls remain marked internal in both failure and reconciliation events, and their result stays out of model-visible tool history. Unit/API tests verify the missing-file refusal, path/hash-only metadata, restored-file acceptance and internal-event visibility. The focused recovery/API/create suite passes 63 tests, the browser flow covers separate create/delete controls, TypeScript and production build pass, contracts are current, Ruff passes, and Pyright reports zero errors. A fresh-process frozen-worker crash acceptance kills the run after deletion but before result persistence, restores the saved bytes, and resolves without re-deleting (`.acceptance/29168a16-b00d-4fec-ba42-df79f0b1e5ef/report.json`; engine SHA-256 `ef9d85ace780ed4456cda919ee823c23f52950436b071f67225ff149402be61a`). Hosted run [42550134883](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42550134883) passed both Windows jobs, including this frozen deletion acceptance. Its `workspace-delete-owner-recovery-42550134883` artifact reports the original hash restored, `not_applied_after_review`, no unresolved intent, and the run still blocked; the engine SHA-256 is `752f6012f9e3ddbdf3481ad7919d2823fb2da792853a82a908a5674bfd2d765b`. No model inference was used.

## Makefile test suggestions (2026-11-08)

Final project review now recognizes explicit GNU Make test targets in `GNUmakefile`, `Makefile`, and `makefile`. It selects GNU Make's normal filename precedence, supports nested projects and both recipe-block and inline recipes, and does not suggest execution for comment-only declarations, empty targets, malformed files, or names that only contain `test`. The path-labeled command runs in the same restricted, network-disabled project snapshot and requires the existing command grant; successful output still requires user inspection and selection. Focused validation-suggestion tests pass (10 tests); Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42537730193](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42537730193) passed both source and installer jobs; the full Python suite reports 899 passed with one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning, and the installer acceptance passed. No model inference was used.
## Require explicit pytest configuration for Python validation (2026-11-08)

Python validation suggestions now require pytest-recognized configuration: `pytest.ini`/`.pytest.ini`, `pytest.toml`/`.pytest.toml`, `[tool.pytest]` or `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in `pyproject.toml`, `[pytest]` in `tox.ini`, or `[tool:pytest]` in `setup.cfg`. Generic project metadata and malformed config no longer imply pytest, and multiple pytest configs in one project produce only one suggestion, using pytest's documented precedence. The current project matrix and unit tests verify each supported form, nested paths, ignored generic files, malformed input and duplicate suppression. Focused suite passes (11 tests), Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. The behavior follows the official [pytest configuration rules](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/reference/customize.html). Hosted Windows run [42538808702](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42538808702) passed both source and installer jobs, including 900 Python tests (one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning), full static/browser checks and installer acceptance. No model inference was used.

## Opt-in sign-in startup for recovery (2026-11-08)

Desktop settings now let the owner register X.A.A. to start when they sign in. The current-user registry entry carries a background flag so the tray and engine start without opening the main window; manual launches remain visible, and the default remains disabled. The native boot smoke enables and disables the setting in a unique disposable registry value, verifies both states, and cleans it up after failure. The first smoke report, `.acceptance/e22767e8-4052-4616-96a0-92e5f70f192b/report.json`, recorded a Playwright checkbox timeout before diagnostic state was captured; the harness now waits for the saved setting and includes UI status in failures. Local rerun `.acceptance/de74a259-4efd-44ca-8827-8964d8561bc8/report.json` passed the toggle, setting restoration, hidden-window, authenticated-engine and shutdown checks. Hosted run [42541245473](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42541245473) passed the build and general install path but did not yet verify cleanup of a registered startup entry. Follow-up [42542169124](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42542169124) passed both Windows jobs; `.acceptance/installer-startup-42542169124/build/installer/install-smoke.json` confirms the startup entry survived upgrade and was removed on uninstall, while backup/restore and shutdown checks passed. The installed smoke requested no model inference. Rust unit tests (8), formatting, TypeScript typechecking and the production frontend build also pass. This does not establish whole-machine reboot, power-loss, or task-level recovery.
## Discord dependency notices (2026-11-08)

Added the exact installed license files for the nine Discord adapter runtime distributions to the provisional notice draft, grouped by SHA-256, and included both package `NOTICE` files plus aiohttp's vendored llhttp MIT license. The package versions and metadata paths are recorded in the draft, and `docs/releases/dependency-inventory.md` now distinguishes this completed text collection from the remaining package-wide copyright review. Hosted Windows run [42524315530](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42524315530) rebuilt and installed the updated draft and passed the manifest hash check for all three bundled release documents. This closes the known Discord dependency text omission in the draft. It does not resolve the X.A.A. application license, audit every platform-conditional Python lock entry, or constitute legal approval. The license text collection used local installed distribution metadata and did not invoke model inference.

The phases have very different scope, so counting these rows equally would produce a misleading percentage. The prior 16% was not recomputed from this gate view and is not presented as a current measurement.

## Ruby Rake project validation suggestions (2026-11-08)

Final project review now detects captured `Rakefile` test tasks declared as `task :test`, `task "test"`, or `Rake::TestTask`. For a project with a `Gemfile`, a matching `Gemfile.lock` is required and the suggestion runs through `bundle exec`; projects without Bundler use the detected Rakefile directly. Comment-only declarations, Rakefiles without a test task, and unlocked Bundler projects produce no suggestion. Commands remain path-labeled, run in the existing restricted network-disabled project copy, and successful evidence still needs user inspection. The focused validation-suggestion suite passes (9 tests); Ruff, format, strict engine/scripts Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42514078530](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42514078530) passed both Windows jobs, including the full source suite and installer acceptance. Command options follow the official [Rake CLI](https://ruby.github.io/rake/doc/command_line_usage_rdoc.html) and [Bundler guide](https://bundler.io/guides/rationale.html).

## Controlled managed-browser redirect enforcement (2026-11-08)

The actual managed Microsoft Edge worker follows a Postman Echo redirect from `postman-echo.com` to the separately approved `httpbin.org/html` fixture and returns the expected Moby-Dick marker. With only `postman-echo.com` approved, the same redirect service points to `www.iana.org`; the worker returns `read_failed`, no final URL, and persists the result as completed. Evidence: `.acceptance/browser-redirect-edge-cc525856cc174f8cb70b5ad780a90378/report.json`. The focused browser unit suite passes 29 tests, Ruff and formatting pass for the smoke, and strict Pyright passes. The Windows source workflow now repeats both cases and uploads the report; hosted acceptance is pending. The fixture endpoint is Postman Echo's documented testing service ([Postman documentation](https://learning.postman.com/docs/reference/developer-resources/echo-api)). Live subresource-chain, internal proxy-bypass, and broader redirect-chain coverage remain open; no LLM inference is involved.
## Close unexpected managed-browser pages (2026-11-08)

The disposable browser context now closes pages opened beyond the primary page. Popup requests still pass through the context-wide GET/HEAD and approved-host guard and the DNS-pinning proxy, while only the primary page can supply returned text or screenshots. A focused async unit regression verifies the primary page stays open and an additional page is closed. Local Microsoft Edge 157 acceptance opens an `about:blank` popup through the actual browser event and confirms the popup closes while the primary page remains open (`.acceptance/browser-popup-edge-25c9de8fe27049ee81fc5d03b5ef68bf/report.json`). Hosted Windows run [42526498899](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42526498899) repeats the smoke on Microsoft Edge 156 and uploads only its report (`.acceptance/hosted-edge-popup-42526498899/report.json`). Public-site popup and redirect-chain acceptance remain open; no live LLM inference is involved. See [`managed-browser.md`](architecture/managed-browser.md).

## Honor explicit app-data deletion during uninstall (2026-11-08)

The pinned Tauri NSIS template's data checkbox removes bundle-ID directories, while X.A.A.'s main database and artifacts are stored in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XAA`. The release installer now uses a `POSTUNINSTALL` hook that removes only that exact app-owned directory when the user checked Tauri's delete-data box and the operation is not an update ([pinned Tauri 2.12.4 uninstaller](https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/tauri-v2.12.4/crates/tauri-bundler/src/bundle/windows/nsis/installer.nsi#L823-L838)). The hosted smoke installs in a disposable GitHub runner, adds markers under the data and roaming-config directories, selects the actual NSIS checkbox through Win32, and requires both paths and app binaries to be gone. The existing install smoke continues to verify that silent/default uninstall preserves its data. Hosted run [42516865424](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42516865424) passed the source job and default-preserve installer smoke, but the new opt-in step failed because the uninstaller exited code 0 before the UI harness found its checkbox. The follow-up explicitly launches the normal window from the install directory and captures process/runtime diagnostics; opt-in removal remains unverified. PowerShell parsing, embedded Win32 helper compilation, config-hook resolution, and diff checks pass locally. Hosted run [42522700758](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42522700758) subsequently passed both Windows jobs, including the explicit removal and preserve-data installer flows. No model inference is used.

## Complete pinned review of platform-category exclusions (2026-11-08)

Reviewed the remaining three platform-category pins at their exact source commits: DeepInfra Sandbox, Discord TPS, and Render. DeepInfra is a billable terminal backend and its behavior is cross-referenced to the execution audit. Discord TPS only publishes the latest model name and token rate as public bot presence over an existing Discord connection; it is not a message adapter and does not use another bot token. Render is an untrusted hosted Streamable HTTP MCP server with a bearer API key plus a bundle of 20 Render skills; the package's verifier confirms all 127 skill files exactly match its recorded `render-oss/skills` commit after checking committed LF bytes. Inventory regeneration and `--check` pass; Ruff passes for the generator. The inventory now has source evidence for all 49 catalog platform-category candidates (39 messaging/gateway declarations, nine other candidates, and Home Assistant's gateway/toolset). No plugin was installed or imported, and no live service was contacted for this source audit. This closes only the category's pinned static review; catalog discovery beyond this category, external runtime validation, and X.A.A. channel/backend implementation remain open.

## Live model generated dependency dispatch (2026-11-08)

The updated `scripts/live_plan_dispatch_diagnostic.py` passed with local `google/gemma-4-e4b` at `.acceptance/58720685-6f7e-4eca-9a37-20247bb1e559/report.json`. A real model generated the inspect→review dependency graph; the researcher read one random fixture token and cited its durable journal evidence; the harness accepted that prerequisite only after comparing its candidate with the saved exact read; then the dependent reviewer independently reread the same file and produced a matching candidate. Both tasks reached their expected states, no tool calls remained unresolved, and all 12,457 tokens were measured. The dependent review correctly remains pending owner decision. The harness now matches candidate evidence to journal IDs and permits schema-rejected `plan_save` retries when the final persisted graph is exact. Earlier `.acceptance/fc5986e4-d6cc-48e5-85e0-25062e13ecf0/` and `.acceptance/b3d4b412-23e9-46c4-8429-d63ef8070a2f/` reports preserve the two harness false negatives that exposed these assumptions. This is one successful model/configuration sample, not proof of broad model reliability or the full Phase 4 gate. Hosted run [42505871787](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42505871787) passed both Windows jobs on the prior committed source; the harness follow-up still needs hosted validation. A subsequent native WebView attempt with Qwen3.5 9B failed in the harmless tool preflight before any plan or task was created (`.acceptance/ec2347b0-7ff1-4ea3-a30a-f46d3d3175f9/report.json`). The smoke now accepts `XAA_NATIVE_PLAN_MODEL` to select another already discovered model for future native acceptance. The next Gemma native run was stopped at the user’s request to defer model testing; it produced no acceptance claim.

## Specialist browser screenshot delegation (2026-11-08)

Specialists may request a browser screenshot when their saved task scope includes `web.browser.read` and the parent run approved the browser and exact hostname. The screenshot remains an untrusted run artifact; its metadata is included in the task tool result. Lost-worker recovery attaches the durable browser result and artifact without repeating navigation. Focused specialist and task-recovery tests pass (4 cases); Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. Hosted Windows run [42498137879](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42498137879) passed both jobs, including full Python validation and installer acceptance.

## Discord private-DM ingress (2026-11-08)

A text-only Discord gateway slice now verifies bot tokens through the official API, stores the secret in Windows Credential Manager, and listens with only the unprivileged DM message intent. Pairing codes expire after five minutes and can be consumed once; paired users are scoped to the bot account. Guild messages and unpaired DMs cannot create runs. Accepted DM text is durably deduplicated by Discord message ID and creates an Observe-mode run without workspace or tool grants. Added an authenticated API and desktop settings for token management, pairing, status and revocation. Pairing codes are scoped to their Discord bot account, and malformed snowflake event IDs are rejected. Five store tests and two focused API tests, the browser channel workflow, Ruff, strict Pyright and TypeScript checks pass locally. Hosted Windows run [42502461091](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42502461091) passed both jobs after the account-scoped pairing and malformed-ID checks, including the full Python suite and frozen installer acceptance. Outbound run replies, ambiguous-send reconciliation, media/voice, guild support and live Discord acceptance remain open. See [discord.md](channels/discord.md).

## Signed generic webhook ingress (2026-11-08)

Messaging channels can create, rotate and disable a generic webhook HMAC secret; the key is saved in Windows Credential Manager and revealed only on creation/rotation. `POST /webhooks/inbound` verifies a five-minute timestamp, signature over exact timestamp/event-ID/body bytes, strict text payload schema and a 64 KiB streamed body limit. Durable run idempotency returns the same Observe-mode run for an identical event and rejects reuse with changed content. Inbound text receives no workspace, command, browser, search or MCP grants. API tests (31), the channel settings browser test, Ruff, formatting, Pyright, TypeScript, and generated API contract checks pass locally. Hosted Windows run [42499585163](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42499585163) passed both source and installer jobs. Provider-specific live delivery and remote deployment remain unverified.

## Latest hosted verification (2026-11-08)

Run [42504107642](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42504107642) passed both Windows jobs. Source validation passed the pinned Hermes audits, Ruff, formatting, Pyright, API contracts, desktop client checks, the full Python suite, and specialist/Telegram recovery diagnostics. The installer built and passed native desktop/API restart recovery, preference and sourced-knowledge UI, specialist recovery, frozen ACP/snapshot/worker checks, private task command scopes, the 0.1.0 predecessor install, 0.1.0→0.1.1 binary hash verification, backup preservation, boot, and uninstall. The run validates Discord's durable private-DM reply outbox code alongside the existing application suite; it does not perform live Discord interoperability. Runs [42498137879](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42498137879), [42499585163](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42499585163), and [42496696548](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42496696548) provide the preceding specialist browser, signed webhook, and specialist MCP acceptance evidence.

## Bundled release notices verification (2026-11-08)

The release Tauri overlay bundles `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, the dependency inventory, and release notes. The installer manifest records each staged file's size and SHA-256; the installed-package smoke checks all three files against those hashes. Hosted run [42478955033](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42478955033) passed the installer smoke. The notices remain provisional: the application license has not been selected, six Windows-target crate archives lack embedded license files, and broader attribution/copyright review remains open. `docs/releases/windows-installer.md` now reflects the verified bundle behavior.

## MCP ambiguous write reconciliation (2026-11-08)

The active-run view now offers an explicit check for MCP write calls whose effects are unknown, without exposing their arguments or response content. After checking the external system, the owner can record whether the effect happened; the decision is authenticated, transactionally audited, and changes the saved tool result for any later continuation without replaying the original request. Focused API coverage verifies authentication, mandatory review acknowledgement, privacy of stored arguments, idempotent refusal of stale decisions, and the durable audit event. The focused API test, desktop typecheck, API contract check and browser acceptance pass. Hosted run [42481282487](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42481282487) passed both Windows jobs, including source validation, the full Python suite and installed-package upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. This is one external-effect path, not complete general reconciliation.

## Block execution after an ambiguous MCP write (2026-11-08)

The coordinator now stops immediately after an MCP write reports an unknown outcome, before it asks the model for another turn. Remaining tool calls in that same model response are durably recorded as not started, preserving a complete replayable tool sequence. The run becomes blocked until the owner inspects and reconciles the MCP effect, then resumes explicitly. A coordinator regression verifies that a batched follow-up write never reaches the MCP manager and both tool results replay in order. The coordinator and MCP stdio suites pass (16 tests), with Pyright, Ruff and formatting clean. Hosted run [42482250360](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42482250360) passed both Windows jobs, including the full Python suite, process-kill recovery diagnostics, installer, native recovery and upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance.

## Telegram ambiguous reply reconciliation (2026-11-08)

The desktop now lists ordinary Telegram replies whose send outcome is unknown, scoped to the currently configured bot account. The owner can inspect the private chat and explicitly mark a reply delivered or return it to the outbox for retry; the UI warns that an incorrect retry may duplicate a message. The authenticated API requires a destination-checked confirmation, only transitions records still in `unknown`, and stores each resolution in a durable audit table. Focused Telegram API/store tests (21), desktop typecheck, generated contract check, client tests (15), and the channel browser workflow pass locally. Hosted run [42476706237](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42476706237) passed both Windows jobs, including full source checks and installed-package recovery/upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. Live Telegram interoperability remains unverified.

## Email ambiguous reply reconciliation (2026-11-08)

The configured mailbox now lists SMTP replies whose result is unknown, scoped to its saved address. The owner can inspect the Sent folder and destination, then explicitly mark delivered or return the existing reply to the outbox. The authenticated API requires a destination-checked confirmation, only transitions a still-unknown UID/UIDVALIDITY record, and stores each resolution in a durable audit table. Seven Email API/store tests pass, including both delivered and retry resolutions; desktop typecheck, generated contract check, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. The channel browser flow now exercises manual SMTP retry. Hosted run [42477643940](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42477643940) passed both Windows jobs. Live IMAP/SMTP interoperability remains unverified.

## Scheduled Telegram ambiguous delivery reconciliation (2026-11-08)

Automations now shows the selected Telegram user for each schedule and provides owner-reviewed resolution for scheduled replies left `unknown`. Resolution is scoped to the configured bot account and exact schedule occurrence, audited durably, and rejects retries after three attempts or after the destination link is revoked. Old unresolved occurrences remain visible even when they fall outside the normal recent-history window. The schedule API, storage/API tests, automations browser flow, contract generation, TypeScript, Ruff and Pyright pass locally. Hosted run [42478955033](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42478955033) passed both Windows jobs; live scheduled delivery and real clock/restart acceptance remain open.

Run [42499585163](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42499585163) passed both Windows jobs, including source validation, the full Python suite, signed webhook acceptance, installer packaging, native recovery checks and installed-package upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. ## CI-enforced pinned Hermes capability inventories (2026-11-08)

The channel and execution inventory generators previously required a manual local invocation. Source CI now fetches the pinned registry, gateway, environment, and catalog paths and runs both generators in `--check` mode. A fresh sparse clone containing exactly those paths passed locally, and hosted run [42475010536](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42475010536) passed both inventory checks. The audit recorded 27 platform manifests, 28 registered names, three core transports, six built-in execution families, eight external declarations, and 25 reviewed candidates. The checkout retains the pinned commit with no local modifications. The wider Hermes source and runtime parity audit remains incomplete.

## Node 24 GitHub Actions runtime follow-up (2026-11-08)

Hosted run 42475010536 emitted the runner's deprecation notice for `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, and `actions/cache@v4`. The workflow now uses the latest major releases verified through their official release metadata (`checkout@v7.0.1`, `setup-node@v7.1.0`, `cache@v6.1.0`); each release's `action.yml` declares the Node 24 runtime. The hosted runner supports these releases. This workflow-only follow-up awaits CI.

## Manifest-based integrated-project check suggestions (2026-11-08)

The final review panel now reads the current integrated project snapshot and suggests bounded check commands when it finds common Python, JavaScript, Rust, Go or .NET manifests. Each suggestion includes its source rationale and exact argument array, and only prefills the editable command field; the user reviews dependencies and explicitly starts the restricted check. Suggestions use the same snapshot hash as final-review evidence, so project changes invalidate prior evidence. Four unit tests cover manifest selection, nested package/project paths, and multiple manifests in monorepos. Four final-review browser specs cover unique-check dispatch and ambiguous manual selection; the specialist-integration suite passes 16 tests. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, generated API contracts, TypeScript and `git diff --check` pass. The source job passed for commit `1c30594`, while installer packaging hit the external NSIS helper HTTP 504 in [run 42465361608](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42465361608). Run [42466145134](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42466145134) found one strict-Pyright issue in the API contract; the list default now has an explicit factory type and the full local engine/script type check passes. The correction is in commit `7aea918`; hosted run [42466313785](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42466313785) passed both source and installer acceptance jobs. Unique-check dispatch is implemented in `d32ff4f`, whose hosted run [42467296633](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42467296633) passed source checks and failed installer packaging on an HTTP 504 from Tauri’s NSIS download. The current uncommitted monorepo manifest-coverage and installer-download-retry follow-ups pass seven focused unit tests and all four final-review browser specs; they have not yet been included in hosted CI.

## Enumerate project manifests before automatic validation (2026-11-08)

The earlier detector selected only the first nested JavaScript, Rust or .NET manifest, which could make a monorepo look like it had one unambiguous check. Suggestions now enumerate every supported Python configuration file, `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, Go module and .NET solution (or project when no solution exists) in the bounded snapshot, with package paths in labels and argv. The existing automatic dispatcher therefore runs only when the full supported-manifest scan yields one command. Four focused unit tests pass, including ten distinct suggestions from a multi-language monorepo; all four final-review browser specs pass. Ruff, format, full engine/script Pyright, TypeScript, production build and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42467296633](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42467296633) passed source checks for prior commit `d32ff4f` and failed its installer step on an external NSIS HTTP 504; this manifest-coverage follow-up awaits hosted validation.

## Retry transient NSIS download failures during installer builds (2026-11-08)

The installer build now streams Tauri output and retries up to twice, with short backoff, only when the NSIS download reports HTTP 502, 503 or 504. Configuration and other build failures remain immediate errors, and the final exception retains captured output. This responds to repeated hosted failures while fetching Tauri’s `nsis-3.13.zip`; run [42467296633](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42467296633) passed source tests but its installer job failed on HTTP 504. Two focused tests cover transient retry, non-network failure, and the three-attempt bound. The retry change is local and awaits hosted validation.

## Automatically dispatch one unambiguous integrated-project check (2026-11-08)

When final review requires project validation, has exactly one manifest-based suggestion, and has neither prior validation evidence nor an unresolved command intent, the review panel now runs that command automatically under the run’s existing restricted-command grant. It still runs for at most 15 seconds against the isolated project copy without network access; command changes are discarded. The exact argv and saved output remain visible, and the successful result stays unselected until the user inspects it and confirms it for final acceptance. Multiple or missing suggestions remain manual; failed or ambiguous executions are not automatically retried. Four final-review Playwright scenarios pass, including unique-check dispatch and ambiguous-selection behavior; TypeScript and the production desktop build pass. Hosted run [42467296633](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42467296633) passed the Windows source job for `d32ff4f`; installer packaging failed on HTTP 504 downloading the Tauri NSIS 3.13 archive.

## Live independent specialist review (2026-11-08)

The real-model review diagnostic passed on the already-loaded `qwen/qwen3.5-9b`: researcher and reviewer tasks independently read a generated random marker, the researcher candidate matched the file evidence, and the reviewer reported the exact matching value. The reviewer reached `verifying`; the final reviewer decision remains pending user review. The harness accepted the researcher prerequisite based on its independent token/evidence check; this is not autonomous user approval. Usage recorded 5,998 prompt and 1,056 completion tokens with zero unmeasured calls. Evidence: `.acceptance/381110d5-8aeb-4f87-b01a-b5034450296d/report.json`. This closes one real-model reviewer evidence-read case, not broad reviewer reliability or final human acceptance.

## Reserved specialist throughput (2026-11-08)

Runs with exact pre-inference token reservations can now execute two independent specialist assignments concurrently. Each model request remains serialized from the shared-budget check through prompt/output reservation, inference, and durable usage recording; non-inference work such as workspace reads and private edits overlaps between specialists. Reported-token runs without exact reservations retain their conservative serial policy. The regression uses delayed workspace operations to prove overlap in both unlimited and reservation-backed modes while confirming hard-budget reported-only work stays serial. Focused specialist, token-reservation and specialist-quota suites pass (27 tests); Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Hosted run [42455417901](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42455417901) passed both Windows source and installer jobs, including the full engine suite and installed-package recovery, upgrade, boot and uninstall checks. This increases useful specialist throughput while preserving the local provider's single-inference behavior. Broad model reliability, external-client accounting and Phase 4 parity remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## WSL specialist command setup and Phase 4 repeat (2026-11-08)

Live Phase 4 repeat attempts showed a repeatable setup cost in `WSLTransport.discover`: it launched one WSL process for each of five resource paths, each capped at 10 seconds. Discovery now maps all five paths inside one bounded WSL launch (up to 30 seconds), then rechecks the resource fingerprint before returning the transport. Twelve focused WSL transport tests pass; Ruff, formatting, and strict Pyright pass. Hosted run [42453419338](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42453419338) passed both Windows jobs, including the full Python suite and installer recovery/upgrade acceptance.

With a fresh 16,384-context `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` instance and conservative context admission, the live run at `.acceptance/69f071a7-cf26-4a79-af90-4b9703d6ef1b/report.json` completed both real-model specialists, private edits, Ubuntu-24.04 restricted validation, conflict refusal without a choice, hash-backed selection of beta, and final integration. The original file remained unchanged before review; both tasks completed, there were no unresolved calls, and the owned model instance was unloaded. This is a second end-to-end live Phase 4 sample, using conservative context admission because an independent exact SDK count was unavailable.

Recent failed samples are retained: `.acceptance/1daa400f-58ac-4ca5-bed5-2144bd78ae56/report.json` had a 512-token cap that ended one specialist response without a structured call; `.acceptance/4cf88d55-fdf6-470e-8eb1-924b01219dad/report.json` completed one specialist but timed out the other's WSL command setup before a command journal; `.acceptance/b3050e75-7275-49f8-b4f5-d30e38656f60/report.json` stopped in the parent tool diagnostic because LM Studio's exact SDK counter could not count the prompt. None changed the original fixture or left an unresolved call. The optimization and new success improve the repeat evidence but do not establish broad model or orchestration reliability; Phase 4 remains partial and overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Desktop and terminal shared conversations (2026-11-08)

The desktop can now explicitly load terminal or desktop chat sessions from the shared SQLite store, open a session bound to its original model/workspace/mode, continue it through the normal run coordinator, and delete a session for both interfaces after confirmation. It adds up to 5 KiB of bounded, untrusted prior text to the run and saves a public answer after it reaches review or completion. Shared turns are text-only and capped at 2,000 UTF-8 bytes; the session picker lists the latest 50 sessions. Focused API/storage and desktop browser coverage passed, including the shared deletion flow, along with TypeScript, client tests, generated-contract checks, Ruff, Pyright and the production desktop build. Hosted run [42451236280](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42451236280) passed both Windows jobs, including the full Python suite and installed-package acceptance This proves deterministic session exchange, not another live-model or installed-desktop session workflow. Session editing, attachments, cross-profile sync and broader conversation parity remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Specialist inference timeout classification (2026-11-08)

Specialist model-call `TimeoutError`s are now persisted with the bounded stage `chat` or `chat_streamed` and a stable error class; raw exception text is not saved. This improves diagnosis without automatically replaying an inference whose usage may be unknown. The focused specialist suite passes (11 tests), Ruff and formatting pass, and strict Pyright passes. A new real Qwen3.5 9B attempt stopped earlier in the harmless parent tool-use diagnostic because the model did not return the expected tool result; both specialists remained queued, there were no unresolved calls or workspace changes, and the owned instance was unloaded. It does not validate the new specialist timeout classification. Evidence: `.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-timeout-classification-20261108/report.json`. Hosted run [42449682521](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42449682521) passed both Windows source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite and installed-package acceptance

## Atomic mixed linked-resource procedure edit (2026-11-08)

The desktop now lets the user independently grant linked-resource creation/replacement and removal for the same selected latest procedure. With both grants, the model receives one `procedure_resource_revise` tool that can perform up to eight total linked-resource operations in one transaction, including at most one removal; without the removal grant, `remove_paths` is omitted from the schema and coordinator execution rejects it. Main-text revision remains mutually exclusive with resource editing. Storage verifies both durable grants, the selected version, run lease, every removal target and all paths/content before inserting one inactive candidate; missing removal targets roll back the whole edit.

The focused procedure/drafting suites pass (30 tests), the procedure consent browser test passes, and TypeScript, generated API contracts, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Live Qwen3.5 9B coordinator evidence called the tool once to replace `references/guide.md` and remove `references/obsolete.md`, preserved version 1, reached `verifying`, left the candidate inactive, and left no unresolved calls or workspace files: `docs/releases/evidence/live-procedure-resource-mixed.json`. The first attempt was blocked before any tool call because LM Studio used its smaller default context; the repeatable diagnostic now explicitly loads a 16K context. Multi-call batching and broader manager semantics remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired). Hosted run [42448509574](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42448509574) passed both Windows source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite and install/boot/uninstall acceptance.

## Atomic multi-resource procedure edits (2026-11-08)

Expanded the existing explicit resource-edit permission to allow one atomic model call to create or replace up to eight distinct linked UTF-8 files in one inactive candidate version. Each file remains limited to 64 KiB; the package still enforces its 32-file and 1 MiB total limits. Duplicate paths, invalid relative paths and over-limit batches are rejected before storage. The focused procedure suite passes (21 tests), including a three-file single-version write; the browser consent flow, production desktop build, generated contract check, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Live Qwen3.5 9B acceptance called the revision tool once to replace one reference and create two more, verified exact bytes and unchanged version 1, reached `verifying`, and left no unresolved calls: `.acceptance/live-procedure-resource-batch-31dbc25d7db24a12995f9dbffac3249b/report.json`. Hosted source and installer jobs passed in [run 42445001886](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42445001886). Mixed create/remove batches, multi-call batching and broader manager semantics remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Separately authorized linked procedure resource removal (2026-11-08)

Added a third, mutually exclusive per-run grant for removing exactly one normalized linked resource from the selected latest procedure. The model receives `procedure_resource_remove` only under that grant. Storage validates the selected identity, live lease, version and target path, then writes a new unevaluated candidate version and its completed tool result/audit events atomically; the old version, active pointer, body, permissions, metadata and every other asset remain intact. The native UI consumes the removal grant at submission. Twenty-one focused tests pass, including coordinator tool execution, API persistence, grant exclusivity, missing-resource refusal, path traversal rejection, multi-file atomic revision and preservation of the previous package. Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Live Qwen3.5 9B acceptance called the tool once, reached `verifying`, removed the selected linked file in an inactive candidate, preserved the previous version, and left no unresolved calls: `.acceptance/live-procedure-resource-removal-cc4894947aaa4b5eac4c5635b0773785/report.json`. Mixed create/remove batches, multi-call batching and broader Hermes manager semantics remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Model-authored content-only procedure revisions (2026-11-08)

Added a separate per-run local-user grant to let the model draft a new version of the exact latest procedure selected for the run. The tool can change only procedure text; it inherits the saved title, dependencies, permissions, metadata and linked resources, leaves any active pointer unchanged, and creates an unevaluated candidate version. Run creation binds the grant to the selected ID/version, the UI disables it for historical versions and consumes it on submission, and storage rechecks the grant, lease and latest-version condition inside the transaction that saves the version, tool result and audit events. Eight focused revision tests plus seven draft tests pass (15 total); both the procedure-library and objective browser workflows pass, including grant consumption after an ambiguous 503. Contracts, TypeScript, desktop client tests (14), production desktop build, Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. A live LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` coordinator run called `procedure_view` then `procedure_revise`, created version 2 with the requested path-citation instruction, retained version 1 and unchanged permissions, left the candidate inactive/unevaluated, and created no workspace changes or unresolved calls. Evidence: `.acceptance/live-procedure-revision-b44ba3f63dcd43bab2de3c4739f78f3e/report.json`. Patch shapes, package writes, model deletion/batching, wider Hermes skill semantics and the release gates remain open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

Fast follow-up: `procedure_revise` now also supports exact old/new replacement in the main procedure body; ambiguous repeated matches fail unless the model explicitly requests `replace_all`. Linked package files and all metadata remain outside this grant. Ten revision tests and seven drafting tests pass (17 total); Ruff, strict Pyright, generated contracts, TypeScript and desktop production build pass. A live Qwen3.5 9B run created the exact targeted version. The model then made one duplicate stale retry, which the store rejected with `effect=not_started`; only one version was created, the original stayed unchanged and there were no unresolved calls. The harness marked its overall attempt false because it required exactly one call; the independent audit confirms the targeted-write and stale-retry behavior in `.acceptance/live-procedure-revision-f668547c889647918faef9bf3ead13da/targeted-patch-audit.json`. Hosted run [42440225518](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42440225518) passed both Windows source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite, native acceptance, install, boot and uninstall. Broader lifecycle, skill-package manager operations, and release gaps remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Phase 4: dispatch newly unblocked dependencies after review (2026-11-08)

Plan execution now records a durable `plan.execution_authorized` event when started through the coordinator tool or authenticated local-user dispatch. After a user explicitly accepts a prerequisite candidate, the API checks that saved authorization plus fresh graph readiness under a new run lease and automatically dispatches newly ready dependent tasks. Candidates still require the user to inspect their evidence and confirm every criterion; rejection never unlocks dependents. The UI tells reviewers that accepting a prerequisite may start its newly unlocked tasks. Seven task-review cases and two graph cases pass; the three browser review/conflict checks, Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript and desktop build pass. This removes repeated manual dispatch between reviewed dependency layers. Live-model review remains scripted, and the wider Phase 4 reliability/resource gates remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Separately authorized linked procedure resource revisions (2026-11-08)

Added the per-run `allow_procedure_resource_revision` grant and a `procedure_resource_revise` coordinator tool. It creates or replaces up to eight distinct normalized relative UTF-8 linked resources atomically in one new version of the exact selected latest procedure. The resource grant is separate and mutually exclusive with main-body revision; other assets, body text, dependencies, permissions, metadata and active pointer carry forward unchanged. New versions stay inactive and unevaluated; scripts remain inert. Storage validates grant, lease, selected version, path, content/package limits and tool call identity within one transaction. The focused procedure revision/drafting suites pass (28 cases), including authorization, package preservation, path traversal, API grant persistence and coordinator tool exposure/execution. The browser revision workflow passes latest-version, mutually exclusive grant and submission-consumption checks; generated OpenAPI/TypeScript, desktop build, Ruff and strict Pyright pass. Mixed create/remove batches, multi-call batching, broader Hermes package semantics and release gates remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

Live Qwen3.5 9B acceptance called the resource tool once, replaced `references/guide.md` with the requested text, preserved the procedure body and version 1 asset bytes, and created one inactive candidate with no unresolved tool calls or workspace effects. Evidence: `.acceptance/live-procedure-resource-revision-3254264d548b45ecbb20593524c2b094/report.json`. Evidence: `.acceptance/live-procedure-resource-batch-31dbc25d7db24a12995f9dbffac3249b/report.json` confirms the model used one call to replace one linked file and create two more in the same inactive candidate, with exact expected bytes and no unresolved calls.

## Explicitly authorized model procedure drafts (2026-11-08)

Added a per-run local-user permission to let the model create up to three reusable procedure candidates. The new `procedure_draft` tool validates through the existing bounded version schema and stores the candidate, source run/call provenance, completed tool result and audit events in one SQLite transaction. Candidates stay inactive and unevaluated; the tool cannot revise, evaluate, activate, select or execute them. The desktop checkbox defaults off, is sent with run creation, and the grant is consumed as soon as submission starts, including when the request fails ambiguously; the library refreshes when a candidate is saved. Focused storage/API/coordinator coverage passes (7 tests), including grant denial and rollback; the objective browser workflow checks grant consumption after an ambiguous 503. A live LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` coordinator run called `procedure_draft` exactly once, saved one inactive candidate with empty evaluations, reached `verifying`, and left no unresolved calls; evidence is in `.acceptance/live-procedure-draft-d43bf43663fe4b3c98ece59a6c39fcbf/report.json`. The full Python suite passes (779 passed, one skipped, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, generated contracts, TypeScript, desktop client tests and production desktop build pass. Hosted Windows source and installer jobs both pass in [run 42437914408](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42437914408). This is a create-only drafting slice; model edits/batches, multiple library scopes, lifecycle review and package readiness remain open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Scanned PDF OCR and usage accounting (2026-11-08)

Added bounded local rendering and vision-model OCR for scanned PDF pages, with per-page and aggregate size limits, explicit vision-capability checks, and original/OCR/image artifacts retained with integrity metadata. OCR calls use LM Studio's local chat endpoint, do not persist provider-side conversations, and their measured tokens are now included in run usage. A configured run token budget below the per-page minimum is rejected before OCR inference. Native Windows acceptance passed PNG, text PDF, scanned PDF, DOCX and XLSX cases on Gemma 4 e4b; the scanned-page OCR used 520 tokens and its run totaled 1,871 tokens with no unmeasured calls or outstanding reservations. This is one model and fixture, not a general OCR quality guarantee. Focused local tests pass (63 cases); hosted Windows run [42435619239](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42435619239) passed both source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite (773 passed, one warning), desktop specialist-conflict acceptance, installer build and installed-package recovery/install checks. Overall goal completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired); remaining Phase 4 repeatability and wider release gates are still open.

## Live Phase 4 specialist conflict and WSL validation (2026-11-07)

Restored local LM Studio availability for an authorized integration run by starting its API on loopback only; model inventory exposed the already-installed `qwen/qwen3.5-9b`. The live acceptance selected and verified its exact 16,384-token instance, used LM Studio's exact SDK prompt counter, and ran two independently assigned real-model specialists in separate private workspace snapshots. Each read the fixture, edited its own module, and ran the provided validator in Ubuntu-24.04 through the restricted WSL/Bubblewrap command worker. The engine rejected integration without a conflict choice; after a fresh hash-backed preview, the automated fixture reviewer explicitly chose the proposed beta version. Final file validation passed; both tasks completed; the parent history and original source stayed unchanged; no unresolved calls remained. Report: `.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-wsl-exact-1024-20261107/report.json`.

The acceptance harness now exposes `--max-output-tokens`; the proven run used 1,024 because the 512-token run was cut off at the generation limit after successful validation. Exact SDK counting also avoids a conservative UTF-8-byte estimate blocking a later request. Separate 4,096- and 8,192-context attempts correctly blocked before effects; a 16,384 conservative WSL attempt let one specialist reach review but blocked the other on prompt estimate, and a 16,384 exact run with 512 output tokens rejected a truncated final answer. Those attempts remain in `.acceptance/` as diagnostic evidence. The passing run used synthetic data and scripted plan/review decisions; it does not establish autonomous review, broad model reliability, or approval of real user work. The harness unloaded only its owned model instance, inventory confirmed zero loaded instances, and the loopback LM Studio server was stopped afterward. Phase 4 now has one full live integration acceptance for private edits, command validation, and explicit conflict resolution; repeat reliability and broader orchestration/resource gates remain open. Overall goal completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Review-gated batch training export (2026-11-07)

Added `xaa export-training BATCH_DIR --output FILE.jsonl`, a local/offline export for explicitly accepted batch candidates. Export verifies the saved trajectory's SHA-256 and size, completed run identity/state, and passing deterministic checks; it takes the prompt from the public trajectory and the target from its final assistant message. Tool-proposing trajectories are skipped because flattened text would discard tool semantics. Writes are capped at 10 MiB, atomic, and refuse overwrite. CLI and docs state that this is text-only dataset preparation, not provider fine-tuning/upload. Added fixture tests for reviewed content provenance, integrity mismatch, overwrite refusal, and operation without an API token. Local verification: **759 Python tests passed, 1 skipped** in an isolated temp directory; Ruff passed across `engine` and `tests/unit/test_batch.py`; changed files pass formatting; strict Pyright reports zero errors. Training export is now a partial implemented slice; replay, comparative benchmarking, broader redaction audits, and provider-specific training integrations remain open. Overall project completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Resumable evaluation batch CLI (2026-11-07)

Added `xaa batch INPUT --output DIR --model MODEL` for bounded JSON evaluation sets (up to 32 cases). It submits through the authenticated run API, writes an atomic checkpoint after each accepted run, polls saved state, exports capped public trajectories with SHA-256 metadata, and resumes without duplicating an ambiguously acknowledged POST. Client request IDs bind a payload digest to one run in SQLite schema 20. Optional input `checks` apply deterministic `final_equals`, `final_contains`, `tool_called`, `tool_completed`, and `state_equals` assertions to the redacted trajectory; checks stay out of the model prompt, and per-case plus aggregate counts are stored in the batch manifest. Fixture coverage includes run/export, resume, modified-input rejection, duplicate IDs, interruption after server acceptance, review-pending `verifying`, and mixed passing/failing assertions. The final local code passes 41 focused batch/API/memory/CLI tests, all-repository Ruff/format/Pyright, generated contracts and TypeScript. Hosted run [42416072264](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42416072264) passed both Windows jobs after the scorer changes: 757 Python tests passed, and the native installed-package acceptance passed.

A real local LM Studio `qwen3-0.6b` evaluation now records six attempts across exact text, arithmetic, and workspace-read cases: five yielded candidates, one blocked at the 256-token cap, one strict exact-output match, four semantically correct responses, and two successful workspace reads. A separate live scored batch passed all four configured checks (final contains, proposed tool, completed tool, and `verifying` state) against the real trajectory. All five candidates remain `verifying` for user review; the deterministic score does not approve them. Total reported use was 19,415 tokens and summed run durations were 30.006 seconds (not wall-clock throughput); LM Studio reported 609.82 MiB on load. Evidence, including the failed attempt, score manifest, and redacted trajectories, is `.acceptance/live-batch-eval-0fc5a2606f6342c184a5f5789a844ed4/evaluation-report.json` and adjacent files. This single-model sample does not establish comparative reliability or a default model. Replay and broader redaction audits remain open; reviewed text-only dataset export is implemented as a partial slice. Overall project progress remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Specialist recovery reservation accounting (2026-11-07)

The lost-worker task summary now counts only unanswered reservations belonging to that task. Reservations with a durable measured task response are released in the summary, while an unresolved reservation makes `safe_to_resume` false. Parent-engine recovery records a separate block reason; the task API normalizes that reason so restart-blocked tasks reach the recovery panel. Recovery readiness accounts for specialist iteration/token/time limits, shared run limits and outstanding reservations. Resumed specialists receive only their unspent time allowance. A new Windows diagnostic kills the authenticated API after a specialist's file-read result is durable, restarts it against the same database, inspects/resumes the task, then confirms the saved nonce is returned with exactly one read result and unchanged fixture bytes. It expires the dead run lease directly in the disposable database to avoid waiting 180 seconds; the report records that. Three source acceptances passed at `.acceptance/specialist-api-restart-c62ae51d-4825-44a2-9de1-88e860e99e3a/`, `.acceptance/specialist-api-restart-4c92c9f4-6ded-4587-b4fb-9c62e1e1676a/`, and `.acceptance/specialist-api-restart-f2400cd6-b0a7-412f-9628-114c790f73e3/`; 36 focused API/task-claim/parent-recovery tests, Ruff, formatting and Pyright pass. Windows source CI now runs this process-kill diagnostic. This deterministic fake-provider case does not establish live-model recovery, desktop/Tauri lifecycle restart, reboot recovery or reconciliation of arbitrary external effects; frozen specialist API restart is separately verified below. The wider Phase 4 and release gates remain open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Frozen specialist API restart (2026-11-07)

Added `scripts/frozen_specialist_restart_diagnostic.py` to exercise lost-specialist recovery in the actual frozen engine. It seeds a read-only specialist task, runs through authenticated dispatch and the harmless tool diagnostic, kills the packaged API after the file-read result is journaled, starts a new API against the same SQLite database, inspects recovery, resumes through the authenticated endpoint and verifies a nonce-bearing candidate with exactly one read result and unchanged fixture bytes. Two consecutive development-engine passes on SHA-256 `43db9a8c1c4f4de82443559cfeb92800627420e1b696c753bd837009aad2e748` are recorded at `.acceptance/frozen-specialist-restart-04a0086f-0556-4df6-ac73-82dcd3db4952/` and `.acceptance/frozen-specialist-restart-0858652a-6434-43b3-85d6-50e6b194adcf/`. Added a Playwright browser scenario for the desktop review panel with a conflicting private edit. It checks that integration stays disabled until the project preview loads, a user chooses either the current or proposed version, separately authorizes the choice, and reviews the criterion and file evidence. The accepted request must carry the preview's current-file hash and selected resolution. All three task-review browser tests pass locally. Runs [42388598452](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42388598452) and [42393555900](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42393555900) passed source and installer jobs, including install/boot/uninstall and upgrade acceptance. The saved reports show installed desktop and engine SHA-256 values matching the manifest entries. The single earlier checksum failure has not recurred across these runs; its cause remains unexplained and the instrumented check stays in place.

## Per-instance context limits and default admission fixture (2026-11-07)

Model discovery now keeps exact loaded-instance context limits separate from base model aliases. A base alias that could reach multiple instances is bounded by the smallest loaded context, exact IDs retain their own limits regardless of inventory order, and successful native inventory refresh removes stale unloaded-instance limits. Loading a second instance no longer overwrites the base alias before inventory refresh. Regression tests cover ordering, ambiguous aliases, stale entries, and conservative admission. The live Phase 4 fixture now supports both default conservative context admission and exact SDK counting. SDK inventory requests have previously timed out before RPC, and no SDK internals are monkeypatched. Probe evidence is `.acceptance/sdk-websocket-investigation-41f3aaf1529747148945d05f90973592/`. At the time of this earlier attempt, the local API refused connections; the live conflict and WSL gate has since passed with exact counting and a 1,024-token cap (see the newer live acceptance above). Overall progress remains not quantified (historical figure retired); repeat reliability and broader release gates remain open.

## Conflict diagnostic instance routing correction (2026-11-07)

The private-edit conflict diagnostic previously selected a loaded instance at the requested context but passed the base model ID to the run and specialists. The reported selected context therefore did not establish the actual inference route. The harness now verifies the selected ID and context against fresh native inventory, saves that ID as the run and specialist route, checks the saved task routes, and uses it for the parent diagnostic. A fixture verifies routing and rejects ambiguous instance inventory. A real LM Studio probe loaded two owned Qwen3 0.6B instances at 2,048 and 4,096 context; HTTP accepted and echoed the distinct `qwen3-0.6b:2` ID, but the SDK model inventory timed out, so SDK counting for that alias remains unverified. Both probe-owned instances were unloaded, and before/after inventories showed no loaded models. Evidence: `.acceptance/instance-routing-alias-probe-ba1dd54c25844f54ad11a167745789ca/report.json`. A subsequent run of the corrected conflict harness selected and saved `qwen3-0.6b` at 4,096 context for the parent and both tasks, but stopped in the parent tool diagnostic on `LMStudioWebsocketError` at `model_inventory`; no specialist was dispatched, there were zero unresolved calls, and its owned model was unloaded (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-routed-20261107/report.json`). The fixture lease now covers its full 600-second run budget rather than expiring before the 360-second specialist deadline. No conflict acceptance was performed by these attempts. The Phase 4 conflict gate remains open; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Transient workspace metadata retries (2026-11-07)

Read, directory-list and recursive-search preflight now also retry transient errors from the target metadata check, with at most three attempts and 50/100 ms backoff. Each attempt rechecks the parent and applicable specialist lease; retry events record a bounded stage and delay. Containment is checked first, and missing files, access denial and exhausted retries fail before any tool intent. The focused read/list/search and retry suites pass 36 tests; Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. These checks do not change write or reconciliation retry policies. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired); real-model conflict integration and release gates remain open.

## Transient canonical-path resolution retries (2026-11-07)

Workspace read, directory-list and recursive-search preflight now retry canonical path resolution up to three attempts for only the existing recognized transient filesystem errors, with 50/100 ms backoff. Each retry is lease-fenced and records only call ID, stage, attempt and delay; retries happen before the durable tool intent, and the eventual operation still records one intent. Permission denial, missing paths and paths that resolve outside the selected workspace remain immediate failures. Focused read/list/search/path-resolution tests pass (29); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. That change did not add retries to writes or unrelated filesystem calls; the subsequent metadata follow-up is recorded above. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired); Phase 4 real-model conflict integration and broader release gates remain open.

## Recoverable invalid paths in private specialist reads (2026-11-07)

The Qwen3 0.6B live run returned a rooted `/shared.py` path; the private snapshot correctly rejected it, but the worker error failed the task instead of allowing a corrected read. Isolated specialist reads now validate portable relative paths before dispatch and return a bounded `invalid_path` tool result so the model can retry without any filesystem effect. The live acceptance prompt now names the exact relative path. A regression drives `/evidence.txt` → tool error → `evidence.txt` → verified private read and reviewable candidate. In the latest live rerun, one specialist used the prompted relative `shared.py` path and received the expected file/hash evidence, then its next exact prompt count failed at LM Studio's model-inventory WebSocket stage. The other specialist exhausted its 512-token output allowance without a tool call. The updated report records `LMStudioWebsocketError`/`model_inventory` for the blocked task (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-qwen3-06-cac5aff-20261107/report.json`); the live invalid-path correction itself remains only deterministically verified. Coordinator tool-use diagnostics now also persist safe prompt-counter failure details, and the live harness includes up to eight bounded events in failure reports; a regression confirms the SDK error type, stage, and code are recorded without sending an uncounted inference. The combined focused suites pass 41 cases; Ruff, formatting, and strict Pyright pass. Hosted run [42353707320](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42353707320) was superseded and cancelled; the later [42354107200](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42354107200) passed both jobs. Phase 4 conflict integration remains open, overall completion not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Bounded retry for LM Studio's internal engine fetch failure (2026-11-07)

Live acceptance against Qwen3 0.6B with 16,384 context requested (the actual routed instance/context was not established) first stopped during the harmless tool-use diagnostic: LM Studio returned HTTP 400 with `Engine protocol predict request failed: fetch failed` (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-qwen3-06-20261107/report.json`). The OpenAI-compatible client now retries that exact internal fetch failure once with the existing short bounded delay; other 400 responses remain immediate errors. On rerun, the diagnostic passed and both specialists were dispatched, but the full conflict scenario still failed: one task returned the rooted `/shared.py` path and ended with `FilesystemWorkerError`/`PermissionError`; the other was blocked by an LM Studio tokenizer WebSocket timeout. There were zero unresolved calls and no conflict integration (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-qwen3-06-retry-20261107/report.json`). A later attempt stopped during parent exact token counting (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-qwen3-06-recovery-20261107/report.json`). The focused diagnostic suite passes all 12 cases, including retry and permanent-rejection boundaries; Ruff, formatting, and strict Pyright pass. Hosted run [42352788987](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42352788987) passed source and Windows installer jobs for the retry change. Phase 4 conflict integration is still unverified; overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Safer model reuse in the private-edit conflict acceptance harness (2026-11-07)

The Phase 4 private-edit conflict harness inspects LM Studio's native inventory, selects an already-loaded instance at the requested context, refuses to add a duplicate at a different context, and unloads only instances it loaded itself. Earlier runs still sent the base model ID to inference rather than the selected instance ID, so their reported context length is unproven. Task outcomes now persist bounded failure reasons and token-counter details. LM Studio still reports two Qwen3.5 9B instances at 16,384 context after the acceptance attempts. Two runs stopped before specialist dispatch at the harmless diagnostic with HTTP 400 (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-safe-20261107/`, `.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-safe-retry-20261107/`). A diagnostic against already-loaded Qwen3 0.6B also stopped before dispatch with the LM Studio internal fetch failure; see the entry above. Later Qwen3.5 9B attempts did dispatch specialists but did not pass the whole conflict scenario: one reached `verifying` while its parallel task hit `prompt_count_unavailable` (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-codes-20261107/`); another task pair ended `failed`/`blocked`, with the blocked task reporting `LMStudioServerError` at tokenizer stage (`.acceptance/live-task-workspaces-details-20261107/`). All saved these outcomes with zero unresolved calls; no candidate integration decision ran. The failure-reason persistence regression passes, as do Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright. Hosted run [42351179135](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42351179135) passed both jobs for the earlier safe-reuse harness change; failure-detail persistence CI [42352297669](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42352297669) was superseded and cancelled; later run [42354107200](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42354107200) passed both jobs. This is evidence of a live-model reliability gap, not a passing conflict integration acceptance; Phase 4 remains open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Current local-model evaluation and benchmark safety (2026-11-07)

Refreshed the three-model real LM Studio benchmark at 16,384 requested context after runtime diagnostic/accounting changes. The 12 trials completed with 0.5B 1/4, 0.6B 0/4 and 9B 1/4; all 19 parseable proposals were schema-valid, yet only two runs reached the required exact result. The run was not an idle-hardware comparison: multiple model instances were already resident and host GPU memory sampled around 15.4 GiB / 16.3 GiB, with GPU use shared across instances. A separate post-fix 9B sample reused an existing 16,384-context instance, and both tasks failed (provider error, timeout). Evidence and limitations are in `docs/evaluations/local-model-baseline.md`, `.acceptance/a5144fab-5e9d-4f65-ab40-e87aae067e24/` and `.acceptance/ad33a895-0772-448a-b994-d431395a95e3/`. No production default was selected.

Fixed the benchmark lifecycle to inspect LM Studio's native loaded-instance list, refuse incomplete native inventory, prefer an already-loaded instance at the requested context, record the actual selected instance/context, and unload only instances created by the benchmark. The post-fix live run left existing instance counts/configurations unchanged. Verification: Ruff formatting/lint and strict Pyright pass; the full engine suite passes with 711 passed, one skipped and one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning when run with an isolated pytest basetemp. The initial shared-temp run produced 22 setup-only `PermissionError`s under `%LOCALAPPDATA%/Temp/pytest-of-hicks`; the isolated run had no such errors. This makes future measurements less disruptive; an idle, repeated comparison and peak per-process resource metrics are still needed before default-model selection.
