All 363 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning; Ruff and strict engine/scripts typing pass. The journal checkpoint hosted run 41812880770 also passed; subsequent hosted runs were still active when inspected, so this is not a completed new installer acceptance claim.

Owned snapshot capture, durable controller/supervisor integration, startup/reboot recovery, cross-engine admission, resource packaging, UI grants and real model/native command validation remain unfinished. No model or UI command tool is enabled. Overall completion remains approximately 10%.


## Owned capture and command-controller workflow (2026-11-04)

The internal command controller now joins explicit saved grants, lease-bound identity reservation, isolated owned snapshot capture, registered manifests, original-lease launch authorization, WSL execution, output hash/size checks, registered output artifacts and journal completion. Capture cannot accept a caller-selected path; it derives the project from the saved intent and checks original ownership before and after capture. The bounded binary worker handles up to 8 MiB of file contents and can be stopped independently of the engine event loop. Frozen entry dispatch is implemented but newly packaged/native snapshot-worker acceptance remains pending.

Actual .acceptance/cd208958-8b70-44ab-8065-04cec00fd15a/ verifies a complete successful workflow with three registered artifacts and private writes leaving the original project unchanged. The actual saved cancelled result is failed/not_verified with no exit code; no command remains unresolved. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-command-controller.json. The actor is an automated diagnostic, not a model or UI user.

Remaining: coordinator/supervisor ownership, lease renewal and durable deadline propagation; pending-result/startup/reboot recovery; repeated-cancellation hardening; cross-engine admission; packaged manager resources/frozen worker acceptance; explicit permission UI and real model/native defect validation. ## Cancellation retains cleanup ownership (2026-11-04)

Repeated caller cancellation is now deferred until owned cleanup finishes, then returned to the caller. Cleanup errors remain observable. This applies to command reconciliation, binary IPC termination, filesystem/tokenizer worker termination and supervisor worker waits. Supervisor close cancels all recovery/run owners before awaiting their combined cleanup.

Actual WSL acceptance .acceptance/710f9a80-908e-4f2c-a264-830131bc08fd/ deliberately holds remote reconciliation after pause/lease revocation, sends three additional cancellations, verifies the controller remains waiting, then confirms actual Linux cleanup and failed/not_verified journal resolution without changing the paused revision. Tests also exercise a real sleeping child and repeated cancellation during its awaited shutdown, observed cleanup failure and concurrent supervisor owner cancellation.

The full suite passes 377 tests with the existing Starlette/httpx warning. The final filesystem/tokenizer adoption additionally passes 21 targeted tests. Ruff lint/format and strict engine/scripts typing pass. This closes the covered repeated-cancellation ownership gap; abrupt engine death/startup/reboot recovery, coordinator integration, global admission, resource packaging and model/native permission workflows remain open. No UI/model command tool is enabled; overall completion remains approximately 10%.


## Fresh-controller pending-result recovery (2026-11-04)

An active replacement lease can now recover a saved command identity through the controller without capturing input or launching execution again. Missing/altered output or an absent result remains explicitly not_verified with no fabricated exit code. Stale owners are refused before remote control. Cleanup of a known identity still works if its original project directory has been removed; new capture/launch validation is unchanged.

The diagnostic observes the dead child and explicitly releases its fixture lease; it does not claim production lease-expiry dispatch or a whole API/desktop restart was tested.

## Explicit resume reaches real local-model continuation (2026-11-04)

The coordinator now reconciles pending commands under its new lease before provider diagnostics/inference on resume. Unavailable/mismatched backends block without provider calls. An expired parent deadline blocks before inference after cleanup. Later replay of an already-restored candidate adds no new parent model turn or duplicate feedback.

SDK/HTTP prompt counts match (313, 145, 3119). The candidate reaches verifying rather than accepted completion. The owned model instance is unloaded, with zero loaded instances observed afterward. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/wsl-command-model-resume.json.

This is recovered-result continuation, not a claim that the original command proposal was model-generated.

## Fixed backend readiness API (2026-11-04)

Authenticated POST /execution/readiness now runs only a fixed nonce-based private sandbox self-test for an explicitly selected distro. It verifies identity, capabilities, seccomp, read-only/private mounts, environment and network/namespace denial through the actual cgroup-gated backend, then separately confirms cleanup/dead-manager status. It accepts no project code/path, argv or profile override and creates no run or command grant. Per-engine one-at-a-time admission, short bounded caches and profile invalidation prevent repeated concurrent probes from queuing; unavailable backends fail without host fallback.

Actual source and rebuilt frozen API checks pass ready/cache reuse, missing-distro unavailable, 401 authentication, 422 command injection refusal, zero runs/grants and graceful shutdown. Frozen PATH is System32-only. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/execution-readiness-api.json. The initial environment calibration rejected fixed PWD; the corrected allowlist verifies it is exactly /workspace. The tested readiness sidecar SHA-256 is b2640cc08f78ced53e525d42ebf57d5523ca69a01738c6a166637cd4970ff90e.

All 400 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning, plus Ruff/format, strict typing, generated-contract checks, TypeScript and seven client checks. ## Explicit per-run command authorization (2026-11-04)

POST /runs now accepts an optional strict CommandGrant. Before creating any run/event, the authenticated API requires a current successful server-held readiness observation for the same distro and current profile digest. Both monotonic and UTC expiry, platform support and the full verified-check list are checked. A supplied digest alone cannot establish readiness. The existing store then binds the local-user grant to an existing permitted workspace in the same transaction as run creation. Missing/stale/forged/unavailable authority is refused, with no partial run. Read-only runs continue without grants.

The desktop offers an unchecked Allow isolated commands for this next run control only with Workspace mode, a selected project and a current sandbox result. Workspace/mode/backend/check changes and expiry clear the selection; a successful creation clears it for the next objective. The saved run exposes its immutable grant and displays the selected distro, duration and private-copy/network policy. The grant stays with that run across pause/resume; a fresh run needs a new selection. Command/answer-repair activity labels and saved-run state refresh make the flow visible. The API revalidates even if a client ignores these UI constraints.

Actual Edge acceptance with the real source API, LM Studio qwen/qwen3.5-9b and Ubuntu-24.04 selects the project/distro, runs the fixed check, explicitly grants and creates a run, observes one generated command/one launch/one completed result, shows its exact stdout code and saved grant, and verifies the next-run checkbox reset. The original project is unchanged, private generated output is absent from it, and no execution remains unresolved. The screenshot was visually reviewed. The model instance was unloaded and zero loaded instances were observed. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/command-grant-ui.json. This uses a test connection shim in Edge, not actual Tauri IPC, a rebuilt frozen sidecar or installed GUI acceptance. State remains verifying; no user approval was fabricated.

Eight new engine cases cover auth/no implicit probe, missing/failed/stale checks under both clocks, profile drift, unsupported platform, incomplete checks, forged profile/extra fields, Observe/protected/missing-workspace refusal, atomic saved authority and ordinary runs without grants. Mocked browser coverage verifies permission reset on workspace/mode changes and expiry, including LM Studio unavailable. ## Task-owned filesystem authority before isolated specialist edits (2026-11-04)

The orchestration audit found a prerequisite gap: specialists renewed their own leases before dispatch but their filesystem child processes and output publication checked only the parent run lease. The worker protocol now carries a paired private task identity/token from the trusted runner. A task-scoped RunStore validates the parent lease plus the exact running task, live task token, saved read permission and unchanged task/parent workspace binding at filesystem intent/result transactions. Its run view is Observe-only and omits the parent's command grant. Scoped worker requests accept only internal read/list operations. The parent runner publishes specialist model/tool output through the same task-authority check in the event transaction.

This is consumed by existing read-only specialists, not unused write infrastructure. Ordinary parent workers keep their existing behavior. No database migration or public API field is added. It closes stale/sibling-task authority reuse at these boundaries before isolated editing is enabled; it does not yet create private specialist copies, grant specialist writes/commands or integrate conflicting changes.

Nine new cases exercise actual source worker processes with valid, expired and sibling task tokens, changed workspace/permission/status, revoked parent ownership, scoped listing, protocol-pair validation and rejection of inherited write/command authority. A deterministic opened-file test expires the task after read and proves neither tool result commitment nor specialist publication can succeed while the parent lease remains valid. The unfinished read remains explicitly unresolved for existing reconciliation rather than being represented as completed.

Actual two-specialist qwen/qwen3.5-9b source acceptance retrieves both independent random file codes through the scoped owned workers, leaves parent conversation history empty, and stops with both candidates awaiting review and no unresolved calls. An earlier model attempt repeated harmless reads until its unchanged three-iteration limit; it is retained/indexed. Clearer single-read fixture instructions produced a fresh passing run; no gate or allowance was relaxed. The model is unloaded, with zero loaded instances observed.

A rebuilt frozen --tool-worker acceptance independently verifies Unicode read output under forced CP1252 and System32-only runtime PATH, rejects a scoped write and an expired task, preserves the original project and retains the still-valid parent lease. Exactly one intended/completed read and zero unresolved calls remain. Engine SHA-256: d0f3f1c87dda45b66487f2436bda3dba0b6a0b22d7c188ca5b62b09e3b3e1e64. Evidence: docs/releases/evidence/task-worker-scope.json. Installer CI now runs this WSL/model-free frozen check after packaging; its actual hosted result remains pending.

All 435 engine tests pass with the existing Starlette/httpx warning. Ruff lint/format, strict engine/scripts typing, API-contract check and the native development build pass. This slice changes no renderer or public application contract. Isolated specialist edits, conflict integration, durable routing expansion and the broader orchestration/release gates remain open. Overall completion remains roughly 11%; the goal is active.

Review shows actual commands/output and refuses missing, failed or stale validation selections before accepting these tasks. Task scope is rechecked across command admission/capture/launch/results; cleanup and reconciliation remain available after revocation without replay. ## Durable local scheduling slice (2026-11-05)

Added persisted one-time, daily and weekly schedules using the existing SQLite schedule tables and shared run supervisor. Occurrences are claimed transactionally with unique local-time IDs; scheduled runs use Observe mode, selected local model, saved resource limits, and optionally the selected workspace. Missed work can be skipped or run once at the latest due occurrence; overlapping runs are skipped. Time zones use IANA names, nonexistent daylight-saving wall times are recorded as skipped, and repeated wall-clock times resolve to the earlier offset. The desktop Automations panel lists schedules and execution history and supports create, pause and resume. Delivery destinations and channel delivery are not implemented, and restart/DST/native UI acceptance remains open.

Tests and native schedule acceptance were not run for this slice. The matrix marks scheduling implemented (partial) and does not claim Hermes delivery parity. Overall goal estimate remains approximately 14%.

Scheduling verification follow-up (2026-11-05): the first Windows test exposed that standard-library `zoneinfo` had no IANA database in this Python distribution, also reflected by PyInstaller's missing-`tzdata` warning. Added and locked `tzdata` as an application dependency; the Windows frozen-engine build now processes PyInstaller's `hook-tzdata`. Corrected occurrence history so a claimed run begins in `queued` and can transition with its durable run. Seven new engine tests cover named zones, DST gap and fold policy, DST-skip persistence and advancement, latest-missed `run_once`, overlap skipping, idempotence across SQLite store reopen, selected-model/workspace validation, and schedule pause/history APIs. The desktop test covers model selection, schedule creation, timezone propagation and pause/resume. Full verification passes 495 engine tests, 14 browser tests (two live-only skips), Ruff/format, Pyright, OpenAPI and generated-type checks; the native development app and frozen engine rebuilt. This does not prove cold desktop restart, real-time DST behavior, external notifications or channel delivery. Overall goal remains approximately 14% complete.

## First messaging adapter: Telegram (2026-11-05)

Added a Windows Credential Manager-backed Telegram token setting and private-chat pairing UI. The engine verifies bot tokens with `getMe`, polls only `message` updates, and requires an unexpired high-entropy local pairing code before linking a Telegram user. Linked private text enters the existing run coordinator as an Observe-mode objective with the selected model and no workspace grant. Telegram update IDs, messages, runs and cursors persist transactionally; duplicate updates cannot queue another run. Final public answers and generic terminal-failure notices are delivered only to the paired private chat. Delivery state is claimed before sending; definitive rejection is marked failed, while ambiguous outcome is marked unknown and never automatically resent. A stale cursor is cleared before Telegram's documented weekly update-ID randomization window. Details: [Telegram setup and operation](channels/telegram.md).

Nine engine fixtures cover pairing and authorization, private-only handling, selected-model gating, duplicate input, output history, no-retry ambiguous delivery, cursor reset, token redaction, API settings and a fake-transport service flow from paired message through coordinator submission to reply. A schema 7→8 migration test preserves prior events. A browser test covers secure token input clearing, status, pairing instructions and token removal. Real Telegram interoperability and external sends remain unverified because no bot token or authorized test chat is configured; media, group chats, further channels and cross-channel linking remain open. Do not treat fixtures as live delivery evidence.

Private validation acceptance: `.acceptance/294a45bc-157b-45c1-b76f-b8644715e5f8/` passed two real qwen/qwen3.5-9b specialists, actual Ubuntu-24.04 private-snapshot validators, native validation/output review, explicit conflict integration and graceful shutdown. Frozen capture separately refuses missing/cross-task/expired task scope under System32-only PATH. All 462 engine tests, ten browser checks, seven client checks, strict typing, formatting and contracts pass. Eight preceding live attempts remain indexed, including diagnostic truncation/timeout, wrong CAS hash, over-escaped code and iteration exhaustion. Explicit line-array writes and visible remaining request budgets address observed failure modes without decoding guesses or increasing task allowances. This is a bounded fixture success, not a representative model reliability benchmark or default-model recommendation. Overall release estimate remains approximately 12%; the full objective is still active.

## Scheduled Telegram result delivery (2026-11-05)

Automations can now optionally target one currently paired Telegram private account. The authenticated endpoint validates the destination against the configured bot's active linked-user bindings. Each claimed schedule occurrence creates a `schedule_deliveries` outbox row transactionally with its execution. The shared Telegram service sends the saved final answer, or a generic notice for a failed terminal run, and records `sent`, `failed`, `unknown`, or `revoked` in execution history. Send intent is durable before network I/O; abandoned sends recover to `unknown` and are not retried. Revoking users immediately marks their queued notifications revoked. No destination means no outbox row or external message.

Verification at the original delivery milestone passed all 511 engine tests, 15 browser checks (two live-only skips), seven client checks, static/type/contract checks and native startup/schema smoke. Hosted run 42029567414 later passed both Windows source tests and installer jobs for that commit. Actual Telegram delivery is still unverified because no test bot/chat is configured. Other delivery channels, retry policy, quiet hours and full scheduling parity remained open at that milestone.

### Local-time Telegram quiet hours (2026-11-05)

Scheduled results can now optionally defer Telegram messages during a daily local-time quiet window configured as `HH:MM-HH:MM` in the schedule's timezone. Daytime and overnight windows are supported, including timezone and DST conversion through `zoneinfo`; malformed and zero-length windows are rejected. The scheduler still executes at the configured occurrence; only its authorized result notification is deferred. Pending messages remain pending (no send intent is recorded) until the first Telegram poll outside quiet hours. API/storage, window-boundary and overnight behavior tests plus a browser form scenario were added. Live Telegram delivery remains unverified; bounded delivery retries and failure destinations remain open.

This update verifies **515 engine tests**, 15 browser checks with two live-only skips, seven client checks, Ruff/format, Pyright, OpenAPI/generated types, TypeScript and a rebuilt native app. The native restricted-PATH smoke passed authenticated engine readiness and graceful shutdown in `.acceptance/1c742961-77ff-4923-97d8-cced7d9666bf/report.json`. Hosted run [42032739336](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42032739336) passed the full Windows source suite and installer build, frozen-worker checks, and installed boot/uninstall job. A full-suite Windows restore test also exposed transient directory rename locks; portable bundle publication now retries only known Windows sharing/access-denied errors with a short bounded backoff, and the bundle tests pass. Overall completion remains approximately 14%; real Telegram interop, retries/failure routing, other scheduler requirements and broad product parity remain incomplete.

### Bounded retry for scheduled Telegram rate limits (2026-11-05)

The scheduled delivery outbox now supports a bounded retry only when Telegram explicitly rejects a send with HTTP/API 429 and provides a valid `retry_after` of 1–300 seconds. Retry attempt count and UTC due time persist in schema 10, with a due-retry index. At most three total sends are made; after that, the delivery is failed. The service only retries scheduled result messages. Other definitive rejections fail immediately, while network timeouts, server errors, malformed responses and interrupted sends remain `unknown` and are never repeated. Authorization and quiet-hour checks are performed again before each retry. Schema 9→10 migration preserves existing outbox evidence and initializes old rows with zero attempts. Focused Telegram and migration tests pass (29 cases); the full engine suite passes **519 tests**, the client suite 7, and Ruff/format, Pyright, OpenAPI/generated types and TypeScript all pass. Rebuilt native boot under restricted PATH passed; its fresh database reports schema 10, both delivery indexes, integrity `ok` and no foreign-key errors in `.acceptance/f15b9754-545f-43b4-ba76-5aad73b0d7c2/`. Hosted run [42036532080](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42036532080) passed Windows source checks and the installer build, frozen-worker checks, and installed boot/uninstall workflow. Live Telegram interoperability is unverified; overall completion remains about 14% and wider scheduler, channel and release gates remain open.

Automations execution history now exposes the persisted delivery attempt count and UTC retry deadline through the authenticated schedule API. The browser scenario confirms these are rendered beside `retrying` evidence. This prevents the worker's durable rate-limit wait from appearing indistinguishable from a stuck notification. The full engine suite passes **519 tests**, the focused schedule/Telegram tests pass 27, the Automations browser scenario passes, client checks pass 7, and static/type/contract checks pass. Rebuilt native startup smoke also passes in `.acceptance/e0f45388-2a41-4b1a-a4bc-9436573af063/`. Hosted run [42039881351](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42039881351) passed both source checks and the Windows installer build, frozen-worker checks, and installed boot/uninstall workflow.

Retry restart regression: the bounded Telegram retry test now closes and reopens the SQLite-backed store after a 429, proves the saved attempt remains ineligible one second before its persisted UTC deadline, and claims the same occurrence at the due time. The focused test and Ruff checks pass. The existing retry policy remains limited to explicit bounded 429 responses; real Telegram delivery still requires a configured bot and authorized test chat.

Retry revocation regression: revoking the paired Telegram identity while a 429 delivery is waiting marks the durable retry revoked, retains its single attempted-send count, and prevents it from being claimed after the deadline. Four focused Telegram retry/revocation cases pass with Ruff lint/format. No external Telegram bot was configured, so this verifies local authorization and outbox behavior, not live delivery.

Pinned web/browser/MCP audit slice (2026-11-05): confirmed `.hermes-audit/` is exactly the matrix's pinned Hermes commit and inspected source-level web search/extraction, SSRF transport, browser CDP execution and URL policy, MCP tool registration/liveness, and agent plug-in configuration/security. Added [`web-browser-source-audit.md`](parity/web-browser-source-audit.md) with observed behavior and acceptance requirements per source path, then linked it from the parity matrix. X.A.A. still lacks broad web/browser/MCP parity; this source audit does not count those features as implemented. The phase-7 order is web adapters and SSRF policy, managed Playwright, then MCP lifecycle and permission mapping. The estimate in this historical entry is stale; current accounting is summarized at the top of this document.

### Per-run public web fetch (2026-11-05)

Implemented the first narrow Phase 7 web tool, `web_fetch`. A run can carry up to ten explicitly approved exact hostnames through the desktop form and authenticated run API; the grant is retained in the durable run event log. Fetches accept only HTTPS on the approved hostname and standard port, reject credential-bearing query parameters, resolve and pin only globally routable IPs, and validate each redirect against the same exact host list. Reads are bounded to 512 KiB and 20,000 extracted characters, accept HTML/plain text/JSON, and return/journal content labeled `untrusted_web_content`. The model is instructed to treat returned text as data. This does not yet provide search or browser interaction.

Verification: full engine suite **528 passed**; focused web/API checks passed; Ruff, Pyright, OpenAPI and generated types, TypeScript, Playwright UI workflow, and desktop production build passed. A direct live HTTPS fetch of `https://example.com/` succeeded with pinned DNS and extracted the expected title/content. Repeatable live evidence is recorded in [`web-fetch-live.json`](releases/evidence/web-fetch-live.json) by `scripts/web_fetch_smoke.py`. Overall release-scope estimate remains approximately **15%**; browser workflows, configured search, MCP, and the broader product/release gates remain open.

### Configured web search and durable cancellation (2026-11-05)

Added a Brave Search adapter at the documented fixed HTTPS endpoint. Its API key has authenticated status/save/clear routes and is stored under its own Windows Credential Manager profile; the desktop explains that approved search queries are sent to Brave. Each run must explicitly enable search, separate from its exact-host fetch grants. Search returns at most ten bounded HTTPS results, strips fragments, filters IP literal, credential-bearing, and non-HTTPS URLs, and marks results untrusted. A 1 MiB response limit, 12-second deadline, one provider only, and no redirect or fallback are enforced. Search intent/results are journaled, and web read/search cancellation now saves a cancelled result before propagating cancellation so the tool journal is recoverable.

Verification: the full engine suite passes **537 tests**; the final focused web/provider/API run passes **45 tests**; Ruff, Pyright, OpenAPI and generated types, TypeScript, desktop production build, client suite, and the browser objective/search-settings flow pass. HTTP-level fixtures verify the Brave endpoint, query parameters, authorization header, invalid-response handling, and result filtering without sending a live query. The Windows vault currently has no Brave key configured, so live-provider acceptance remains open. [Brave Search API reference](https://api-dashboard.search.brave.com/api-reference/web/search/get) documents the endpoint and required `X-Subscription-Token` header. The overall release estimate remains approximately **15%**; managed browser automation, live Brave acceptance, MCP, and wider release requirements remain incomplete.

### Streamable HTTP MCP transport (2026-11-05)

Extended the MCP integration settings with the official Streamable HTTP transport alongside stdio. Users can configure HTTPS endpoints (or HTTP on loopback only), optionally store one bearer token in the OS credential store, discover tools, and invoke them through the existing permission and journal path. Endpoint URL credentials, query strings and fragments are rejected; HTTP redirects remain constrained by the MCP SDK's same-origin policy. Schema 15 migrates old MCP server rows to `stdio` without changing their permissions or history. The desktop includes setup for both transports.

Verification: the full engine suite passes **577 tests**. A real local MCP Streamable HTTP fixture test covers tool discovery, default-deny, credential-header delivery, tool invocation, trust labeling, and clean disconnect. Endpoint policy tests reject remote cleartext and embedded URL credentials; a schema-14 migration preserves a legacy stdio server. The stdio integration suite also passes. The focused desktop browser test verifies both setup forms; 11 client tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, API contract and generated-type checks, TypeScript, and the production desktop build pass. Hosted [Windows source and installer checks](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42131511619) passed on commit `4311263`. Broader OAuth, remote-provider, resources/prompts, package discovery, native credential-store acceptance, and full MCP parity remain open. Overall completion remains approximately **16%**.

#### Native Windows MCP setup and permission acceptance (2026-11-05)

Added `npm run test:native:mcp` to rebuildable native acceptance procedures. On the rebuilt Tauri development app and bundled frozen engine, the smoke test launched the actual SDK stdio fixture from the Integrations screen with a System32-only application PATH; discovery returned `add` and `slow`, both started at `none`, granting `read` to `add` left `slow` disabled, and disconnect/removal succeeded. The window closed and the bundled engine logged graceful shutdown. Binary hashes are checked against the stage manifest. Evidence: `.acceptance/3393e4d4-03a4-42d0-b4ff-3fc11fb3aac1/` (`report.json`, screenshot, build digests). This is native configuration, discovery and permission evidence without model inference; model-mediated MCP calls are verified by the source integration fixture, while hosted HTTP/OAuth and installed native MCP acceptance remain open.

### Email restart recovery after transport acceptance (2026-11-05)

Added `scripts/email_crash_diagnostic.py` to exercise the ambiguous SMTP side-effect boundary across an actual worker-process kill and a fresh service cycle. Its deterministic, fsynced local acceptor records one accepted message; the process is killed before the delivery journal can save success. Restart uses the normal `EmailService.cycle()` reconciliation path, records the row as `unknown`, and proves the acceptor did not receive a duplicate. The acceptance report is saved under `.acceptance/<id>/report.json`. This closes a source-level restart-reconciliation evidence gap for the Email slice, but does not claim live SMTP interoperability. The overall goal remains not quantified (historical figure retired) complete; live channel delivery, other audited channels and the broader release criteria remain open.

### Managed browser live read acceptance (2026-11-06)

Added `scripts/browser_smoke.py` and ran it against the public `https://example.com/` page through the actual isolated Microsoft Edge worker subprocess. The run used a one-host browser grant, returned the expected title and bounded visible text, captured a 45,560-byte PNG artifact labeled as untrusted web content, and persisted a completed `browser.run` result. Evidence: [`browser-live.json`](releases/evidence/browser-live.json). This proves the tested Windows Edge navigation, screenshot, cleanup and journal path; it does not verify forms, downloads, persistent sessions, arbitrary sites or general browser parity. The Phase 7 browser slice is now live verified for read/navigation. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired); remaining release criteria are broad.

Hosted verification for these browser and email recovery updates: run [42164666101](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42164666101) passed the Windows source checks and full suite, then built the installer, passed the three frozen-worker diagnostics, and installed, booted and uninstalled the unsigned package. Run [local browser live evidence](releases/evidence/browser-live.json) covers live Edge; local email process-kill evidence remains under `.acceptance/` and intentionally does not claim SMTP interoperability.

### Pinned external plugin source audit and hosted diagnostic timeout (2026-11-06)

Reviewed the pinned official Home Assistant plugin (`4d492b1dc5326af3e38bb2cf3e4b9ad6d65f0072`), Bubblewrap plugin (`a50b0fea59d10f9a51b488206f587d9cb6ca6163`), community WSL Containers plugin (`5bad3fb2baf04ce6befecfefce6d14f4c9c98934`) and Singularity/Apptainer plugin (`d46aced3031a8f533fdbf0806bc4cf0d0b28be07`) without installing or executing them. The audit records Home Assistant's optional entity filters, trusted service-event ingress, accepted cleartext HTTP URLs, non-durable event identity and service-action risks; Bubblewrap's host-path writable workspace, optional host-network profile, per-process rlimits and lack of seccomp; WSL Containers' persistent task writable layers, unsupported hardening flags and unenforced disk limit; and Singularity's mounts, overlays, optional resource flags, advisory-only disk warning, and absence of an explicit network-denial policy in the inspected startup path. Runtime behavior remains unverified. See `parity/external-plugin-source-audit.md`; generated channel and execution inventories encode the reviewed source states without changing X.A.A. feature status.

Hosted run [42165596756](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42165596756) passed Windows source checks and the full test suite, but the installer job timed out on the third frozen private-snapshot worker launch at its per-launch 10-second diagnostic timeout; installer boot/uninstall was consequently skipped. Rebuilt the frozen engine locally and reran the diagnostic sequence successfully after raising only this acceptance diagnostic's per-launch allowance to 30 seconds, within the workflow's existing two-minute step limit. The private command snapshot, frozen snapshot-worker, and task-worker scope diagnostics now all pass locally on the rebuilt engine. Hosted rerun is required; this is a diagnostic timing adjustment, not a product command timeout or a parity claim.

## ACP stdio bridge (2026-11-06)

Implemented the first programmatic host integration through the official `agent-client-protocol` Python SDK pinned at 0.9.0. `xaa acp` serves ACP over stdio and delegates each prompt to the existing authenticated `/runs` API; the run coordinator remains the only model/tool loop. It requires an explicitly selected model and fixed workspace, defaults to observe mode, rejects workspace drift, extra directories, host-provided MCP servers, non-text prompts, and nonnumeric/non-loopback API URLs. ACP session context is persisted in the existing chat-session store, and ACP cancellation calls the durable run-cancel endpoint.

For installed desktop interoperability, the engine publishes its private per-launch bearer token and numeric loopback API URL to temporary Windows Credential Manager entries while it is running; `xaa acp` uses those entries when the corresponding environment variables are absent, and normal engine shutdown deletes them. The frozen sidecar exposes `--cli acp` for installed editor hosts. If the vault cannot be updated, the engine continues to run and ACP remains usable with an explicitly provided protected environment token.

Verification: the actual SDK stdio client spawned `xaa acp` as a subprocess against an authenticated local FastAPI fixture. Four focused cases pass: local URL policy, environment-token precedence/vault lookup, two durable conversational turns with persisted context, and client cancellation reaching the run API. Ruff and strict Pyright pass for the adapter/CLI/credential startup files. The local Windows installer build succeeded, and the frozen engine answered `--cli acp --help`; hosted run 42178236475 passed source checks, frozen worker diagnostics, and the installed package smoke, including `xaa-engine.exe --cli acp --help`, boot, uninstall and data preservation. This does not verify a live LM Studio ACP session, editor-specific behavior, a complete editor launch against an installed desktop process, tool-call progress streaming, additional directories, image/audio, or evaluation batch/training export. ACP session discovery is documented in the later lifecycle entry. Reviewed text-only batch export is now available; provider-specific training integration remains open. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### ACP session reload (2026-11-06)

ACP now advertises session/load, validates the saved session's model/workspace/mode against the selected adapter, replays saved user/assistant text to the host, and continues prompts through the same durable chat record after an adapter restart. A real SDK stdio client test spawns a second process and completes a third turn; four focused ACP cases pass, with Ruff and adapter Pyright clean. Session discovery is now implemented through a bounded authenticated chat-store listing filtered to ACP IDs and the selected model/workspace/mode; the ACP SDK stdio fixture lists the saved session. Editor-specific acceptance and broader programmatic/training parity remain open. Overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

ACP lifecycle now implements session close: it cancels an active run, releases process-local session state and preserves durable history for later reload. Ruff, adapter Pyright and four focused ACP cases pass. Hosted run [42182698984](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42182698984) passed both Windows jobs, including the source suite and packaged CLI, boot and uninstall acceptance. Overall goal remains active.

### Versioned installer upgrade gate (2026-11-06)

Advanced the application version consistently from 0.1.0 to 0.1.1 and added a disposable-runner upgrade path that installs the retained checksum-verified 0.1.0 installer before upgrading in place with the current package. Acceptance checks predecessor version ordering, prior installation, current binary hashes and ACP CLI, desktop boot, user-data preservation and uninstall. Hosted run [42183684313](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42183684313) passed both Windows jobs, including the full source suite, frozen worker diagnostics, and install/upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. The downloaded installer report confirms `prior_version: 0.1.0`, `upgrade_verified: true`, `user_data_preserved: true`, and `passed: true`; the package manifest reports version 0.1.1. The 0.1.0 CI artifact is retained for 14 days, so this provides immediate version-to-version evidence but not a durable release channel. Ruff, Pyright, Cargo lock validation, and npm generated contract checks also pass locally. Overall release remains active.

### ACP tool lifecycle and version metadata (2026-11-06)

The ACP bridge now forwards persisted public coordinator tool intents and outcomes as ACP `tool_call` / `tool_call_update` session notifications, including pending, running, completed and failed states. It omits internal tool calls and does not send raw arguments or results. ACP and FastAPI/OpenAPI version metadata now come from the installed `xaa-engine` distribution; the PyInstaller build copies that metadata and ACP exposes `--version`. The installer workflow adds a frozen-engine ACP version check.

Verification: five focused ACP/API cases pass, including SDK stdio delivery of successful and failed tool lifecycles, internal-call omission, and no raw input/output fields. Ruff, strict Pyright, and generated local package version checks pass. A rebuilt frozen sidecar passes `scripts/frozen_acp_diagnostic.py` and reports ACP version 0.1.1 without model inference. Hosted run [42187623993](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42187623993) passed both Windows jobs, including the source suite, frozen ACP entry-point/version check, frozen worker diagnostics, and installed package smoke. Its initial run surfaced the expected OpenAPI version drift; the contract was regenerated, and the corrected run passed. ACP still has no streamed tool content or editor-specific end-to-end acceptance; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Live ACP and local-model verification (2026-11-06)

ACP polling now treats `verifying` as a prompt-terminal state: it returns the candidate plus an explicit desktop-review notice and does not store the candidate as a completed chat turn. The pinned ACP SDK's unstable `session/close` method is explicitly enabled, and the stdio lifecycle test calls it and verifies durable history remains reloadable. Added `scripts/acp_live_smoke.py`, which uses only an already-loaded local model, an authenticated temporary API, a random disposable workspace fixture, and no user-project writes.

Live acceptance passed twice against already-loaded LM Studio `qwen3-0.6b`: the model invoked `workspace.read_file`, received the fixture contents, returned the exact random token, and produced ACP `pending` and `completed` notifications. The coordinator correctly remained in `verifying`, and the adapter returned the result as a candidate awaiting independent desktop review. The sanitized evidence is in `docs/releases/evidence/acp-tool-lifecycle-live.json`. Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, the focused ACP/API selection (6 passed), and generated API contract check pass. One existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning remains. Editor-specific acceptance and the broader ACP capability gaps remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### ACP durable session deletion (2026-11-06)

Implemented ACP v1 `session/delete`, which the pinned Python SDK does not yet expose as a typed route. X.A.A. registers the standard method on the SDK router, advertises `sessionCapabilities.delete`, and deletes only a matching ACP-shaped session bound to the selected model, workspace and mode. Unknown/already-deleted sessions return success. Concurrent deletion removes the session from local use, cancels an active run, and synchronizes with prompt persistence so deleted history is not recreated. The authenticated API performs the setting check and row deletion in one SQLite write transaction.

The real SDK stdio test exercises the wire-level delete method after cross-process session reload, verifies the session disappears from `session/list`, and deletes an active run while confirming cancellation. Storage and API tests cover setting mismatch, authentication, idempotence and malformed IDs. Full local verification passes: Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API/OpenAPI and generated desktop contract checks, TypeScript, 12 client tests, and **620 Python tests** (one Windows symlink skip; one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). Hosted run [42194174464](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42194174464) passed both the Windows source suite and installer job, including frozen ACP version verification and installed upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. Protocol behavior follows the [ACP v1 session delete contract](https://agentclientprotocol.com/protocol/v1/session-delete). Streamed tool content, editor-specific acceptance, provider-specific training integration and broader integration gaps remain open; overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Bounded ACP tool result content (2026-11-06)

Completed public tool calls now include their persisted result as ACP content, capped at 16 KB. An authenticated API endpoint returns only a resolved result for a public call and does not expose arguments or internal tool results; the ACP bridge leaves raw input/output fields unset. SDK stdio and storage/API coverage verifies the content delivery, size cap, internal-call filtering and authentication. The full Python suite passes (622 passed, one Windows symlink skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning), along with Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, OpenAPI export and desktop type generation/checks. Hosted run [42196654721](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42196654721) passed both Windows jobs, including frozen ACP/version checks, worker diagnostics, and installed upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. Incremental streaming, editor-specific acceptance, evaluation batches/training export and broader programmatic gaps remain open. Overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### ACP text delta forwarding (2026-11-06)

The ACP adapter forwards persisted `model.delta` events as ordered `agent_message_chunk` notifications, splits large updates into bounded chunks, and suppresses duplicate final text while preserving any unsent review notice. The SDK stdio fixture verifies fragment order, final-answer de-duplication and final-only fallback. The Tauri desktop launcher now enables streamed coordinator responses by default; direct engine/API launches remain opt-in with `XAA_STREAM_RESPONSES=1`. A real ACP smoke against already-loaded `qwen3-0.6b` passed with two persisted deltas and the exact fixture answer; sanitized evidence is `releases/evidence/acp-tool-streaming-live.json`. The rebuilt native desktop completed a real `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` streamed workspace edit: five delta events, three validated tool proposals before effects, partial text visible in the UI, correct file result and graceful engine shutdown. Evidence: `.acceptance/2a00f639-72ea-4968-bcb5-9f130a47213c/`. An earlier 0.6B attempt stopped before tool dispatch with a filesystem-worker interruption; its sanitized trace is preserved under `.acceptance/acp-streaming-failure-0debe829-1628-4c9c-9909-ae52d5ee1691/`, and the smoke now retains a disposable evidence directory for diagnosis. Rust launcher regression test, native build, focused ACP tests and the full Python suite pass (623 passed, one Windows symlink skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, OpenAPI and desktop type checks pass. Hosted run [42203490382](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42203490382) passed engine checks and the Windows installer build, frozen ACP/worker checks, and installed upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. Real editor acceptance, reconnect behavior, incremental tool output and native-WebView specialist inference remain open; task-scoped specialist streaming is implemented and has live local provider plus browser evidence in the next section. Overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Specialist response streaming (2026-11-06)

When streamed responses are enabled, specialist inference now uses the provider's streaming path and persists content as task-scoped `task.model.delta` events in bounded 128-character chunks. The desktop plan panel displays each task's partial draft as unverified; final candidates remain behind the existing independent review flow. Unit coverage checks task identity, event ordering, bounded chunks, saved final output and coordinator flag propagation; the browser test verifies task-specific rendering. A real LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` run completed two read-only specialists, each with four deltas preceding its final candidate; the sanitized report is `releases/evidence/specialist-streaming-live.json`, with the retained local report under `.acceptance/5edc5f3b-dcea-41e7-9b36-2e7ff89f8ce4/`. The 26 focused specialist/task tests pass, and the full Python suite passes (624 passed, one Windows symlink skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning); Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, desktop typecheck and the task-specific browser test pass. The rebuilt native application smoke found 29 models, exited normally and left its engine endpoint unreachable using an isolated `.acceptance/` app profile; no shutdown-log markers were available in that custom profile, so graceful log confirmation is not claimed. The opt-in seeded native specialist scenario bypasses model-generated plan creation but exercises the visible diagnostic, real specialist inference, task-panel streaming, evidence and reviews. Passing run `.acceptance/0756e43e-8932-461b-a062-8ddfceddc7e0/` on `qwen3-0.6b` completed both dependent tasks; inspect and review each recorded its own real file read, 3 and 4 bounded deltas respectively were shown in their task panels, independent review gates were accepted, and the native app exited with its engine stopped. This proves the seeded native specialist vertical slice, not model-generated planning or broad model reliability. The run also prompted an explicit runtime instruction requiring specialists to ground workspace claims in actual tool results. A 9B attempt failed before task dispatch because LM Studio returned “chat request failed; check the local server status” during its harmless tool diagnostic (`.acceptance/8ece3a7a-c9a2-42fc-9a5f-547a5d5af07f/`). Earlier native failures remain counterexamples: stream assembly failures in `.acceptance/ca692feb-3a8e-44d9-9ac0-58189e17f99e/` and `.acceptance/382215b8-4b6c-4e67-90ea-f9d035ce3d46/`, and a Qwen2.5 Coder 1.5B diagnostic failure in `.acceptance/a937583f-0d0b-4f00-aa5c-dad795dcb88f/`. The original model-generated plan failure remains in `.acceptance/42279cba-d7ee-4a4e-ba6c-e20d462b0831/`. Reconnect behavior, incremental tool output, editor-specific integration and broad per-model reliability also remain open. Hosted run [42207993265](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42207993265) passed both the Windows source and installer jobs for the preceding streamed-specialist implementation. Overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).


### Cross-model tokenizer compatibility probe (2026-11-06)

Extended `scripts/tokenizer_parity_diagnostic.py` with explicit installed-model/context selection, bounded count timeout, one persistent SDK connection for direct runs, per-fixture HTTP failure capture, strict non-null parity checks and safe worker error-type reporting. The source-owned worker now skips the SDK context manager's separate one-second HTTP health probe, which timed out during cross-process tests despite a working WebSocket connection. Engine-owned Qwen3 0.6B parity passes all four fixtures (11, 159, 193, 38); Qwen2.5 0.5B also passes all four (32, 177, 216, 38) on repeat after one transient history WebSocket error. Evidence is `.acceptance/0b80855a-9ee4-4207-8aaf-cad562e6c828/` and `.acceptance/2268146f-47f0-4d4e-a709-2264e5707aa7/`. Direct Qwen3 counts also complete across all fixtures when the probe skips that health check, though one HTTP fixture failed transiently in `.acceptance/ed47cfb3-bba1-4e2c-9136-6cb515d5471b/`. Full scope and earlier misdiagnoses are reconciled in `../evaluations/tokenizer-parity.md`.

Exact-context mode remains opt-in and fails closed on tokenizer errors. The worker lifecycle discrepancy is fixed for the tested small models; broader models, templates and long histories remain to be evaluated before widening exact-budget claims. Its bounded worker allowance is now 20 seconds including queue wait because a cold first count exceeded five seconds. Real exact-context acceptance on Qwen3 0.6B/context 8192 rejects normal and streamed over-context requests before prediction, then completes an admitted streamed response with matching SDK/HTTP prompt counts (13): `.acceptance/cc1b501b-b60f-4ee7-a608-4c4377039af0/`. Timing evidence is `.acceptance/b4667abb-cd9e-4db2-9ab0-72653aebff34/`. Engine-owned parity passes 4/4 on Qwen3 0.6B and Qwen2.5 0.5B. Focused mutation/tokenizer/context tests pass 34 cases. Hosted run [42222978132](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42222978132) exposed a Windows-only WSL UNC resolution failure in the reserved-private-root test; the policy now checks that protected UNC namespace before resolving or contacting the share. Follow-up hosted run [42224481116](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42224481116) passed the full Windows source suite and installer acceptance, including frozen workers and installed upgrade/boot/uninstall. Overall release remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Desktop event-stream retry continuity

The event client previously counted every transient disconnect over the lifetime of a run against its five-retry limit, even after successfully delivering later events. It now resets that bounded retry counter after each validated persisted event, so a long task can survive repeated recoveries while consecutive failures remain bounded. The regression test injects eight distinct truncated SSE interruptions, checks cursors advance from 0 through 8, and confirms the client yields events 1–9 exactly once. `npm run test:client` passes all 13 tests and `npm run typecheck` passes. The existing browser fixture covers one interrupted stream and saved candidate recovery; native-WebView reconnection during a real model run remains open. Overall release remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Bounded coordinator recovery for incomplete streamed replies

A native generated-plan attempt on 2026-11-06 (`.acceptance/37d5cdf4-9833-4f77-8d05-daf028ad2574/`) listed its workspace and then blocked because the following streamed response could not be assembled. The coordinator previously discarded the safe assembler error code and stopped immediately. It now records `model.stream_rejected` with only the inference ID and bounded error code, then allows one fresh inference for incomplete/empty responses or invalid assembled tool arguments, if the run's iteration and token budgets permit. The repair prompt states that no part of the rejected response ran and tells the model to continue from completed tool results without repeating effects. Partial tool calls still never execute; a second invalid stream blocks the run. `tests/unit/test_stream_assembly.py` passes 17 cases, including a recovered response and two failed partial-write attempts with no file change or tool intent. Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass for the changed files.

The rebuilt native desktop then passed the previously failing **model-generated planning** acceptance on local LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b`: it listed a random fixture workspace, saved exactly two tasks with the requested inspect→review dependency, returned the final “plan is saved” response, kept tasks queued for user dispatch, and shut down the engine. Evidence and build hashes are in `.acceptance/ae214f45-f0d4-4dac-96b9-07d387277092/`; the plan was not dispatched in this planning-only scenario. A broader native attempt to generate and dispatch specialists did not reach planning: the first harmless diagnostic inference succeeded, but its second request failed with `ProviderError`; no specialists were dispatched. Evidence: `.acceptance/5379a902-0b28-439f-9600-f6b0f8a05a90/`. This closes the model-generated planning smoke gap but does not establish specialist execution from a model-generated graph, general model reliability, or native stream interruption recovery. The focused stream suite passes (17 tests), and changed-file Ruff/formatting/strict Pyright pass. The first local full-suite invocation hit 15 Windows `PermissionError` setup errors on the shared `%TEMP%\pytest-of-hicks` directory; rerunning with isolated `--basetemp=.pytest-tmp-stream-recovery-20261106` passed **629 tests, 1 platform skip, 1 existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning**. Hosted run [42231043694](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42231043694) passed both Windows source checks and installer acceptance, including frozen workers and installed upgrade/boot/uninstall. Overall release remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

Hosted run [42226318542](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42226318542) passed the full Windows source suite and frozen worker checks but failed the installed desktop boot smoke at its 15-second close deadline. Its retained diagnostics show the engine completed graceful shutdown and the desktop process exited immediately after that deadline. The smoke now waits up to 30 seconds for desktop exit and records the actual close wait; installer revalidation is pending.

### Managed browser form structure inspection (2026-11-06)

Added an opt-in `inspect_forms` operation to the disposable Edge worker. It reports at most 20 visible forms, 100 visible controls, and 20 choice labels per select, with bounded tag/type/name/label/required metadata. The worker never reads current control values or form actions; the browser remains limited to approved HTTPS hosts and GET/HEAD requests, with filling, submission, button clicks, downloads and persistent profiles unavailable. Updated the desktop grant explanation, output schema and parity matrix. The full Python suite passes (643 passed, one Windows-only skip, one existing Starlette/httpx warning) using an isolated repository-local pytest temp directory; 27 focused browser/API tests pass; Ruff lint, formatting, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript, client tests (13), and generated API types check pass. Live Edge inspection of `https://httpbin.org/forms/post` found one form and 12 controls, journaled the bounded result, and confirmed control values are absent in [`browser-forms-live.json`](releases/evidence/browser-forms-live.json). Hosted [Windows source and installer checks](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42257809643) passed, including frozen worker diagnostics and installed boot/uninstall acceptance. The one filtered browser scenario was a live LM Studio test and skipped because it is not applicable to this UI change. Wider browser security acceptance remains open; this does not claim form interaction or general browser parity. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Managed browser DNS address hardening (2026-11-06)

Expanded the resolver rejection matrix across loopback, private, link-local, carrier-grade NAT, documentation, unspecified, IPv6 unique-local, and multicast ranges. It caught that Python reports IPv4 multicast as `is_global`; the proxy DNS gate now also rejects multicast and unspecified addresses, allowing only globally reachable unicast answers before pinning the connection. Focused browser tests pass (22), Ruff and strict Pyright pass, and the full suite passes (659 passed, one Windows-only skip, one existing Starlette/httpx warning). These are resolver-policy tests; live proxy bypass and redirect/subresource cases remain open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Managed browser request guard coverage (2026-11-06)

Extracted the Playwright route callback into `guard_browser_route`, the same helper still registered for every request with `**/*`. Focused route tests cover an allowed document and HEAD request, blocked cross-host document navigation (redirect target), blocked cross-host script subresource, and blocked POST. The full suite passes (664 passed, one Windows-only skip, one existing Starlette/httpx warning); Ruff and strict Pyright pass. This tests the shared routing decision, not a live redirected network chain; live redirect/subresource behavior, internal-address bypass attempts, and cancellation/process-tree acceptance remain open. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Managed browser cancellation process-tree acceptance (2026-11-06)

Added a Windows integration test that runs the managed worker wrapper around a controlled child process which continuously writes a marker. Cancelling `_execute_worker` invokes the real owned-process cleanup; the test confirms the child stops writing and the worker exits. This verifies cancellation kills the owned process tree without starting Edge or contacting a website. The full suite passes (665 passed, one Windows-only skip, one existing Starlette/httpx warning); Ruff/format and strict Pyright pass. Real Edge cancellation during navigation, timeout, and crash recovery remain separate acceptance cases. Overall completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Per-request tokenizer worker stage diagnostics (2026-11-06)


Exact-count failures now preserve a bounded worker stage (`request_validation`, `server_connection`, `model_inventory`, `prompt_formatting`, `tokenization`, or `context_lookup`) alongside the exception class, and the coordinator journals those codes in `context.admission_failed` without logging prompt contents or SDK error text. A maximum of two retries is allowed only for `LMStudioWebsocketError` during the read-only loaded-model inventory call, with 0.25/0.5-second backoff under the original 20-second deadline; tokenization and other errors remain fail-closed. Tokenizer worker reports may now include only a bounded machine-style LM Studio error code matching a strict identifier pattern; free-form SDK messages, prompt contents and paths stay excluded. The code is preserved in `context.admission_failed` and shown in desktop activity when an exact-count failure occurs. ### Reviewed specialist deletion integration

Write-enabled specialists can now propose deleting a file from their private snapshot using the file hash they just read. The 16-file/1 MiB specialist change limit now counts deleted paths by original byte size and supports restoring a deleted path under the net limit. Completed private journal replay now applies write/create/delete operations in event order and checks each expected hash against the prior private bytes, including matching deletion results. Two new regressions cover write→delete→restore and a tampered expected deletion hash. The 27 focused specialist workspace/scope tests pass, and the real Qwen3.5 9B deletion diagnostic passed again against this replay validation in `.acceptance/28aabe4f-8676-40c6-b175-c20b30d6f992/report.json`. The review diff labels deletions; the integration preview checks the current project hash, and conflicts require an explicit choice tied to that hash. An approved deletion is performed only by the internal integration worker: it holds exclusive Windows handle access, rejects reparse points and hard-link aliases, checks the bytes against the saved hash, records `tool.executing`, and marks the opened file for deletion. Interrupted deletion recovery observes missing/unchanged/conflicting states and never replays the delete. Focused specialist workspace and task scope tests pass (25); deletion-focused tests including recovery pass (4); quota regression confirms deletions count toward the 16-file/1 MiB budget and restoring a deleted path returns the net diff under the cap. The full Python suite passes (699 passed, one platform skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning). The latest local checks pass Ruff lint/format (248 files), strict Pyright, API export and generated-type checks, desktop TypeScript, and all 13 client tests. Hosted run [42310520632](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42310520632) passed both Windows source and installer jobs. Commit `26fa01c` adds ordered journal-replay validation; its hosted Windows source checks and full suite passed in [42311612579](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42311612579), and both the Windows installer build and install/boot/uninstall acceptance passed. Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript, API schema export/check, generated type check and `git diff --check` pass. `npm run build:native:dev` succeeded, and the rebuilt frozen `--tool-worker` diagnostic verified a private deletion proposal left the source file intact, then removed that exact file only after it received a saved user authorization; evidence: `.acceptance/49029969-18e9-4856-a8d0-11a5e71d7d1f/report.json` with engine SHA-256 `c9ce063e831c279c4b98ed78234b4917600bb523617034eb1aae1aca47d5d664`. A follow-up removed the standalone internal worker delete operation so integration through saved authorization is the only worker path to host deletion. The focused Playwright review-panel suite passes four cases, covering edit/delete proposals with and without private-validation requirements. A real Qwen3.5 9B specialist also proposed a private deletion, preserved the original until explicit scripted review, then deleted only the selected file while retaining its neighbor; report: `.acceptance/72fec94f-9e4e-4ee9-add9-56546cd232ab/report.json`. The initial real-model attempt failed exact SDK prompt counting during preflight before any specialist action; the successful behavior diagnostic disabled exact-context admission, whose separate intermittent blocker remains documented. Frozen acceptance is deterministic and does not cover native UI integration. The operation fails closed on non-Windows platforms. Hosted run [42309419325](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42309419325) stopped at `ruff format --check` before type, contract or test steps. The formatter issue was fixed in a later pushed revision; subsequent hosted source job passed. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Specialist worker-loss recovery visibility (2026-11-06)

Expired specialist workers remain fenced, and the authenticated plan/recovery API plus desktop panel let the user inspect saved candidates, completed tool results, proposed calls, unresolved intents, iteration attempts and measured/reserved tokens. Three explicit decisions are available: resume only from a safe persisted boundary and within the parent budget, send a final saved candidate through the ordinary evidence-review path, or mark the task failed. Resume reconstructs the conversation from saved messages and tool results under a new worker lease; it never repeats a completed tool effect. Ambiguous in-flight actions remain unresolved and cannot be retried automatically. The focused specialist recovery/claim and authenticated API cases pass, as does the desktop browser panel scenario. The full Python suite currently passes 709 cases (one platform skip and one existing Starlette/httpx warning), with Ruff, strict Pyright, generated contracts, TypeScript and all 13 client tests passing. ### Live reviewer model follow-up (2026-11-06)

The reviewer artifact inspection path now has an end-to-end deterministic runtime test: it rejects a premature candidate, reads a private changed file, pages stdout in 5-byte ranges, inspects stderr, and submits only after the saved byte ranges cover the complete outputs. A real LM Studio reviewer acceptance was attempted against the configured local models. `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` timed out during the harmless tool diagnostic; `qwen3-0.6b` and a subsequent `qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct` request returned HTTP 400; `qwen/qwen2.5-coder-1.5b-instruct` completed the diagnostic but returned no structured tool call. These attempts used random fixtures under `.acceptance/` and did not modify project files.

### Paged independent review of large changed files (2026-11-06)

Reviewer access now pages changed UTF-8 artifacts in bounded 16 KiB character ranges, preserving exact file hashes and rejecting offsets beyond EOF. The candidate gate requires contiguous coverage of every changed file from character zero through EOF, with a separate deletion marker for removed paths. This removes the earlier 64 KiB read ceiling while keeping each model result bounded.

The deterministic runtime test reads a changed artifact in 5-character pages alongside paged stdout/stderr, verifies the premature candidate is rejected, confirms the gate opens only after complete contiguous coverage, and checks deletion proposals through explicit deletion metadata. The full Python suite passes (709 passed, one platform skip, one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning); the updated focused reviewer deletion case passes. Ruff, strict Pyright, desktop TypeScript and generated API contract checks pass. Hosted run [42328038513](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42328038513) passed both Windows jobs, including frozen-worker and installer upgrade/install acceptance. Live model reviewer acceptance remains open due the model-specific tool failures recorded above; overall release progress remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Opt-in active procedure discovery (2026-11-06)

`GET /skills` now exposes only bounded name, description and category metadata for desktop discovery, choosing the active version's metadata when one exists. The user can explicitly ask for suggestions against the current objective; the desktop considers active procedures only, uses conservative token overlap (two distinct metadata terms or exact name), displays matched terms, and returns at most five. Suggestions do not select a run version or load procedure text. The user inspects the exact active version and separately selects it; run creation still transactionally checks that it remains active and fits current mode/workspace. Candidate drafts are excluded, and changing the objective clears prior results. Storage coverage verifies active-version metadata is shown instead of a newer draft; client and browser tests confirm candidate filtering and explicit selection. Checks: 19 skills tests pass, all 14 client tests pass, the targeted browser flow passes, TypeScript, generated OpenAPI/types, Ruff and strict storage typing pass. This is opt-in lexical assistance, not semantic retrieval or full Hermes discovery parity. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Fresh-process write recovery acceptance (2026-11-06)

Strengthened `scripts/write_crash_diagnostic.py`: after killing the run-owning process, recovery now happens in a separate newly launched process that reopens SQLite, waits for the expired lease, acquires fresh ownership, and reconciles without replay. Source runs passed at both the post-fsync/pre-result boundary and the exclusive-create/pre-content boundary. The same two boundaries passed through the rebuilt frozen `xaa-engine.exe` reconciliation worker (SHA-256 `c9ce063e831c279c4b98ed78234b4917600bb523617034eb1aae1aca47d5d664`). Applied bytes retained their original mtime; the incomplete empty creation remained conflicting and unresolved, with the run blocked. Ruff lint/format and strict Pyright pass for the diagnostic. This proves fresh-process run-owner/recovery-worker behavior for these filesystem journal boundaries; it does not exercise a full API/Tauri restart, reboot, or external service effect. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Authenticated API restart recovery (2026-11-06)

Added `scripts/api_restart_diagnostic.py`, which starts authenticated FastAPI subprocesses against a disposable SQLite database and uses a deterministic fake provider. The inference scenario kills the API before a response/effect; the restarted supervisor recovers the expired run and saves one candidate for review. The persisted-write scenario waits for the managed filesystem worker's durable completion, kills the API during the next blocked provider request, and verifies the restarted prompt contains the saved call/result without rewriting the file. A Windows-only `write_crash` scenario creates an exclusive file in a disposable nested fixture path, holds a SQLite writer lock immediately after intent persistence, and kills the API plus owned filesystem-worker process tree while creation is paused before `tool.executing` and content. After releasing the lock, the harness confirms no child advances the effect. The restarted supervisor quarantines the unresolved intent; authenticated resume attempts reconciliation, finds the empty file conflicts with the requested content, and leaves the run blocked and unresolved without execution, completion, or provider retry. File bytes and mtime remain unchanged. A second Windows-only `write_result_crash` scenario starts with an existing file, waits for `tool.executing`, then locks SQLite while the worker overwrites and fsyncs 64 KiB; it kills the API and worker before `tool.completed` can be journaled. Restart quarantines the unknown write, and authenticated resume matches the exact requested bytes, records the reconciled result and completion, then continues to a candidate without rewriting the file (mtime is unchanged). Evidence: inference `.acceptance/api-restart-d7561c40-5fba-4fa4-aa57-8003a42cacb6/`; persisted write `.acceptance/api-restart-544afa52-bc63-443f-9a3e-edf5d131f515/`; create crash `.acceptance/api-restart-374707c3-0976-4b9c-ba71-7e89d059b363/`; existing-file post-fsync crash `.acceptance/api-restart-7cbf32ce-7900-4c62-b607-eb9f9105d18f/`. The first attempt acquired the SQLite lock before the worker emitted `tool.executing`; a later run passed all recovery invariants but had an incorrect harness check for a state-change event not emitted by recovery. Those diagnostic iterations remain in `.acceptance/api-restart-c14142df-8b38-4941-9748-b8a351a2905c/` and `.acceptance/api-restart-730a5cb6-dce3-4472-8935-873498ac9ad3/`. Ruff, formatting, and strict Pyright pass. These source diagnostics use a deterministic provider and shortened lease. They do not prove live LM Studio, frozen API recovery at the existing-file fsync boundary, desktop/Tauri restart, reboot recovery, external-effect recovery, or specialist adoption. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Frozen authenticated API restart at an incomplete create (2026-11-06)

Added `scripts/frozen_api_restart_diagnostic.py` and exercised packaged `xaa-engine.exe` (`a0890bf26a3751c0ca9a1c7ddbd9ce6bda81ce9d7c3a7815cafbc9e029aae4`) against a local deterministic OpenAI-compatible fixture; no LM Studio server/model or external provider is used. The exclusive-create acceptance passes at `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-76735679-38b8-4515-b23e-bd40ca11f78d/` and, after a polling change, again at `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-565f53cc-850a-4481-a1fd-e60f2c843921/`: it kills the frozen API and owned worker while SQLite blocks the post-create execution journal, then confirms restart and authenticated resume keep the conflicting empty file blocked without retry. Three intermediate repeats missed the narrow pause; pre-opening the SQLite lock connection and busy-polling the journal resolved the repeatability issue. Their evidence remains in `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-1e285a72-c9bf-4536-8273-6074d47f8b5d/`, `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-490e1908-ab8a-4af2-ac83-845207d56936/`, and `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-37abf080-2a65-41e4-b2b7-5e641d22a576/`. The harness now also accepts `XAA_FROZEN_API_RESTART_SCENARIO=write_result_crash`. It starts with an existing file, locks SQLite after `tool.executing`, kills the frozen API/worker after a 64 KiB overwrite is fsynced but before `tool.completed`, and restarts the packaged service. Authenticated resume reconciles the exact bytes, records completion and continues to verifying without rewriting (unchanged mtime). Evidence: `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-7d40bcc0-b8f4-41b7-a056-09f769aa0348/` (latest rerun after polling adjustment); an earlier pass is `.acceptance/frozen-api-restart-04194850-15a4-4378-98c6-3e7fb5ed32d3/`. Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass; the harness cleans its generated launch credentials. This closes both source and frozen API recovery at the existing-file post-fsync boundary, but not desktop/Tauri lifecycle restart, reboot, live LM Studio, external effects or specialist task adoption. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).
## Locked dependency and license inventory (2026-11-07)

Added `docs/releases/dependency-inventory.md`, a snapshot table of 540 unique ecosystem/name/version/license/source records from the locked Python, npm and Cargo dependencies: 69 Python distribution records, 61 npm records (six runtime), and 411 Cargo package records including target-specific and build dependencies. Raw scans and the npm audit report are retained under `.acceptance/dependency-inventory-971fa5b/`. `npm audit --json` reports zero current lockfile vulnerabilities; the 2026-11-03 OpenAPI generator advisory is no longer reported although the same package chain remains locked, so the incompatible override stays reverted. The inventory exposes unresolved release legal work: the repository has no root LICENSE/NOTICE and the project package metadata has no selected license; PyInstaller metadata includes GPLv2 and its packaging exception needs review; the full-text notice draft, dependency inventory, and release notes are staged in the unsigned installer with manifest-hash verification; copyright review and application licensing remain open. These facts are not a license decision or legal approval. Hosted run [42340416648](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42340416648) passed both Windows jobs at code commit `971fa5b`, including the full source suite and installer upgrade/boot/uninstall checks. All six previously unrepresented Windows crate licenses are now attached to their package attributions: five recovered from exact upstream VCS revisions and selectors from Mozilla’s official MPL-2.0 text. All six downloaded crate SHA-256 values match Cargo.lock. The notice-attribution audit found zero missing entries in its scoped inputs: 67 installed Python development distributions, five external npm runtime packages, and all 247 third-party Windows-target Cargo packages. Source/text and attribution details are retained in `.acceptance/dependency-license-upstream-20261107/verification.json`; Python scope remains the packages installed on this host. The unsigned installer stages the notice draft, dependency inventory, and 0.1.1 release notes, with manifest hashes checked during install acceptance. Crate archives still omit the text files; copyright and exception review and the undecided application license remain open. This draft excludes X.A.A.’s own license and remains provisional.

### Native Tauri desktop process-death recovery (2026-11-07)

Added `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs`, `npm run test:native:restart`, and a Windows installer-CI step.
