Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Native named-specialist recovery after desktop process death (2026-11-07)

Extended `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs --specialist` and added `scripts/seed_native_specialist_restart.py` plus a Windows installer-CI step. The actual Tauri app and bundled engine use an isolated profile and deterministic local provider. A named researcher reads a random fixture exactly once, saves the result, then blocks during its continuation request; the harness kills the desktop process tree, edits the global researcher identity/instructions, expires the run/task leases and restarts against the same database. The authenticated recovery summary reports one saved result and `safe_to_resume`; after the explicit resume decision, the same saved role prompt and route continue and the final candidate contains the exact nonce. The source workspace remains unchanged, there is one read proposal/result and one recovery decision. The local Windows report is `.acceptance/native-specialist-restart-96981757-132e-4e40-bd9a-a488a07e26c2/report.json` (three role-prompt checks span the first request, pre-crash continuation and post-restart continuation; the changed profile does not affect the persisted task). The earlier harness attempts exposed and corrected two acceptance assumptions: private instructions are confirmed through the actual model prompt because the plan API omits them, and the pre-crash result continuation is also a role-prompt check. Hosted Windows source and installer acceptance passed in [run 42403339439](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42403339439), including this native scenario, ordinary desktop restart, frozen specialist restart, packaged worker checks, and install/boot/uninstall. Provider behavior is deterministic and local; live LM Studio specialist recovery, reboot recovery, ambiguous external effects, cross-account continuity and broader orchestration acceptance remain open. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Native live-model specialist restart (2026-11-07)

Added an opt-in `--live-specialist` mode to `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs`. It proxies native-engine traffic to the actual LM Studio OpenAI-compatible server and only holds the researcher’s post-read continuation, creating a reproducible desktop process-death window without fabricating model output. On the Windows development build, LM Studio `qwen3-0.6b` passed its harmless tool diagnostic, called `workspace_read_file` once, saved the random result, and reached the held continuation. After Tauri/engine process death, the same profile/database restarted, global role identity/instructions had been changed, the authenticated summary allowed safe resume from one saved result, and the resumed request contained the original role prompt plus that exact saved result. The actual model returned the exact nonce and reached `verifying`; workspace bytes were unchanged and no second read was needed. Evidence: `.acceptance/native-live-specialist-restart-ff431ca1-0301-4209-98d0-6b6434b31bbb/report.json`; desktop SHA-256 `5060057e80aeed2a60ac755b92c5907d403c3d21104de5ad0d9a5c1d969aaae7`; engine SHA-256 `43db9a8c1c4f4de82443559cfeb92800627420e1b696c753bd837009aad2e748`. The model was loaded at 16,384 context; the script now fails early if the configured model is unloaded or below 8,192. One initial attempt at 4,096 context correctly blocked after the saved read because the continuation exceeded context. A later retry showed that this small model can sometimes propose a redundant but read-only file read after recovery; the passing report required the saved result in the resumed prompt and exact candidate evidence, while the task had only `workspace.read` permission. The pass reports zero measured model tokens because this LM Studio streamed response omitted usage totals, so it is not token-accounting evidence. LM Studio was not left running and the test launch credential pair was cleaned. This is one real-model native restart path, not broad model reliability, reboot recovery, write-effect recovery or external-effect reconciliation. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Live-model private specialist conflict integration (2026-11-07)

Continued Phase 4 conflict acceptance with LM Studio `qwen/qwen3.5-9b` at the exact loaded 16,384-token context and conservative context admission. The actual model independently edited `shared.py` in two private specialist snapshots, returned distinct randomized values, reread the saved edits, and both variants passed static AST validation while the original project file stayed byte-identical. The fixture review accepted and integrated the first candidate. It refused the second candidate without a conflict choice; after a fresh integration preview, it supplied the current SHA-256 and explicitly selected the proposed content. The selected version integrated, both tasks reached `completed`, parent model history stayed untouched, and no tool calls were unresolved. Measured usage was 21,374 tokens (18,949 prompt, 2,425 completion). Report: `.acceptance/86478b0e-e5f2-488f-a477-fcfd81a08955/report.json`.

The acceptance attempt first exposed an LM Studio inventory edge case: `/api/v1/models` returned duplicate rows for one key and the later unloaded row hid the loaded instance, causing conservative admission to fail closed before any inference. Provider discovery now merges duplicate rows, retains every loaded instance, chooses the minimum advertised context and requires unanimous capability declarations; the regression checks both row orders. `tests/unit/test_exact_context.py` passes (25 tests), Ruff passes, and strict Pyright passes for `engine/xaa/providers/lm_studio.py` and `scripts`.

This verifies one real-model private-edit and explicit conflict-resolution path, using a scripted fixture reviewer with direct static checks and no WSL command validation. It does not establish autonomous reviewer parity, live human review UI, exact SDK token counting for this route, broad model reliability, or full Phase 4 completion. Hosted CI for the provider fix is pending. Overall project progress remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Native Tauri preference-panel acceptance (2026-11-07)

Added `scripts/native_memory_smoke.mjs` and a `test:native:memory` command for the actual Tauri desktop and bundled engine. The native UI acceptance saved a uniquely labeled local preference, edited it while preserving its record ID, searched for the updated value, deleted it, and confirmed the deleted record was absent from both the authenticated memory search and export endpoints. Evidence: `.acceptance/native-memory-panel-b4f76b10-6249-4c05-a276-8c3f50a6f56a/report.json`. It requested no inference and contacted no external services. The same scenario is now included in the Windows installer workflow.

The targeted native run passes locally on the development build. Combined with the existing real-model preference recall/deletion acceptance, this verifies the main saved-preference UI and retrieval-index lifecycle on one local Windows machine. It does not establish cross-account identity, sourced knowledge, multi-user isolation, or complete Phase 5 acceptance. Hosted validation subsequently passed in run [42420075422](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42420075422), including the native preference and sourced-knowledge UI acceptance step.

### User-sourced knowledge lifecycle and prompt context (2026-11-07)

Added source-backed knowledge as a distinct memory kind. The authenticated API and native Tauri panel now support user-entered content plus a source reference, edit-in-place with stable identity, FTS search, export, and delete; a schema-18-to-19 migration adds the nullable reference column without rewriting existing memories. The newest scoped sourced entries are recalled within a 4 KiB budget into parent and specialist prompts with an explicit unverified-data label and an instruction not to follow embedded instructions. Preferences retain their separate recall label and behavior. The app does not fetch, authenticate or independently verify the cited sources, and it does not automatically extract knowledge.

Focused storage/API/recall coverage passes (30 tests), including deletion from indexes, scope isolation, source-reference edits, untrusted prompt labeling and migration preservation. Ruff check/format, strict Pyright for `engine` and `scripts`, generated OpenAPI/desktop types, and TypeScript checks pass. Native Tauri acceptance passes locally at `.acceptance/native-memory-panel-5d57d720-4b53-4171-895d-29dc01c682ad/report.json`, covering preference and sourced-knowledge create/edit/search/delete plus authenticated search/export absence after deletion. The earlier installer run [42407583021](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42407583021) failed before UI checks because the smoke harness chose a different WebView debug port from the installer policy. The harness now honors `XAA_NATIVE_DEBUG_PORT`, and the subsequent source and installer run [42420075422](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42420075422) passed, including native preference and sourced-knowledge acceptance. Source verification is not broad knowledge-management or source-verification parity.

Full-suite rerun using a dedicated workspace basetemp passes: 747 passed, one skipped, with the repository's existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning. The initial run's 22 setup errors were caused by denied access to the shared system pytest temp root; no assertion failures remained in the isolated rerun.

### Phase 5–8 acceptance gate evidence (2026-11-07)

Phase 5 is supported by direct evidence across the live model and native desktop: the real-model preference test recalls an explicitly saved random label, then does not recall it after deletion; FTS search and export are empty after removal. Native Tauri acceptance verifies preference and sourced-knowledge create/edit/search/delete and index cleanup. The live skill lifecycle test evaluates two versions on representative ASCII/Unicode fixtures, activates and reuses version 2, rolls back and reuses version 1, and confirms deprecation blocks new selection; all fixture bytes remain unchanged and SDK/HTTP token counts agree. Evidence: `.acceptance/2ef31a86-c0f9-424f-93df-56a9637fdac0/report.json`, `.acceptance/native-memory-panel-5d57d720-4b53-4171-895d-29dc01c682ad/report.json`, and `.acceptance/6ca2d0ab-c427-4fe2-a020-36097897fdd7/report.json`.

Phases 6 and 7 also have evidence for their stated gates: the native onboarding flow connects LM Studio, selects a workspace, runs a real read-only task and completes explicit review (`releases/evidence/native-onboarding.json`); MCP stdio/HTTP fixture suites verify output trust labels, cancellation and disconnect handling without leaving unresolved intents (`architecture/mcp.md`). Phase 8's scheduler restart dispatch test proves one queued run is recovered exactly once; named-zone gap/fold and reopen/idempotence tests cover daylight-saving and persistence; Telegram duplicate-update and ambiguous-send restart tests preserve at-most-once external effects (`progress.md`, 2026-11-05; `releases/native-acceptance.md`). These gates are verified on the documented Windows/fake-server/model fixtures. Hosted run [42420075422](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42420075422) passed both jobs and native memory acceptance. Phase 10's installer gate also passes on a clean GitHub Windows runner: run [42422057691](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42422057691) installed, upgraded, booted, recovered and uninstalled the unsigned package. The product is not release-complete: broad Phase 9 feature coverage, live Telegram/email, full Hermes skill/plugin parity, cross-account identity, application licensing and package copyright review remain open. This phase-gate evidence does not imply full objective completion.

### CI turnaround (2026-11-07)

The source and Windows installer jobs use the same checkout and do not exchange artifacts, so the installer job no longer waits for the source job. Hosted run [42422057691](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42422057691) passed both jobs concurrently in 6m 03s, versus 9m 53s serially in [42421255799](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42421255799), saving 3m 50s (about 39%) on these two passing runs. The workflow now skips docs-only pushes and pull requests; future documentation updates will not consume a full Windows build. Code and workflow changes still trigger both validation jobs.

Hosted run [42467978003](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42467978003) failed before source validation when `setup-uv` received HTTP 504 responses, and the installer exhausted its three bounded Tauri NSIS download attempts on HTTP 504. This is an external download failure, not a source test failure. Added a successful-build-only Actions cache for Tauri's NSIS tools, keyed by the locked Tauri dependencies, to avoid repeating that download on later installer runs. The next hosted run must confirm the cache restore and both jobs.

Hosted run [42468445590](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42468445590) restored `setup-uv` and npm/Cargo caches and passed the full Windows source suite, but installer packaging failed because Tauri's NSIS helper DLL download returned HTTP 504 on all retries. Runs [42469180312](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42469180312) and [42469538103](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42469538103) then failed in both jobs during `setup-uv` installation after repeated HTTP 504 responses. Run [42469909739](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42469909739) was rejected before job creation due to an unsupported runner-context expression in job-level environment; no checks ran for that commit. The corrected workflow seeds Tauri's cache from the 2.4 MiB compressed official toolset, verifies archive SHA-256 and Tauri-required executable/plugin SHA-1 values, and saves the cache before packaging. It replaces setup-uv's GitHub Release download with the exact `uv==0.13.0` Windows wheel from PyPI pinned by SHA-256, and caches both uv's package data and the complete Tauri toolset. Hosted run [42470012376](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42470012376) passed both Windows jobs: source lint, typing, contracts, client/browser tests and full Python suite, plus installer build, frozen-engine checks, native restart/memory/specialist acceptance, and install/upgrade/uninstall smoke. Both uv and NSIS cache seed steps succeeded. The next run should confirm cache hits and reduced setup time. NSIS provenance and the archive's included COPYING file are recorded in the provisional notices.

### Live local-model image input (2026-11-07)

The current LM Studio model inventory advertises vision for installed `qwen/qwen3.5-9b`. Added `scripts/live_image_diagnostic.py`, which builds a synthetic PNG without third-party image libraries and sends it through the normal run/artifact/coordinator path. The real model correctly answered “2 red circles”; the run reached `verifying` with no tool calls or unresolved actions, and the restored image matched the exact 372-byte source SHA-256. Reproducible summary: `docs/releases/evidence/live-image-vision.json`; local reports are in `.acceptance/67fc99e2-ce41-4ff8-ac33-8da3783a3b69/` and the failed first attempt `.acceptance/c5475af7-65cb-4755-a39e-db30800405fc/`. The first attempt hit the 128-token output limit during model reasoning and was correctly blocked; the diagnostic uses 512 tokens for the successful run. Ruff and Pyright pass. LM Studio was off before the check and has been stopped again; the model is unloaded. This verifies one synthetic visual task on one model only. General visual accuracy, live document reading, OCR, audio and other model families remain open; overall release estimate remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Live local-model PDF reading (2026-11-07)

Added `scripts/live_document_diagnostic.py`, which generates a one-page PDF with a random marker, passes it through the production PDF text extractor and persisted document-artifact path, and asks the real local Qwen3.5 9B to return that marker. The model returned it exactly; the run reached `verifying` without tools, both the source PDF and extracted-text artifacts were preserved, and the extracted text restored exactly. Summary evidence: `docs/releases/evidence/live-document-reading.json`; local report: `.acceptance/3c5649db-50b8-4cc2-b433-7208c5499de7/report.json`. Ruff, Pyright and PDF parser smoke check pass. LM Studio was stopped again and the model unloaded after the run. This verifies one text-based PDF case on one model; OCR, scanned documents, office formats and broad document accuracy remain open.

### Native desktop media input acceptance (2026-11-07)

Added `scripts/native_media_smoke.mjs` and `npm run test:native:media`. The harness builds the development Tauri app, starts it with an isolated profile and workspace, connects to the actual desktop window, verifies the local model's vision capability and harmless tool-use diagnostic, then submits a generated image, PDF, DOCX and XLSX through the UI file inputs. On `qwen/qwen3.5-9b`, the native runs counted two red objects and read random markers from all three document types; each reached `verifying` with no tool calls, restored source artifacts by digest, retained extracted-text markers and left the workspace unchanged. Reports and screenshots: `.acceptance/native-media-51d5a724-1948-4f3c-8141-600cb404bfc9/`; durable summary: `docs/releases/evidence/native-media-ui.json`. The model was unloaded before the native window closed; the desktop exited with code 0 and its engine endpoint stopped. LM Studio server was stopped and no models remain loaded. Hosted run [42426051406](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42426051406) passed both Windows jobs for the preceding native-media-harness commit, and [42427323411](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42427323411) passed both for DOCX input. DOCX and XLSX extraction read bounded XML parts without writing archive members to disk; DTD/entity declarations and external worksheet relationships are rejected. Spreadsheet extraction covers shared strings, inline strings and cached cell values, not formula evaluation or workbook formatting. All extracted content follows the existing untrusted-text artifact/prompt path. This verifies one synthetic image and one example each of PDF, DOCX and XLSX on one model, not broad media accuracy, OCR, scanned documents, other model families or independent review acceptance. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

### Scanned PDF OCR and native media acceptance (2026-11-07)

Scanned PDF pages are now rendered with bundled PDFium and sent to the selected vision model through LM Studio's native chat endpoint with reasoning disabled. OCR uses the user's request as a relevance hint, never acts on instructions shown in the page, and becomes untrusted document text for the normal agent loop. OCR input/output tokens are journaled and included in run totals. A configured run token budget must allow a conservative 4,608 tokens per scanned page before OCR begins. Original PDF, extracted OCR text and rendered page PNG stay checksum-verified artifacts; page PNGs are not redundantly sent through the main tool loop. Bounds remain four pages, 1,600 pixels per side, 2.5 megapixels per page, 2 MiB per PNG and 6 MiB total.

Actual Windows Tauri acceptance passed a raster-only PDF with an unseen random code on `google/gemma-4-e4b`. The OCR call used 520 tokens; the full run reported 1,871 tokens, zero unmeasured calls and no outstanding reservations. The same complete native run also passed a PNG count, text PDF, DOCX and XLSX marker, with zero tool calls, preserved artifact digests, unchanged workspace, unloaded model and clean engine shutdown. Durable summary: `releases/evidence/native-media-ui.json`; full local report and screenshots: `.acceptance/native-media-a50dce5d-bf05-41c4-8706-aa05868f2271/`. Qwen3.5 9B returned inconsistent OCR on the synthetic page and some attempts blocked in the later agent turn; vision metadata does not guarantee OCR accuracy. The Qwen limitation remains open for further model-specific work.

Verification for the OCR-usage follow-up: 63 focused tests pass with one existing Starlette/httpx deprecation warning, and Ruff, strict Pyright, TypeScript, API contract check, native development build and five-format native media acceptance pass. The immediately preceding version passed 772 tests with one skipped; hosted full-suite and installer checks are queued for this follow-up commit. Overall release completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired); this capability slice does not close broad Hermes parity or release gates.

### Loopback WAV transcription input (2026-11-08)

Added a first voice-input path to the desktop objective form. A run may attach one PCM WAV (4 MiB maximum, 10 minutes maximum) and provide an OpenAI-compatible `/v1/audio/transcriptions` service URL. The API accepts only numeric IPv4/IPv6 loopback addresses, disables proxy environment variables and redirects, bounds the response, validates WAV structure, and treats the transcript as untrusted document text. The original recording and transcript are retained as run artifacts; the desktop can download the WAV. No ASR dependency, model download, credential, or cloud fallback was added. This slice depends on the user installing and running a compatible local speech service; a native desktop and real local-service acceptance is still needed.

Five focused transcription/API tests pass, including URL restrictions, WAV bounds, multipart request shape, redirect rejection, API artifact persistence, and WAV download. The API integration test now starts a real loopback HTTP transcription fixture, receives the multipart WAV through the production client, and verifies retained source/transcript artifacts. A Playwright desktop workflow selects a WAV, enters a loopback service URL, and verifies the exact audio/transcription fields sent in the run request; this spec is included in the Windows source CI browser step. Ruff, strict Pyright on changed engine modules, TypeScript typechecking, generated API contract checks, and a production desktop build pass. Live ASR accuracy, other audio codecs, and broad voice parity remain open. Native UI-to-API voice acceptance with a loopback transcription fixture is now verified below. Overall project progress remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

Environment check: no service is listening on the desktop's default transcription port 8000, and `faster_whisper`, `whisper`, `vosk` and `speech_recognition` are not installed in the project environment. LM Studio is available, but its current model inventory did not identify a dedicated ASR service. A live transcription/model-accuracy acceptance therefore cannot run until a compatible local service is configured; no model or dependency was downloaded automatically.

### OpenAI-compatible provider adapter core (2026-11-08)

Added `OpenAICompatibleProvider` for explicitly supplied HTTPS server origins or HTTP loopback origins. It discovers `/v1/models`, uses the OpenAI Chat Completions tool-call and streaming contract, reports provider token usage, and sends the current `max_completion_tokens` field. It disables environment proxies and redirects, keeps bearer credentials in memory for the client, and returns only generic bounded errors. It fails closed for exact SDK token counting, model load/unload, and scanned-PDF OCR, which require provider-specific support. The token parameter follows the [official Chat Completions API reference](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create).

The initial adapter implementation passed 44 focused provider/limit/metadata/configuration tests, including compatible model discovery, harmless tool round trip, streaming assembly, output-token parameter selection, and explicit unsupported-feature behavior. Provider profile APIs, vault-backed token management, desktop selection, consent copy, and namespaced routing are now implemented as described below. A live second provider and packaged native acceptance remain open.

### Selectable OpenAI-compatible provider routing (2026-11-08)

Wired the optional adapter through authenticated provider settings and durable model routing. Provider endpoints and selected model IDs are stored in the existing SQLite provider profile table; API tokens use a separate Windows Credential Manager profile. The desktop displays a clear remote-data notice before model selection. LM Studio remains the default, and selecting a model clears the other profile selection so reopening settings restores one unambiguous choice. Provider IDs use `openai-compatible::` namespacing; the existing `runs.model` and specialist route fields preserve the chosen adapter identity across restart and settings changes. Coordinator, specialists, diagnostics, streaming, token counting and scheduled runs now dispatch by that identifier. Unavailable optional services do not prevent local model discovery. Exact SDK token counting, provider model lifecycle and scanned-PDF OCR remain explicitly unsupported by this adapter.

Evidence: 24 focused provider client/router/profile tests pass, including an API-created compatible-model run that discovers and selects the namespaced model, completes a streaming coordinator inference, persists provider identity, and reaches completion through explicit user review. A separate 59-test API, coordinator, specialist and schedule suite passes; two Playwright settings/execution specs pass, including optional endpoint, model selection, secure-token UI and compatible diagnostic. Ruff, strict Pyright on changed engine modules, TypeScript typecheck, production desktop build, OpenAPI export/check and generated-type check pass. The end-to-end run uses a deterministic HTTP fixture; no real second provider was configured, so external service interoperability, live remote authentication and native packaged-desktop acceptance remain open. Project-wide completion remains not quantified (historical figure retired).

## Transitive specialist dependency failure acceptance (2026-11-08)

Expanded the task-claim regression to exercise a failed prerequisite with two queued downstream levels. A claim poll marks both descendants blocked with the public `dependency_failed` reason, emits one durable block event for each, and a repeated poll leaves those events unchanged. The focused task-claim suite passes (9 tests). This strengthens the Phase 4 failed-dependency gate at the storage boundary; it does not add a new dependency behavior or establish broad concurrent failure/recovery reliability.

## Windows junction containment acceptance (2026-11-08)

Verified the existing workspace snapshot guard against an actual Windows directory junction: `capture_workspace` rejects the reparse point before following it and the outside sentinel bytes remain unchanged. The complete focused snapshot suite passes (15 tests), including Unicode/binary preservation, bounds, manifest integrity, opened-handle containment and the junction case; Ruff and `git diff --check` pass. This proves containment for the isolated snapshot capture path on this Windows host. It does not prove every workspace read/write/search/delete or task command path against junctions; each path still needs scoped coverage.

## File-operation junction containment (2026-11-08)

Added a real Windows junction regression for `workspace.read_file` and `workspace.write_file`. Both direct attempts through the junction are rejected before a tool intent is recorded; the external sentinel remains byte-identical. Together with the existing snapshot, listing and search junction cases, the combined executor/snapshot/search/listing suite passes (35 tests) on this Windows host. Ruff passes for the new test and `git diff --check` passes. This verifies these four path families only; deletion, task-worker operations, Git and command backends still require their own scoped containment acceptance.

The junction fixture was extended to cover reviewed deletion as well as reading and editing: all three operations reject the external target before creating a tool intent, and its bytes remain unchanged. The five scoped suites (executor, snapshot, search, listing and task claims) pass together: 44 tests.

## Native voice workflow acceptance (2026-11-08)

Added `scripts/native_voice_smoke.mjs` and `npm run test:native:voice`. A real native Tauri app submitted a generated PCM WAV to a temporary transcription fixture bound only to 127.0.0.1; the fixture verified the multipart body contained the exact audio and returned a randomized code. Live LM Studio Qwen3.5 9B inference then returned that code even though the objective did not contain it. The acceptance independently downloaded and byte-compared the source WAV and transcript artifacts. The run reached `verifying` with no model tool call. Evidence: `.acceptance/native-voice-42e84bf2-09b8-4cf1-9a83-4316f85ff0f0/report.json`. This verifies the installed UI/API/local-service wiring and that transcript content reaches the model as reference material; it does not verify speech recognition accuracy because the transcription server was a deterministic fixture. A local ASR service is not running here, so live ASR integration, other audio codecs and broad voice capability remain open.

The first native harness attempt raced engine startup; bounded readiness polling fixed it. Two subsequent small-model runs reached `blocked` (one on context size, one on the 128-token output cap), without invalid audio handling or data loss. The successful 9B run used a 512-token cap. All `.acceptance/` reports are preserved.

## Progress estimate correction (2026-11-08)

Earlier `not quantified (historical figure retired)` entries were carried forward from an older rough estimate and not recalculated as implementation continued. They are stale, not a current measured completion value. A percentage is withheld until the objective's phase gates are audited and weighted explicitly; this prevents the old number from implying no recent progress or a precise current measurement.

## Task-worker and command staging junction containment (2026-11-08)

Added Windows junction regressions through the actual frozen-worker entry boundary for specialist private-snapshot initialization and pre-command workspace hashing. Both reject the junction with a redacted `PermissionError`; no private snapshot is published, no execution reservation is created, and outside sentinel bytes remain unchanged. The task-workspace and task-command suites pass together (29 tests), including their existing permission, private-snapshot, recovery and command-output cases. This proves source capture is rejected before specialist/command work proceeds; it does not replace direct auditing of every lower-level Git and WSL worker path.

## Git junction output containment (2026-11-08)

Added a real Windows junction regression for Git status and diff. When the selected repository contains a junction to a sibling directory, neither the outside file name nor its private contents appear in the returned Git excerpts; the outside file remains unchanged. The full Git tool suite passes (3 tests), with Ruff, formatting and `git diff --check` clean. This verifies the bounded Git tool's returned output on Windows; it does not claim Git performs no filesystem metadata access through every reparse type.

## Command-controller junction launch gate (2026-11-08)

Added an end-to-end `CommandController` regression using a real NTFS junction to an external sibling directory. The owned snapshot worker rejects the workspace before the transport's `run` method is called; the controller cleans up and reconciles its already-created reservation, no execution launch event is recorded, and the external sentinel remains unchanged. All 12 command-controller tests pass, as do Ruff, formatting and `git diff --check`. The reservation is deliberately durable before snapshot capture, so this verifies rejection before command launch and successful reservation cleanup, not rejection before reservation. The test uses a fixture transport and does not claim a live WSL launch attempt.

## Real WSL command-controller junction acceptance (2026-11-08)

Extended `scripts/command_controller_diagnostic.py` and reran it against the installed `Ubuntu-24.04` WSL distribution. With a real Windows directory junction in the selected workspace, the owned snapshot worker rejects capture before `execution.launched`; the already durable reservation is reconciled, no tool call remains unresolved, and the outside sentinel remains byte-identical. The same acceptance also completes its actual restricted WSL command and cancellation/recovery cases. Evidence: `.acceptance/2a8717e3-9a71-4999-8426-25b8e06d6edc/report.json`. Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass for the diagnostic. The Windows account cannot create a directory symbolic link (`WinError 1314`, recorded in the report), so that reparse type and broader distro coverage remain open.

## Require current-project validation after specialist integration (2026-11-08)

Final run review now treats any authorized specialist integration with one or more actual writes as a project edit. When the run has a restricted-command grant, the reviewer must select successful validation evidence whose snapshot still matches the current integrated project before accepting the run. Direct workspace edits retain the existing requirement; no-op integration choices do not create a new validation requirement. This closes the gap where the team validated each private snapshot but could accept the combined project without a final check. All 13 run-review tests and 49 related run-review, task-review and API tests pass; both final-review browser tests pass; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Validation command selection and execution remain explicitly human initiated; no check is invented or run automatically.

## Additional project validation ecosystems (2026-11-08)

Expanded manifest-based validation suggestions with Maven and Gradle test commands using offline mode, and Composer only when `composer.json` explicitly declares a `test` script. Each nested manifest produces a path-specific argv; malformed Composer JSON and packages without a test script produce no Composer suggestion. A sole suggestion runs under the existing restricted command grant, while ambiguous selections remain user-chosen. Five focused validation-suggestion tests pass, including exact argv and monorepo coverage; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42470921172](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42470921172) passed both Windows jobs, including 841 Python tests, desktop browser checks, frozen-engine/restart diagnostics, installer packaging and install/upgrade/uninstall acceptance. uv and NSIS caches restored successfully. Toolchain presence and restored dependencies remain the user's environment responsibility, and broader ecosystems remain open.

## Swift Package Manager validation suggestions (2026-11-08)

The bounded project scan now recognizes each `Package.swift` and suggests `swift test`, adding `--package-path` for nested packages. This lets the existing one-command validation flow cover Swift packages while preserving explicit user selection for ambiguous monorepos and human review of results. The unit suite also checks root/nested argv and includes Swift in the distinct-manifest monorepo regression. Six focused unit tests and all four final-review browser tests pass; Ruff, formatting, Pyright and git diff checks are clean. Hosted run 42472350223 passed both jobs, including the Swift-specific browser assertion. Swift availability and package dependencies in the restricted WSL environment remain prerequisites.

## Require declared JavaScript and Composer test scripts (2026-11-08)

The manifest scanner suggests `npm test` only when `package.json` contains a non-empty string at `scripts.test`. Composer accepts either a non-empty `scripts.test` string or a non-empty array of command strings, matching the supported form in the [Composer scripts documentation](https://getcomposer.org/doc/articles/scripts.md). Missing, blank, malformed and wrongly typed entries produce no command; nested valid packages retain their package-specific argv. This prevents a guessed command from being treated as project test evidence. Seven focused tests, Ruff, formatting and strict Pyright pass. Hosted validation is pending; each command still runs only inside the existing restricted project-validation path, with successful evidence requiring human review.

### JavaScript package-manager-aware validation suggestions (2026-11-08)

JavaScript manifests with a declared `scripts.test` now select the command runner from `packageManager`, or from a unique nearest npm, pnpm, Yarn or Bun lockfile when no manager is declared. Conflicting lockfile signals produce no suggestion rather than guessing; projects with no manager metadata retain the prior npm default. Nested package command paths are preserved. Eight focused tests pass, including all four managers, lockfile selection and ambiguity refusal; Ruff, strict Pyright and `git diff --check` pass. Hosted run [42483743736](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42483743736) passed both Windows jobs, including the full Python suite and installed-package acceptance

## Safe restart after a specialist stops before its first reply (2026-11-08)

Task recovery now allows an owner to resume from the assignment itself when no model reply, tool result or tool call was persisted. Specialist tool execution begins only after the model response is saved, so this case has no saved external action to repeat; the previous inference still counts against task and shared limits. If a shared token ceiling is configured, any unmeasured inference blocks this restart. The recovery panel explains when resume restarts from the objective. Fifteen focused recovery/claim tests pass, including authenticated API summary behavior, the owner requeue decision and token-budget refusal; both recovery browser scenarios pass, along with Ruff, formatting, Pyright, API contract checks, generated-type checks, TypeScript and `git diff --check`. Hosted run [42485397733](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42485397733) passed both Windows jobs and installer acceptance.

## Reconcile a saved specialist command after worker loss (2026-11-08)

When a specialist dies after reserving a workspace command but before persisting the model tool result, recovery now records the model call ID in the durable execution reservation. The blocked-task summary exposes only matching task-owned command identities; the authenticated reconciliation route seals the saved WSL execution, recovers an exact journal result if durable output hashes match, otherwise attaches the interrupted-command failure result. It never relaunches the command. The desktop exposes this owner-triggered action and refreshes the recovery summary. Focused claim/command/API tests pass (50), both task-recovery browser scenarios pass, Ruff/formatting/Pyright/API schema export/generated types/TypeScript pass. Hosted run [42486783429](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42486783429) passed both Windows jobs. Other unresolved tool effects still block continuation and require their own reconciliation paths; this closes only saved task-scoped WSL command recovery.

### Recover a command result saved just before specialist loss (2026-11-08)

Closed the second command-recovery crash boundary: a completed/failed task-owned command may already have a durable result even though `task.tool.result` was not appended. Recovery summaries now locate that result through the task-owned tool row and reservation mapping, and the API attaches it directly without reconnecting to WSL; only still-intended commands need backend sealing/reconciliation. Added a regression that executes a private task command, fences the worker after its command result is durable, verifies the model call is offered for recovery despite zero unresolved tool calls, then attaches the saved result exactly once. The test exposed and fixed an invalid SQL assumption about `tasks.blocked_reason`; recovery reason is event-sourced. The combined source and Windows installer run [42488095331](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42488095331) passed. An authenticated API-route regression verifies the attach decision, and broader extension to committed private filesystem results remains under local validation.

## Attach committed task filesystem results after specialist loss (2026-11-08)

Specialist recovery now detects completed task-scoped workspace file and directory reads/listings plus isolated snapshot reads, listings, writes, creates, and deletes whose durable `tool_calls` result exists but whose `task.tool.result` event was not persisted. Every new specialist call uses a deterministic run/task/model-call journal ID, and task-scoped tool intents now persist their task ownership. Recovery checks that binding, operation, saved permission, and exact pending model call before an authenticated owner action attaches the existing result; it never repeats the file operation, and duplicate or mismatched attachments are refused. A read-only-worker regression verifies the original fixture bytes remain unchanged, while the private-write and command cases use their existing API coverage. The combined claim/command/API/workspace suite passes 76 tests; all three task-recovery browser scenarios pass; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API schema/generated types, TypeScript, and `git diff --check` pass. Command recovery passed hosted Windows source and installer acceptance in [run 42488095331](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42488095331); the broader filesystem-result extension awaits hosted validation. Reviewer dependency-artifact reads and external effects need separate recovery handling.

### Resolve interrupted read-only specialist operations without inventing content (2026-11-08)

An owner may now reconcile a saved but unfinished specialist `workspace.read_file` or `workspace.list_directory` intent by sealing it with a `read_only` `interrupted_read` failure result, then attaching that explicit error to the pending model call. The response says no previous contents are known and the specialist can request a fresh read later. The path refuses interrupted writes and any intent with execution evidence; completed read/list results continue to attach their exact saved data. A parameterized test exercises both completed and unfinished read recovery through the storage/API boundary, including unchanged source bytes and exact error code. The 76-test claim/command/API/workspace group, three task-recovery browser scenarios, Ruff, formatting, Pyright, API contract and generated type checks, TypeScript, and diff check pass. Hosted validation is pending.

### Seal interrupted reviewer dependency reads (2026-11-08)

Reviewer `review_dependency_file` and `review_dependency_output` calls now use the same no-content-safe recovery path. Since these operations read immutable saved dependency evidence without a separate tool journal, the owner can attach an explicit `read_only` `interrupted_read` result to the exact pending reviewer call. No dependency contents are assumed and the read is never replayed by recovery. Only blocked reviewer tasks with the exact pending read call qualify. Parameterized tests cover both tool types. The combined claim/command/API/workspace suite passes 76 tests; all three task-recovery browser scenarios pass; Ruff, formatting, strict Pyright, API schema/generated type checks, TypeScript, and diff checks pass. Hosted validation is pending.

## Specialist research with inherited web approvals (2026-11-08)

Researcher specialists may now use `web.search` when the run has explicit Brave Search approval and `web.fetch` only for the exact public hosts already approved for that run. Plan validation refuses web permissions outside those saved approvals; the specialist exposes only its delegated tool permissions, uses the existing bounded readers, persists each result under the deterministic task/model-call journal identity, and labels retrieved content untrusted. The recovery panel can attach an already saved web result or seal an unfinished read as `interrupted_read` without replaying it. Focused specialist, task-command/claim, web-fetch and web-search suites pass (57 tests); task recovery browser scenarios pass (3); API contract/type generation, TypeScript, Ruff, formatting, Pyright and diff checks pass. Hosted run 42491008441 passed both Windows jobs for the preceding interrupted-reviewer-read commit; hosted validation of this delegation slice is pending. Browser screenshot delegation, broader web/MCP specialist capabilities and overall Hermes parity remain open.

## Specialist read-only browser access (2026-11-08)

Specialist tasks may now use the disposable, read-only browser when the run explicitly approved browser access and exact hostnames, and the saved task permission includes `web.browser.read`. The tool schema omits screenshot capture, and the executor rejects screenshot requests, keeping this delegated operation within the durable read-result recovery path. Model-facing content remains untrusted. Search, fetch and browser results use task-scoped journals; recovery attaches saved results or seals unfinished reads without replay. The focused specialist, task-command/claim and web reader suites pass 62 tests (after adding browser dispatch and recovery coverage); task recovery browser tests pass 3; client, contract, TypeScript, Ruff, Pyright and diff checks are current. Hosted run 42492721215 passed its Windows source job but failed installer upgrade acceptance: the installed desktop hash matched the 0.1.0 predecessor, while the engine matched 0.1.1. Screenshot delegation and broader Phase 7 compatibility remain open.

## Trace stale desktop binary during hosted upgrade acceptance (2026-11-08)

Hosted run 42492721215 exposed that the 0.1.0 desktop binary survived the 0.1.1 installer while the engine upgraded. The diagnostic attempt in run 42493833862 appended `/LOG` after NSIS `/D`; NSIS requires `/D` to be the final argument, so that run stopped before exercising the upgrade. The invalid argument was removed. Hosted run [42494596003](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42494596003) passed the clean predecessor install and 0.1.0→0.1.1 upgrade, verifying current desktop and engine hashes and preserved backup data. This closes the installer replacement issue for this hosted Windows fixture; broader clean-user and migration coverage remains open.

## Delegate and recover MCP read tools in specialist tasks (2026-11-08)

Specialist plans may grant an exact qualified MCP name only when its persisted user classification is `read`; write tools cannot enter a specialist scope. At runtime the tool must still be connected, read-classified and schema-matched. The specialist saves its MCP intent, tool arguments, read classification and schema digest before calling, then persists the result under the task/model-call identity. Lost-worker recovery attaches a committed result or seals an unfinished read as `interrupted_read` with no assumed content; it does not replay the MCP call. The recovery panel identifies the exact MCP tool without exposing arguments. Focused specialist, task recovery and MCP stdio suites pass 37 tests; all three task-recovery browser scenarios pass; client tests (15), Ruff, strict Pyright, API/OpenAPI and generated TypeScript checks pass. Hosted Windows run [42496696548](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42496696548) passed both jobs. MCP write delegation, broader protocol/authentication compatibility and external-effect recovery remain open.


## Discord private-DM reply outbox (2026-11-08)

Completed the outbound half of the existing private-DM Discord adapter. Paired-DM runs now queue their saved terminal answer through a durable outbox. The adapter records `sending` before REST delivery, stores Discord's returned message ID on success, and classifies definitive rejection separately from ambiguous outcomes. Startup converts interrupted sends to `unknown`; the desktop requires the owner to check the destination before marking delivery or retrying, and caps manual retries at two. Sends disable parsed mentions, cap content at Discord's 2,000-character limit, and use Discord's nonce deduplication only as a short-window duplicate guard. Schema migration, outbox state, crash reconciliation, retry bound, REST request shape, rate-limit/server-error classification, API confirmation, and browser recovery UI have focused coverage. Thirteen Discord/API tests, the Discord channel browser workflow, Ruff, strict Pyright, OpenAPI export/generated TypeScript, TypeScript, and `git diff --check` pass locally. Hosted run [42504107642](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42504107642) passed both Windows source and installer jobs, including the full Python suite, frozen recovery diagnostics, and install/upgrade/boot/uninstall acceptance. Live Discord interoperability remains open; guilds, media, voice and cross-channel identity remain open.

It failed its own assertion because the researcher repeated the same read-only operation; both saved results had the exact fixture SHA-256. `scripts/live_plan_dispatch_diagnostic.py` now allows repeated reads only when every saved result matches the expected text and is non-truncated, and requires the candidate to cite those saved result journals. Fresh retries preserved their distinct failures: Qwen3.5 9B provider timeout before plan creation (`.acceptance/f23af1d7-87a9-4fa5-8b06-411c557cd87c/report.json`); Qwen3 0.6B used its full 512-token diagnostic completion without calling the harmless tool (`.acceptance/4363eaec-fd4e-448c-ab56-cb67b7dd088d/report.json`); Qwen2.5 0.5B passed the diagnostic but its run blocked at context admission (`.acceptance/def3194c-5958-43eb-a8f8-80c147b2f3ee/report.json`; LM Studio continued to report that preloaded instance at 4,096 tokens). ## Preserve desktop close behavior through uninstall (2026-11-08)

The native installer acceptance now starts from the default `keep_running` setting, saves `exit_on_close` in an isolated user profile, uninstalls the packaged app and verifies the preference remains while app binaries are removed. Hosted Windows run [42508906186](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42508906186) passed both the source suite and installer job with this check. The test leaves the real runner profile unchanged. The separate migration path for older user profiles remains open.
## Explicit cross-channel identity links (2026-11-08)

Added an authenticated desktop flow for the owner to link two already-authorized identities from different channels and choose which session history is shared. The owner can add authorized accounts from additional channels to an existing group. Existing history on other accounts stays separate; future ingress from either identity appends to the selected session. Confirmed unlink atomically creates separate empty sessions, clears the link, retains the old shared transcript locally and records an audit event. Telegram, Email and Discord adapter fixtures prove linked inputs use the shared session; API/storage and desktop browser tests cover authorization, channel separation, unlink behavior and schema 20→22 migration. Focused verification: 43 Python tests pass, Pyright reports zero issues, TypeScript typechecking passes, generated OpenAPI/desktop types are current, and the channel browser flow passes. These checks do not make model calls or claim live service interoperability. One identity per channel is supported and existing groups cannot be merged; other existing channel IDs, broader channel delivery and live interoperability remain open. Details: [cross-channel identity linking](channels/identity-linking.md).

## NSIS opt-in removal UI process tracking (2026-11-08)

Hosted run [42517727949](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42517727949) passed source checks, installer build, install/boot/upgrade and default data-preservation acceptance. The opt-in removal UI check failed before locating the checkbox; its report records that the launched uninstaller process exited with code 0 after 0.31 seconds. [NSIS documentation](https://nsis.sourceforge.io/Docs/Chapter3.html) explains that uninstallers normally copy themselves to a temporary directory and execute from there. The smoke now passes the documented final `_?=<install-dir>` option so the interactive UI remains on the process being observed. This is a diagnosis-backed harness change; actual selection and deletion are still unverified until hosted Windows acceptance passes.

Run [42518633016](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42518633016) confirmed the `_?=` change kept the uninstaller alive for the full 60-second UI search, but the checkbox and button were still not found. The smoke now enumerates all top-level windows belonging to that process and will include window/control text, classes and visibility in its report after a timeout. This gathers the missing evidence for the next fix; opt-in deletion remains unverified.

Run [42518633016](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42518633016) exposed a visible NSIS dialog titled `Xyst AI Agent Uninstall` owned by the interactive uninstaller process, but no checkbox/button was matched. The initial nested JSON report was depth-truncated, so the smoke now writes a flat diagnostic entry for each top-level window and child control, including handle, control ID, class, visibility and text, and serializes the report at depth 8. This is to identify the actual Windows control tree before further UI automation changes.

The full control tree from run [42520417549](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42520417549) shows the checkbox caption exactly as expected and the confirmation button caption as `&Uninstall`; the ampersand is NSIS's keyboard accelerator marker. The smoke now matches both `&Uninstall` and `Uninstall`. This should allow the checkbox to be selected and the actual removal hook to execute, but only the next hosted report can verify that behavior.

Run [42521115574](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42521115574) selected the actual checkbox (`explicit_delete_selected: true`) but the uninstaller did not exit after 120 seconds. The acceptance now captures the post-click NSIS window/control tree and checks whether the two app-data directories and both installed binaries still exist at timeout. The user data deletion claim remains unverified until those post-click states and a clean completion are observed.

The post-click report from [42521987816](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42521987816) confirms the removal hook worked: both `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\XAA` and the bundle-ID config directory were absent, and both installed binaries were removed. NSIS was waiting at `Uninstallation Complete` with a visible `&Close` button, which the harness had not clicked. The smoke now clicks that final button and verifies process exit; the hosted run is still needed to verify the end-to-end pass.

## Windows uninstall data-removal release gate verified (2026-11-08)

Added `scripts/browser_cancel_smoke.py` and a Windows workflow step for live Edge cancellation and timeout during HTTPBin `/delay/10` navigation. Cancellation remains journaled as `cancelled`; a deadline now journals an actionable `read_failed` message instead of an empty timeout string. Extended `scripts/frozen_browser_diagnostic.py` to first verify packaged Edge navigation and screenshot output, then launch a separate HTTPBin `/delay/10` request through the one-file engine and terminate only that executable's process tree using Windows `taskkill /T /F`. ## Preserve browser cancellation results through the authenticated API (2026-11-08)

The authenticated `/runs/{id}/cancel` and `/pause` handlers now stop an active coordinator before transitioning the run and clearing its lease. That lets the browser worker persist its cancellation result while the lease is still valid. Added `scripts/browser_api_cancel_smoke.py`: a deterministic fake provider requests an approved real Edge read, then the smoke cancels it through the authenticated API and verifies both the terminal run state and durable `browser.run` result. Local evidence: `.acceptance/browser-api-cancel-1794178779/report.json` (379 ms cleanup). No model inference was used. The smoke is wired into Windows source CI with a run-scoped artifact; hosted verification is pending. The first hosted run exposed a specialist-state ordering regression: 897 tests passed and one API cancellation test expected active specialist tasks to be marked `cancelled`. The API now records specialist stop state before stopping the parent worker, while leaving the parent run lease valid for tool-result journaling. The specialist cancellation/pause regression tests pass locally (2), the API suite passes (33), and the API browser acceptance passes again with 358 ms cleanup (`.acceptance/browser-api-cancel-1794179441/report.json`). Strict Pyright and Ruff pass. Hosted full-suite rerun is pending.

Hosted rerun [42533904823](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42533904823) passed both Windows jobs after the specialist-state ordering fix. The source job passed all 898 Python tests, Ruff, strict Pyright, browser acceptances, and restart diagnostics; the installer job passed native, frozen-worker, upgrade, boot and uninstall acceptance. The new authenticated API/Edge smoke persisted a completed `browser.run` cancellation result and finished cleanup in 169 ms; downloaded report: `.acceptance/hosted-browser-api-cancel-42533904823/report.json`. No model inference was used.

## Check Edge cannot bypass the managed proxy for loopback targets (2026-11-08)

Added `scripts/browser_proxy_bypass_smoke.py` to hold a local listener on HTTPS port 443 and send real Microsoft Edge to the explicitly approved hostname `localhost`. The smoke requires the worker to fail and the listener to receive zero direct connections, exercising the Chromium `<-loopback>` proxy override together with the worker's public-DNS guard. Local result: `.acceptance/browser-proxy-bypass-485eabea2ee94dce9be4759ffd88678b/report.json` (zero loopback connections). Windows source CI now runs and uploads this acceptance; hosted evidence is pending. No model inference was used.

Full Python suite and installer acceptance also passed. No model inference was used. Other internal address classes remain covered by resolver tests; broader address/redirect coverage is still open.

## Stream bounded WSL command output into durable run events (2026-11-09)

The selected WSL manager now emits newline-framed JSON output deltas as it reads the sandbox pipes, followed by one framed final receipt. The application transport validates frame shape, stream name, base64, chunk size, total byte limit, and exactly one matching receipt. The owned child reader delivers complete lines immediately, rejects oversized or incomplete frames, and still owns/cancels/drains the process tree. `CommandController` stores each chunk as a lease-fenced `execution.output.delta` event with an explicit untrusted-output marker; existing sequence-numbered run-event APIs make those deltas resumable. The desktop renders the bounded recent output and uses per-stream incremental UTF-8 decoders. Existing final output artifacts and their receipt hashes remain authoritative. Focused deterministic transport/controller suites pass (37 tests); desktop TypeScript typecheck passes. No LM Studio inference was run. Native WSL end-to-end timing, disconnect/reconnect acceptance, hosted Windows validation and the wider Hermes streaming/tool-output gap remain open.


The actual WSL/Windows IPC check then passed on Ubuntu-24.04 with no model inference. [`scripts/wsl_transport_diagnostic.py`](../scripts/wsl_transport_diagnostic.py) now runs a delayed two-write command and checks that four output frames arrive before the final receipt, reproduce the receipt bytes exactly, and retain cleanup/cancellation and original-workspace checks. Evidence: [`.acceptance/4392f3ad-81fd-4bb6-a64a-f0de6919bfe7/report.json`](../.acceptance/4392f3ad-81fd-4bb6-a64a-f0de6919bfe7/report.json). The desktop browser stream suite also passes all four cases, including durable command output rendering with multibyte UTF-8 split across events and separate stdout/stderr. A stale latest-unfinished-run fixture caused an existing reconnect regression to fail; after correcting that fixture, the stream suite passes. Hosted run [42709891645](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42709891645) for `1aaf2b8` was cancelled during the full Python suite after the follow-up push superseded it. Hosted run [42710984034](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42710984034) for `f149ba8` passed its Windows source job, including the streaming browser suite, typechecks, static checks and full Python suite; the installer job was skipped because the branch is not `main`. The production desktop bundle builds locally. The manually dispatched hosted installer acceptance on `359e546` passed, including package build, installation/startup, standard-user install, opt-in data removal and uninstall. Its installer smoke verifies the new LICENSE and all three release documents against manifest hashes; it does not yet exercise live streamed commands from the installed app. Native app/window reconnect remains open.

## Restore command output after a native desktop process death (2026-11-09)

Extended `scripts/native_restart_smoke.mjs` to seed a bounded, correctly shaped `execution.output.delta` event at the crash boundary, kill the bundled Tauri desktop and engine, relaunch the same profile, and verify the desktop restores the marker from the durable event stream. The complete Windows native acceptance passed with a deterministic loopback provider: exactly one recovery candidate, `run.recovered`, output marker restored, no tool effects, unchanged workspace, and launch credentials cleaned. Evidence: [`.acceptance/native-restart-41931b5d-08f9-478c-9370-4fc6c5fe696c/report.json`](../.acceptance/native-restart-41931b5d-08f9-478c-9370-4fc6c5fe696c/report.json). This proves native process-death replay in the bundled app; it does not claim a real WSL command ran inside the installer. No LM Studio or external provider inference was used. Hosted installer rerun for this acceptance update is pending.

## Add explicitly selected MCP prompt text to run context (2026-11-10)

The owner can now preview an MCP prompt and explicitly add a text-only rendering to the editable run objective. The inserted value records server, prompt and message roles as JSON and carries an untrusted-reference label; the normal system policy continues to treat MCP data as untrusted. Addition is a separate owner action after preview, the objective remains editable for review, the existing 10,000-character limit is enforced, and non-text prompt content remains preview-only. This does not auto-select prompts or grant tools. Desktop `npm run typecheck`, production build and the focused MCP browser workflow pass; the browser scenario confirms preview alone does not create a run, explicit addition is visible, and an oversized addition is refused without changing the objective. Hosted Windows run [42803528613](https://github.com/iExyst/Xyst-Ai-Agent-PC/actions/runs/42803528613) passed the full source workflow, including typechecking, client/browser acceptance, Python suite and process-recovery diagnostics. The installer job was skipped because no packaging inputs changed. Live inference is deferred. Automatic prompt selection, multimodal MCP prompt use, sampling, and the newer `subscriptions/listen` protocol remain open.
